95fn scheduler_commit_record_suffix(
96    scheduler_turn: u64,
97    virtual_nanoseconds: u64,
98    internal_io_poll: bool,
99    runtime_maps_read: bool,
100) -> String {
101    crate::detlog::record_suffix(crate::detlog::DetLogEvent::SchedulerCommit {
102        scheduler_turn,
103        virtual_nanoseconds,
104        internal_io_poll,
105        runtime_maps_read,
106    })
107}

109/// Unique identifier for an action.
110pub type ActionID = u64;
111
112/// A representation of side effects that are happening, or could be happening, right now
113/// in the background.
114#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
115pub struct Action {
116    /// Id for the action
117    #[allow(dead_code)]
118    pub action_id: ActionID,
119
120    /// The action's side effects are completed.
121    #[allow(dead_code)]
122    pub completion: Ivar<()>,
123
124    /// Which action gets the lock after me.
125    #[allow(dead_code)]
126    pub successors: HashMap<ResourceID, ActionID>,
127}
128
129/// The response from the scheduler that wakes back up a guest thread after a request.
130#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
131pub enum SchedResponse {
132    /// Keep running.
133    Go(Option<SchedValue>),
134
135    /// The guest was interupted by a signal while waiting on the scheduler, and will now execute
136    /// the handler.
137    Signaled(Option<Vec<SigWrapper>>),
138    // TODO: Time to exit, or an exit is already under way
139    // Exit,
140}

142#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialOrd, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
143/// A value that the scheduler returns to the guest when resuming.  This is weakly typed in that it
144/// is only relevant to certain scheduler requests, and its meaning is dependent on what was
145/// requested of the scheduler.
146///
147/// It can be used to have the scheduler EMULATE behaviors (syscalls) that would normally happen in
148/// the guest. The first application for this is futexes.
149pub enum SchedValue {
150    /// The action timed out while waiting on the scheduler.
151    TimeOut,
152    // TODO(T137799529) make this more strongly typed, an enum for different scenarios:
153    Value(u64),
154}

156/// A single interaction between a guest and the scheduler: first, request resourcees, followed
157/// by an ACK to "go ahead".  This thread record includes a bit of thread metadata.
158#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
159pub struct ThreadNextTurn {
160    /// The logical Tid of the guest thread.
161    pub dettid: DetTid,
162    /// Address of where the child thread Tid will be cleared if CLEARTID was set on clone.
163    pub child_tid_addr: usize,
164    /// Request from the thread to the scheduler.
165    pub req: Ivar<SchedRequest>,
166    /// A place for the response when that request is fulfilled.
167    pub resp: Ivar<SchedResponse>,
168}

177    pub child_tid_addr: usize,
178    pub reconnect_priority: Option<Priority>,

181/// Request for resources when the thread next parks.
182/// OR the thread might "park" because it's really exited.
183pub type SchedRequest = Result<Resources, ThreadExited>;

190/// A thread waiting on a futex, including the bitset accepted by wake operations.
191#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
192pub struct FutexWaiter {
193    dettid: DetTid,
194    response: Ivar<SchedResponse>,
195    bitset: u32,
196}

198/// Render an already-sorted thread list for a diagnostic, or `none`.
199fn render_tid_list(tids: &[DetTid]) -> String {
200    if tids.is_empty() {
201        "none".to_owned()
202    } else {
203        tids.iter()
204            .map(|t| format!("dtid {}", t))
205            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
206            .join(", ")
207    }
208}
209
210/// Deterministically pick one element from `choices` using the supplied PRNG,
211/// returning `None` for an empty slice. This is the single random-selection
212/// primitive used by the targeted-chaos scheduling points, so their choices stay
213/// reproducible under a fixed `--fuzz-seed`.
214fn chaos_pick<T: Copy>(prng: &mut Pcg64Mcg, choices: &[T]) -> Option<T> {
215    choices.choose(prng).copied()
216}
217
218fn take_matching_futex_waiters(waiters: &mut Vec<FutexWaiter>, wake_mask: u32) -> Vec<FutexWaiter> {
219    let (matching, remaining) = std::mem::take(waiters)
220        .into_iter()
221        .partition(|waiter| waiter.bitset & wake_mask != 0);
222    *waiters = remaining;
223    matching
224}
225
226/// Actions that are blocked on another internal action of the guest, such as a pipe communication,
227/// or are blocked on external conditions such as a network request.  These cannot consume a logical
228/// turn until a matching unblocking action is ready.
229///
230/// This structure will NOT include blocking operations that are implemented via polling.
231/// See NOTE [Blocking Syscalls via Internal Polling] in this folder.
232#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
233pub struct BlockedPool {
234    /// BLOCKED futex transactions, waiting for wakers. Multiple threads may be blocked on
235    /// the same futex.
236    ///
237    /// INVARIANT: because Futexes aren't currently modeled with `ResourceID`, a thread
238    /// waiting on a futex will have a request filled in `next_turns` but for zero resources.
239    pub futex_waiters: HashMap<FutexID, Vec<FutexWaiter>>,
240
241    /// Futex waiters whose deadlines expired and must receive `ETIMEDOUT` when scheduled.
242    /// Timed-out waiters are removed from `futex_waiters` before entering the run queue.
243    pub timed_out_futex_waiters: HashSet<DetTid>,
244
245    /// Threads whose next event is waiting on a point in time to proceed.
246    ///
247    /// This is sorted by soonest time of occurrence.
248    /// NOTE: futex waiters will ALSO appear in here if they have timeouts.
249    pub timed_waiters: TimedEvents,
250
251    /// Threads parked until a matching child process exits logically.
252    pub child_waiters: BTreeMap<DetTid, (DetPid, ChildWaitSpec)>,
253
254    /// Threads parked between logical process exit and a backend's final
255    /// physical-exit report.
256    pub physical_child_waiters: BTreeMap<DetPid, BTreeSet<DetTid>>,
257
258    /// Waiters whose deterministic physical-exit handoff is ready to commit.
259    pub physical_child_ready: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
260
261    /// Blockers on external IO that are in the middle of executing (or have finished) and
262    /// are waiting for permission from the scheduler to resume.
263    ///
264    /// The protocol here is that the `(request,response)` pair (in `next_turns`) for
265    /// threads in `external_io_blockers` will have the request filled in with an
266    /// `BlockedExternalContinue` request when the thread is past its blocking action and
267    /// waiting for permission to resume. A failed operation governed by `BlockingVfork` instead
268    /// reports `VforkFailed`, which follows the same re-admission path after cancelling its
269    /// barrier. The request will stay empty while the thread is doing the blocking action. This is
270    /// different than the normal relationship
271    pub external_io_blockers: BTreeMap<DetTid, ExternalOpId>,
272
273    /// Threads executing the real `rt_sigsuspend` outside the runnable set.
274    /// These retain the kernel's atomic temporary-mask semantics, but unlike
275    /// arbitrary external IO they cannot complete without a signal.
276    pub rt_sigsuspend_blockers: BTreeMap<DetTid, ExternalOpId>,
277
278    /// Parents parked awaiting deterministic delivery of a host-async `SIGCHLD`.
279    ///
280    /// When a guest child process exits, the kernel raises `SIGCHLD` on the
281    /// parent at a moment decided purely by host timing. If the resulting
282    /// `InboundSignal` turn is committed as soon as it arrives, its position
283    /// races whatever guest work was already runnable -- classically a `make -jN`
284    /// jobserver `pselect6` continuation -- and `--strict --verify` diverges.
285    ///
286    /// Instead the parent is parked here, out of the run queue, and re-admitted
287    /// by `step2e_process_signal_deferred` only once no ordinary (non-poller)
288    /// guest work remains: the same deterministic-work-first policy that governs
289    /// `external_io_blockers`. The physical signal has already been delivered by
290    /// the kernel, so the handler's `wait4`/`waitpid` still reaps a real host
291    /// zombie and no synthetic signal is ever generated.
292    pub sigchld_deferred: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
293
294    /// Deferred `SIGCHLD` parents that `step2e_process_signal_deferred` has
295    /// re-admitted to the run queue. Their `InboundSignal` turn must now be
296    /// granted rather than deferred again on the turn the scheduler selects them.
297    pub sigchld_ready: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
298}
299
300impl BlockedPool {
301    /// Returns true if there are NO blocked threads waiting outside the run-queue.
302    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
303        self.no_futex_waiters()
304            && self.timed_waiters.is_empty()
305            && self.child_waiters.is_empty()
306            && self.physical_child_waiters.is_empty()
307            && self.physical_child_ready.is_empty()
308            && self.external_io_blockers.is_empty()
309            && self.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty()
310            && self.sigchld_deferred.is_empty()
311    }
312
313    /// True if there are no runnable threads, and the only blocked ones are externally-blocked.
314    fn only_external_blocked(&self) -> bool {
315        let has_external_wait = !self.external_io_blockers.is_empty()
316            || !self.child_waiters.is_empty()
317            || !self.physical_child_waiters.is_empty();
318        self.no_futex_waiters()
319            && self.timed_waiters.is_empty()
320            && self.physical_child_ready.is_empty()
321            && (has_external_wait || !self.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty())
322    }
323
324    /// Returns true if there are zero threads blocked on futexes.
325    fn no_futex_waiters(&self) -> bool {
326        self.futex_waiters.iter().all(|(_, v)| v.is_empty())
327    }
328}
329
330/// Validate a request made by a thread executing outside the runnable set.
331/// A signal may interrupt a real blocking syscall before its ordinary
332/// continuation request is posted, so an inbound-signal request is ready too.
333/// `vfork` is excluded because a signal does not satisfy its child barrier.
334fn blocking_request_is_ready(
335    req: &Resources,
336    expected: ExternalOpId,
337    signal_can_complete: bool,
338) -> bool {
339    assert_eq!(req.resources.len(), 1);
340    let rsrc = req.resources.iter().next().unwrap().0;
341    match rsrc {
342        ResourceID::BlockedExternalContinue(op_id) | ResourceID::VforkFailed(op_id) => {
343            assert_eq!(*op_id, expected);
344            true
345        }
346        ResourceID::InboundSignal(_) if signal_can_complete => true,
347        other => panic!("expected external continue request, got {other:?}"),
348    }
349}
350
351/// Runtime state for enforcing a [`HappensBeforeProgram`] inside the scheduler.
352///
353/// The scheduler holds each edge's AFTER anchor -- removing that thread from the
354/// run queue -- until the edge's BEFORE anchor has *fired*, so an authored
355/// partial order deterministically reproduces a known race instead of relying on
356/// a seed lottery. An anchor "fires" when its thread is granted passage past the
357/// corresponding checkpoint (see [`Scheduler::hb_checkpoint`]).
358///
359/// Only [`Position::SyscallCount`] anchors are enforced in this milestone. Other
360/// position kinds are retained for diagnostics but never fire; [`HbRuntime::new`]
361/// warns about them so a run never silently ignores an ordering constraint.
362#[derive(Debug)]
363struct HbRuntime {
364    /// The validated, normalized program (anchors indexed by name, plus edges).
365    program: HappensBeforeProgram,
366    /// Names of anchors that have fired. Monotonic: an anchor fires at most once,
367    /// when its thread is first granted passage past it.
368    fired: BTreeSet<String>,
369    /// Threads currently parked at an AFTER anchor, out of the run queue, awaiting
370    /// their gating BEFORE anchor(s). A `BTreeSet` keeps re-admission order
371    /// deterministic.
372    parked: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
373    /// Threads observed at creation time, in deterministic spawn order, so an
374    /// anchor addressed by `spawn_ordinal` resolves to a concrete `DetTid`.
375    /// Index 0 is the root thread; index N (1-based) is the Nth spawned child,
376    /// matching [`ThreadRef::spawn_ordinal`] semantics.
377    spawn_order: Vec<DetTid>,
378    /// Set when a newly fired anchor may have opened a parked thread's gate, so
379    /// [`Scheduler::hb_flush_wakes`] re-admits parked threads at the next
380    /// `step3` boundary. Re-admission is *deferred* to that boundary because it
381    /// pushes to the run queue, which is illegal while a `tentative_pop`
382    /// selection is in progress (as it is inside `block_for_one_resource`,
383    /// where anchors fire).
384    wake_pending: bool,
385}
386
387impl HbRuntime {
388    /// Build runtime state from a normalized program, warning about any anchor
389    /// whose position kind this milestone does not enforce.
390    fn new(program: HappensBeforeProgram) -> Self {
391        for anchor in program.unenforced_positions() {
392            tracing::warn!(
393                "[happens-before] anchor {} uses position '{}', which the scheduler does not yet \
394                 enforce (only 'after N syscalls' is enforced); this ordering constraint will NOT \
395                 be applied",
396                anchor.name,
397                anchor.position,
398            );
399        }
400        Self {
401            program,
402            fired: BTreeSet::new(),
403            parked: BTreeSet::new(),
404            spawn_order: Vec::new(),
405            wake_pending: false,
406        }
407    }
408
409    /// Record a thread at creation time for `spawn_ordinal` resolution. Idempotent
410    /// and cheap; the root thread lands at index 0, the Nth child at index N.
411    fn note_spawn(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) {
412        if !self.spawn_order.contains(&dettid) {
413            self.spawn_order.push(dettid);
414        }
415    }
416
417    /// True when `tref` resolves to `dettid`, by explicit `DetTid` or by
418    /// `spawn_ordinal` against the observed spawn order.
419    fn thread_matches(&self, tref: &ThreadRef, dettid: DetTid) -> bool {
420        if let Some(d) = tref.dettid {
421            return d == dettid;
422        }
423        if let Some(ord) = tref.spawn_ordinal {
424            return self.spawn_order.get(ord as usize).copied() == Some(dettid);
425        }
426        false
427    }
428
429    /// Names of anchors on `dettid` whose enforced position is exactly
430    /// `SyscallCount(count)`.
431    fn anchors_at_syscall(&self, dettid: DetTid, count: u64) -> Vec<String> {
432        self.program
433            .anchors
434            .values()
435            .filter(|a| {
436                matches!(a.position, Position::SyscallCount(n) if n == count)
437                    && self.thread_matches(&a.thread, dettid)
438            })
439            .map(|a| a.name.clone())
440            .collect()
441    }
442
443    /// True when anchor `name` is the AFTER endpoint of a Hard edge whose BEFORE
444    /// endpoint has not yet fired -- i.e. a thread reaching `name` must be held.
445    fn anchor_blocked(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
446        self.program.edges.iter().any(|e| {
447            e.after == name && e.strength == Strength::Hard && !self.fired.contains(&e.before)
448        })
449    }
450}
451
452/// Which end of a thread's priority band a run-queue admission targets.
453#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
454pub(crate) enum AdmitSide {
455    /// Run before an equal-priority peer (`runqueue_push_front`).
456    Front,
457    /// Ordinary tail admission (`runqueue_push_back`).
458    Back,
459}
460
461/// How a run-queue admission's side is determined.
462///
463/// The side is *resolved* — not merely applied — at the deterministic drain
464/// ([`Scheduler::drain_pending_run_queue_admissions`]). Buffering the *intent*
465/// rather than an already-chosen `AdmitSide` is what keeps the admission a pure
466/// function of deterministic scheduler state: any PRNG draw that picks the side
467/// (`RunsPostFork::Random`) is consumed at the drain, in canonical `DetTid`
468/// order, instead of in host RPC / lock-acquisition order at the handler. See
469/// [`Scheduler::admit_to_run_queue`].
470#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
471pub(crate) enum AdmitIntent {
472    /// The side is fixed regardless of scheduler state; consumes no PRNG.
473    Fixed(AdmitSide),
474    /// The side follows the post-fork policy; `RunsPostFork::Random` draws from
475    /// the scheduler PRNG at resolution time.
476    PostFork(RunsPostFork),
477}
478
479/// Why a raw TID must be removed from the physical run queue at the next
480/// deterministic drain.
481#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
482enum RemovalDisposition {
483    /// The current thread incarnation is gone. Any admission recorded for the
484    /// same raw TID is stale and must be cancelled.
485    Retire,
486    /// Linux nonleader exec destroyed the old process leader and reassigned
487    /// its raw TID to the caller's replacement image. Remove the old physical
488    /// queue slot, but preserve the one causally paired replacement admission.
489    ReplaceThenAdmit,
490}
491
492#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
493enum WaitidSignalRequest {
494    /// An in-flight wait that a pending signal should wake: either the legacy
495    /// `waitid` kernel-polling loop or a scheduler-managed child-wait lifecycle
496    /// resource.
497    ///
498    /// Deliberately does NOT distinguish the two. Queue residency is not a
499    /// property of the resource and must be decided at the drain, not inferred
500    /// here: `wake_child_waiters` re-admits a waiter without clearing its
501    /// `WaitChild` request, and `step6_reenqueue` pushes a thread back before it
502    /// issues its next request, so either request can be held by a thread that
503    /// is already in the run queue.
504    Parked,
505    Pending(Vec<SigWrapper>),
506}
507
508/// The state for the deterministic scheduler.
509#[derive(Debug)]
510pub struct Scheduler {
511    /// Monotonically count upwards.
512    pub turn: u64,
513
514    /// The queue of logically UNBLOCKED guest threads waiting for a turn.  After a new
515    /// thread is created, it should always have an entry in here, but it goes to the end
516    /// of the line after its turn. Unblocked threads are dequed in priority order, then
517    /// round-robin within a priority level.
518    /// NB: Polling threads are considered unblocked, and their polling intervals are managed by the RunQueue
519    pub run_queue: RunQueue,
520
521    /// Stores the communication endpoints for rendevous with each guest on its next turn.
522    /// When the thread parks it provides its request for resources, and waits for a
523    /// response.  After a new thread is created, it should always have an entry in here.
524    ///
525    /// Parked threads are READY, waiting only for the scheduler.
526    ///
527    /// (N.B.  This is a BTreeMap because we iterate over it, printing the
528    /// contents, and BTreeMap gives us a predictable order, unlike HashMap.)
529    pub next_turns: BTreeMap<DetTid, ThreadNextTurn>,
530
531    /// Thread pidfds for backends whose scheduler identities do not name host
532    /// tasks directly. The address-space identity prevents stale exec cleanup
533    /// from removing a replacement image's descriptor.
534    physical_thread_pidfds: BTreeMap<DetTid, (MmId, i32, i32, OwnedFd)>,
535
536    /// The current set of actions in the background.
537    #[allow(dead_code)]
538    pub bg_action_pool: HashMap<ActionID, Action>,
539
540    /// The logical, global time consumed by actions that have been committed already.
541    pub committed_time: LogicalTime,
542
543    /// INVARIANT: Thread IDs in `blocked` are absent from `run_queue`.
544    pub blocked: BlockedPool,
545
546    /// Kernel-blocked vfork parents and their children, once registered.
547    vfork_barriers: BTreeMap<DetTid, Option<DetTid>>,
548
549    /// Threads whose run-queue admission was recorded by a global-request
550    /// handler while a `tentative_pop` transaction was live, deferred to the
551    /// next deterministic drain point (`step2`) so it cannot mutate the run
552    /// queue underneath the daemon's tentative selection. Keyed by `DetTid`
553    /// so the drain order among co-pending admissions is canonical rather than
554    /// lock-acquisition dependent. The stored value is the *unresolved*
555    /// [`AdmitIntent`], so any side-selecting PRNG draw is deferred to the
556    /// `DetTid`-ordered drain rather than performed in host RPC order. See
557    /// [`Scheduler::admit_to_run_queue`] and
558    /// [`Scheduler::drain_pending_run_queue_admissions`].
559    pending_run_queue_admissions: BTreeMap<DetTid, AdmitIntent>,
560
561    /// Run-queue *removals* requested by a global-request handler
562    /// (`reconnect_after_exec` -> `logically_kill_thread`), deferred to the same
563    /// deterministic drain point (`step2`). `RunQueue::remove_tid` carries the same
564    /// `tentative_selection.is_none()` guard as the push operations, so a
565    /// multi-threaded exec that reconnects on an asynchronous backend (DBT)
566    /// inside the daemon's tentative window would otherwise trip it, poison the
567    /// scheduler mutex, and hang the run.
568    ///
569    /// A `Retire` target is already logically dead (its `next_turns` entry is
570    /// gone and its request is `ThreadExited`). `ReplaceThenAdmit` is the one
571    /// Linux exception: a nonleader exec has installed a fresh registration at
572    /// the destroyed leader's raw TID, but the old incarnation's physical queue
573    /// slot must still be removed. Both are filtered from
574    /// [`Scheduler::are_all_quiesced`] until this drain establishes the intended
575    /// physical queue state.
576    ///
577    /// The map is drained before admissions. Ordinary retirement cancels a
578    /// buffered admission for the same raw TID; the explicitly classified exec
579    /// replacement preserves exactly one causally paired admission.
580    pending_run_queue_removals: BTreeMap<DetTid, RemovalDisposition>,
581
582    /// Cross-task signals physically queued while their sole target was parked
583    /// in waitid or restartable internal IO polling. Applied at step2, where
584    /// run-queue mutation is safe.
585    pending_cross_task_signals: BTreeMap<DetTid, Vec<SigWrapper>>,
586
587    /// Child-TID futexes whose kernel clear may still be racing a guest join.
588    cleared_child_tids: HashMap<FutexID, DetTid>,
589
590    /// The rendered report for a terminal deadlock, once one has been detected.
591    ///
592    /// Set instead of panicking, and consumed by `sched_loop_inner`, which
593    /// prints it and exits the container. Panicking here does not end the run:
594    /// the scheduler is a `tokio::spawn`ed task, so its panic is captured by the
595    /// task harness while every guest stays parked on an
596    /// `Ivar<SchedResponse>` that only the (now dead) scheduler could fill --
597    /// the run then hangs until an external timeout. Unwinding would also
598    /// poison the scheduler mutex on the way out.
599    terminal_deadlock: Option<String>,
600
601    /// Whether exit-group teardown must explicitly cancel parked backend RPCs.
602    cancel_killed_thread_rpcs: bool,
603
604    /// Whether scheduler identities must be resolved through a host thread
605    /// pidfd before sending process-directed signals.
606    backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals: bool,
607
608    /// Whether this backend can preserve Linux signal semantics when a
609    /// scheduler-managed pipe write is woken by a cross-task signal.
610    backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption: bool,
611
612    /// Raw TIDs removed by logical teardown. Tombstones are permanent for the life of this
613    /// scheduler: accepting Linux TID reuse would let delayed backend RPCs bind to a new thread.
614    logically_killed_threads: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
615
616    /// Accepted address-space incarnation for raw TIDs explicitly reused by exec.
617    exec_incarnations: BTreeMap<DetTid, MmId>,
618
619    /// Tombstoned SaBRe threads whose final asynchronous deregistration statistics were merged.
620    /// Logical exit-group teardown and physical exit cleanup are distinct events.
621    deregistration_accounted: BTreeSet<DetTid>,
622
623    /// Whether the backend will report final physical process exits after logical cleanup.
624    backend_reports_physical_process_exits: bool,
625
626    /// SaBRe process leaders whose tool exit hook ran before the ptrace supervisor observed the
627    /// final kernel exit status. While the run queue is empty, these prevent virtual timers from
628    /// overtaking a child exit that is not physically waitable yet.
629    pending_physical_process_exits: BTreeSet<DetPid>,
630
631    /// Logically exited child processes whose terminal wait status has not been consumed.
632    logically_exited_processes: BTreeSet<DetPid>,
633
634    /// Reporting-backend children whose final physical exit has been observed.
635    completed_physical_process_exits: BTreeSet<DetPid>,
636
637    /// Whether the backend defers spawning a vfork child until after the parent posts its
638    /// continuation, so an unfulfilled vfork barrier at parent continuation means the child is
639    /// still on its way rather than that the clone failed. See
640    /// [`Config::backend_defers_vfork_child_registration`].
641    backend_defers_vfork_child_registration: bool,
642
643    /// Ac table of "locks held": which action is using which resources.
644    /// A given resource can be held by at most one action at a given time.
645    #[allow(dead_code)]
646    pub resources: HashMap<ResourceID, ActionID>,
647
648    /// Initially false, set to true when the first thread is running.
649    /// Invariant: at the moment this becomes full, the queue is nonempty.
650    pub started_up: Ivar<()>,
651
652    /// A model of the the raw ancestry tree of threads, based on parentage at the point
653    /// of thread creation.  This establishes a mapping from each thread to the child
654    /// threads it has spawned.
655    //
656    // FUTURE OPTION:
657    // If this is not used for purposes *other* than `exit_group` handling in the future,
658    // we could probably rip it out and just refer to the `/proc/pid/task/` directory
659    // to determine what threads exit upon `exit_group`.
660    pub thread_tree: ThreadTree,
661
662    /// Tracks the priorities of each thread. New threads should have an entry
663    /// before being inserted into the runqueue.
664    ///
665    /// INVARIANT: Whenever the thread is normally in the run_queue, it's
666    /// priority in the queue should match that stored here. "Abnormal"
667    /// queueings include polling and eager IO polling.
668    ///
669    /// NB: BTreeMap over HashMap for deterministic printing.
670    pub priorities: BTreeMap<DetTid, Priority>,
671
672    /// Tracks explicit optional timeslices to run for each thread.
673    /// If a guest is to be unblocked on a thread the guest will receive this
674    /// information and needs to "cooperate" and setup it's preemption for the amount
675    pub timeslices: BTreeMap<DetTid, Option<LogicalTime>>,
676
677    /// Per-thread distribution of completed timeslice durations (virtual ns),
678    /// collected from each thread as it deregisters at exit. Aggregated into the
679    /// final run report. BTreeMap for deterministic iteration order.
680    pub per_thread_timeslice: BTreeMap<DetTid, TimesliceStats>,
681
682    /// Final syscall count reported by each thread when it deregisters.
683    pub per_thread_syscalls: BTreeMap<DetTid, u64>,
684
685    /// A record of which preemptions occured on each thread.  Only used IF `--record-preemptions`
686    /// was specified in the Config, otherwise this remains empty.
687    pub preemption_writer: Option<PreemptionWriter>,
688
689    /// An instance of replayer that is responsible for replaying events in case --replay-preemptions-from is specified
690    pub replayer: Option<Replayer>,
691
692    /// Count record_event calls which determines the event number if we're recording a schedule
693    /// event trace.
694    pub recorded_event_count: u64,
695
696    /// A copy of the `Config::stacktrace_event` vector.  This is MUTABLE,
697    /// because we pop events off as we handle them.  The u64 is an index into
698    /// the (original) replay_cursor trace.
699    pub stacktrace_events: Option<StacktraceEventsIter>,
700
701    /// PRNG to drive any fuzzing of OS semantics (other than scheduling).
702    fuzz_prng: Pcg64Mcg,
703
704    /// Independent scheduler-seeded stream for post-fork ordering choices.
705    post_fork_prng: Pcg64Mcg,
706
707    /// A cached copy of the same (immutable) field in Config.
708    stop_after_turn: Option<u64>,
709    /// A cached copy of the same (immutable) field in Config.
710    stop_after_iter: Option<u64>,
711    /// A cached copy of the same (immutable) field in Config.
712    recordreplay_modes: bool,
713    /// A cached copy of the same (immutable) field in Config.
714    fuzz_futexes: bool,
715    /// A cached copy of the same (immutable) field in Config. When set (and only
716    /// meaningful in chaos mode) the scheduler biases its nondeterminism points
717    /// toward known race patterns rather than exploring uniformly.
718    chaos_target_races: bool,
719
720    /// Happens-before enforcement state, present only when the run carries a
721    /// `HappensBeforeProgram`. Holds AFTER anchors until their BEFORE anchors
722    /// fire, deterministically constructing an authored race ordering.
723    happens_before: Option<HbRuntime>,
724}
725
726type StacktraceEventsIter = Peekable<IntoIter<(u64, Option<SchedEvent>, Option<PathBuf>)>>;
727
728// type ThreadTree = HashMap<DetTid, Vec<DetTid>>;
729#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
730pub struct ThreadTree {
731    /// Invariant: this is None only if `tree` is also empty.
732    /// That is any ThreadTree of size zero or more has a root.
733    root: Option<DetTid>,
734    /// Invariant: every `DetTid` in the tree has an entry here, though if it is a leaf,
735    /// it will have an empty children-vector.
736    tree: HashMap<DetTid, Vec<DetTid>>,
737
738    /// The subset of threads that are also thread group leaders.  This tracks both the
739    /// Tid, but it is (numerically) the same as Pid for group leaders in Linux.
740    thread_group_leaders: HashSet<DetTid>,
741
742    /// Go from a Tid to the Pid/Tid of the containing process (i.e. a reverse view of a
743    /// transitive closure of `thread_tree`).  Every thread should have an entry in
744    /// here. If, however, a thread is a group leader, this will map back to itself.
745    thread_to_leader: HashMap<DetTid, DetPid>,
746
747    /// Reverse map from a process (group-leader `DetPid`) to its effective Linux
748    /// wait parent, including `CLONE_PARENT`. Populated when a new group leader
749    /// is registered; the root process has no entry. Entries survive logical
750    /// exit until the terminal status is consumed.
751    process_parent: HashMap<DetPid, DetPid>,
752
753    /// Linux child-wait identity for each process leader. Unlike the thread
754    /// tree edge, this records the effective wait parent after CLONE_PARENT,
755    /// the exact creating task for __WNOTHREAD, clone exit-signal class, and
756    /// mutable process-group/session membership.
757    process_wait: HashMap<DetPid, ProcessWaitMetadata>,
758}
759
760#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
761struct ProcessWaitMetadata {
762    wait_parent: Option<DetPid>,
763    wait_owner: DetTid,
764    exit_signal: libc::c_int,
765    process_group: DetPid,
766    session: DetPid,
767}
768
769use pretty::Doc;
770use pretty::RcDoc;
771
772use self::replayer::DesyncStats;
773use self::replayer::Replayer;
774
775impl ThreadTree {
776    /// Internal helper. Add a [child] process to the tree, with the parent being `None`
777    /// if it's the root of the tree.
778    fn add_edge(&mut self, parent: Option<DetTid>, child: DetTid) {
779        match parent {
780            None => {
781                self.root = Some(child);
782                // Ensure an entry, even if the children vector is empty:
783                let _vec = self.tree.entry(child).or_default();
784            }
785            Some(p) => {
786                let vec = self.tree.entry(p).or_default();
787                vec.push(child);
788                let _vec = self.tree.entry(child).or_default();
789            }
790        }
791    }
792
793    /// Read the children of a thread, which is assumed to have an entry in the tree.
794    pub fn get_children(&mut self, parent: &DetTid) -> &Vec<DetTid> {
795        self.tree
796            .get(parent)
797            .expect("Internal failure: tid was not found in ThreadTree")
798    }
799
800    /// Convert to pretty-printed document.
801    ///
802    /// For example, a binary tree of depth two may print as `(1 (2 3 4) (5 6 7))`,
803    /// showing each thread ID grouped with its children.
804    ///
805    /// The thread_group_leaders argument is used for additional context into account when
806    /// pretty-printing a `ThreadTree`.  This will indicate which children are within new
807    /// thread groups using square brackets:
808    ///
809    ///   `[1 [2 [3] 4] (5 6 7)]`
810    // TODO: it would also be nice to store a fixed prefix of the binary name and listing
811    // that along with the thread ID.
812    pub fn pretty_print(&self) -> String {
813        fn walk<'a>(
814            tt: &'a HashMap<DetTid, Vec<DetTid>>,
815            tgl: &HashSet<DetTid>,
816            current: &DetTid,
817        ) -> RcDoc<'a, ()> {
818            if let Some(children) = tt.get(current) {
819                if tgl.contains(current) {
820                    RcDoc::text("[")
821                        .append(RcDoc::as_string(current))
822                        .append(if children.is_empty() {
823                            RcDoc::text("")
824                        } else {
825                            RcDoc::text(" ").append(
826                                RcDoc::intersperse(
827                                    children.iter().map(|x| walk(tt, tgl, x)),
828                                    Doc::line(),
829                                )
830                                .nest(1)
831                                .group(),
832                            )
833                        })
834                        .append(RcDoc::text("]"))
835                } else if children.is_empty() {
836                    RcDoc::as_string(current)
837                } else {
838                    RcDoc::text("(")
839                        .append(RcDoc::as_string(current))
840                        .append(RcDoc::text(" "))
841                        .append(
842                            RcDoc::intersperse(
843                                children.iter().map(|x| walk(tt, tgl, x)),
844                                Doc::line(),
845                            )
846                            .nest(1)
847                            .group(),
848                        )
849                        .append(RcDoc::text(")"))
850                }
851            } else {
852                // This should be unreachable if the invariants are maintained:
853                RcDoc::text("<ThreadTree corrupt, missing tid: ")
854                    .append(RcDoc::as_string(current))
855                    .append(RcDoc::text(">"))
856            }
857        }
858
859        let root = match self.root {
860            None => return "[]".into(),
861            Some(root) => root,
862        };
863
864        let doc = walk(&self.tree, &self.thread_group_leaders, &root);
865        let width = 100;
866        let mut vec = Vec::new();
867        doc.render(width, &mut vec).unwrap();
868        String::from_utf8(vec).unwrap()
869    }
870
871    #[allow(dead_code)]
872    /// Number of threads with entries in the tree.
873    pub fn size(&self) -> usize {
874        self.tree.len()
875    }
876
877    /// Simultaneously update the thread tree and leader tracking to reflect the creation
878    /// of a new child thread.
879    #[cfg(test)]
880    pub fn add_child(
881        &mut self,
882        parent_dettid: DetTid,
883        child_dettid: DetTid,
884        is_group_leader: bool,
885    ) {
886        self.add_child_with_wait_metadata(
887            parent_dettid,
888            child_dettid,
889            is_group_leader,
890            false,
891            libc::SIGCHLD,
892        );
893    }
894
895    /// Add a child while preserving the Linux identities used by wait-family
896    /// selection. `clone_parent` changes the child's effective wait parent and
897    /// owner to the caller's own parent task; threads never enter this process
898    /// metadata table.
899    pub fn add_child_with_wait_metadata(
900        &mut self,
901        parent_dettid: DetTid,
902        child_dettid: DetTid,
903        is_group_leader: bool,
904        clone_parent: bool,
905        exit_signal: libc::c_int,
906    ) {
907        // TODO(T78538674): virtualize pid/tid:
908        if parent_dettid == child_dettid {
909            self.add_edge(None, child_dettid);
910        } else {
911            self.add_edge(Some(parent_dettid), child_dettid);
912        }
913        if is_group_leader {
914            self.thread_group_leaders.insert(child_dettid);
915            self.thread_to_leader.insert(child_dettid, child_dettid);
916            if parent_dettid == child_dettid {
917                self.process_wait.insert(
918                    child_dettid,
919                    ProcessWaitMetadata {
920                        wait_parent: None,
921                        wait_owner: child_dettid,
922                        exit_signal: libc::SIGCHLD,
923                        process_group: child_dettid,
924                        session: child_dettid,
925                    },
926                );
927            } else {
928                let parent_process = self
929                    .thread_to_leader
930                    .get(&parent_dettid)
931                    .copied()
932                    .expect("process child parent must have a thread-group leader");
933                let parent_metadata = self.process_wait.get(&parent_process).copied().unwrap_or(
934                    ProcessWaitMetadata {
935                        wait_parent: None,
936                        wait_owner: parent_dettid,
937                        exit_signal: libc::SIGCHLD,
938                        process_group: parent_process,
939                        session: parent_process,
940                    },
941                );
942                let (wait_parent, wait_owner) = if clone_parent {
943                    (parent_metadata.wait_parent, parent_metadata.wait_owner)
944                } else {
945                    (Some(parent_process), parent_dettid)
946                };
947                if let Some(wait_parent) = wait_parent {
948                    self.process_parent.insert(child_dettid, wait_parent);
949                }
950                self.process_wait.insert(
951                    child_dettid,
952                    ProcessWaitMetadata {
953                        wait_parent,
954                        wait_owner,
955                        exit_signal,
956                        process_group: parent_metadata.process_group,
957                        session: parent_metadata.session,
958                    },
959                );
960            }
961        } else {
962            let parent_leader: DetPid =
963                    *self
964                        .thread_to_leader
965                        .get(&parent_dettid)
966                        .unwrap_or_else(|| {
967                            panic!("recv_create_child_thread: parent {} of child dtid {} does not exist in thread_to_leader map!",
968                                   parent_dettid, child_dettid);
969                        });
970            self.thread_to_leader.insert(child_dettid, parent_leader);
971        }
972    }
973
974    /// The process that created `pid` (its parent process), if `pid` is not the
975    /// root process. Returns a possibly-stale parent if that process has since
976    /// exited; callers deliver through `select_signal_target`, which drops a
977    /// signal to a `Gone` target.
978    pub fn parent_process(&self, pid: &DetPid) -> Option<DetPid> {
979        self.process_parent.get(pid).copied()
980    }
981
982    pub fn process_group(&self, pid: DetPid) -> Option<DetPid> {
983        self.process_wait
984            .get(&pid)
985            .map(|metadata| metadata.process_group)
986    }
987
988    pub fn set_process_group(&mut self, pid: DetPid, process_group: DetPid) -> bool {
989        let Some(metadata) = self.process_wait.get_mut(&pid) else {
990            return false;
991        };
992        metadata.process_group = process_group;
993        true
994    }
995
996    pub fn create_session(&mut self, pid: DetPid) -> bool {
997        let Some(metadata) = self.process_wait.get_mut(&pid) else {
998            return false;
999        };
1000        metadata.session = pid;
1001        metadata.process_group = pid;
1002        true
1003    }
1004
1005    /// Return the set of thread IDs in the "same process" as me (same TGID), including
1006    /// myself.
1007    ///
1008    /// Locks: takes scheduler lock.
1009    pub fn my_thread_group(&mut self, me: &DetTid) -> Vec<DetTid> {
1010        let root_tid: DetTid = if self.thread_group_leaders.contains(me) {
1011            *me
1012        } else {
1013            *self
1014                .thread_to_leader
1015                .get(me)
1016                .expect("thread must be in to_leader table")
1017        };
1018        let mut stack: Vec<DetTid> = vec![root_tid];
1019        let mut acc: Vec<DetTid> = vec![];
1020
1021        while let Some(first) = stack.pop() {
1022            if self.thread_group_leaders.contains(&first) && first != root_tid {
1023                continue; // Stop traversal when we walk into child processes.
1024            } else {
1025                acc.push(first);
1026            }
1027            let children = self.get_children(&first);
1028            stack.extend_from_slice(children);
1029        }
1030        assert!(acc.contains(me));
1031        acc
1032    }
1033}
1034
1035impl std::fmt::Display for ThreadTree {
1036    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
1037        // Print with empty thread_group_leaders since we don't have that information in
1038        // this context:
1039        write!(f, "{}", self.pretty_print())
1040    }
1041}
1042
1043// TODO (T137183027, T137184765)
1044/// A simple backoff strategy while we have any realtime/polling elements in the system.
1045/// When all external polling is removed, we can remove this.
1046struct Backoff {
1047    count: u64,
1048}
1049
1050impl Backoff {
1051    fn new() -> Self {
1052        Backoff { count: 0 }
1053    }
1054
1055    async fn further(&mut self, blocking: bool) {
1056        self.count += 1;
1057        const YIELDS_FIRST: u64 = 10;
1058        if blocking {
1059            if self.count <= YIELDS_FIRST {
1060                std::thread::yield_now();
1061            } else {
1062                let round = self.count - YIELDS_FIRST;
1063                let micros = if round > 13 { 10_000 } else { 2 ^ round };
1064                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_micros(micros));
1065            }
1066        } else if self.count <= YIELDS_FIRST {
1067            tokio::task::yield_now().await;
1068        } else {
1069            let round = self.count - YIELDS_FIRST;
1070            let micros = if round > 13 { 10_000 } else { 2 ^ round };
1071            tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_micros(micros)).await;
1072        }
1073    }
1074
1075    fn reset(&mut self) {
1076        self.count = 0;
1077    }
1078}
1079
1080impl Default for Backoff {
1081    fn default() -> Self {
1082        Self::new()
1083    }
1084}
1085
1086pub(crate) type SchedulerObserver = Arc<dyn Fn(&'static str) + Send + Sync>;
1087
1088pub(crate) async fn sched_loop(sched: Arc<Mutex<Scheduler>>, timer: Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>) {
1089    sched_loop_inner(sched, timer, false, None).await;
1090}
1091
1092pub(crate) async fn sched_loop_external(
1093    sched: Arc<Mutex<Scheduler>>,
1094    timer: Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
1095    observer: SchedulerObserver,
1096) {
1097    sched_loop_inner(sched, timer, true, Some(observer)).await;
1098}
1099
1100async fn sched_loop_inner(
1101    sched: Arc<Mutex<Scheduler>>,
1102    timer: Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
1103    blocking_backoff: bool,
1104    observer: Option<SchedulerObserver>,
1105) {
1106    // The "daemon task starting up" INFO line is deliberately NOT emitted here.
1107    //
1108    // This body runs inside a `tokio::spawn`ed task (see
1109    // `GlobalState::initialize`), so the moment it is first polled is
1110    // unsynchronized with the spawning thread's continued bootstrap. That made
1111    // the line race the root thread's `USER RAND` / `CHAOSRAND` seeding lines
1112    // from `ThreadState::new`, and the L2 comparator reads the INFO stream as
1113    // ordered evidence: the two runs were identical as multisets and differed
1114    // only in this one line's position, which is a real `bitwise_parity: false`.
1115    //
1116    // The announcement is now emitted by whoever starts the daemon, in that
1117    // thread's program order, so it is deterministically sequenced after
1118    // `Scheduler::new`'s `SCHEDRAND` line and before the root `ThreadState`.
1119    // That is the order the ptrace backend already produced reliably.
1120    //
1121    // The observer callback stays here, at the point the task genuinely begins
1122    // executing, because it is a barrier signal for external backends and is
1123    // not part of the compared log stream. Moving the log does not cost us the
1124    // "it actually started" observation for the consumer that needs it.
1125    if let Some(observer) = &observer {
1126        observer("daemon task starting; waiting for guest thread");
1127    }
1128    let (iv, stop_after_iter) = {
1129        // Block until queue is populated.
1130        let sched = sched.lock().unwrap();
1131        (sched.started_up.clone(), sched.stop_after_iter)
1132    };
1133    iv.get().await;
1134    info!("[scheduler] guest in queue, scheduler proceeding..",);
1135    if let Some(observer) = &observer {
1136        observer("guest registered; deterministic scheduler proceeding");
1137    }
1138    let mut iter: u64 = 0;
1139    // We keep track of whether the last turn was a SKIP:
1140    let mut last_res = Err(SkipTurn);
1141    let mut backoff = Backoff::new();
1142    let mut observed_turn = false;
1143
1144    loop {
1145        // TODO (T137183027, T137184765): as part of the current strategy for blocking IO ops (see
1146        // SPINNING below), we need to make sure that other threads can progress so we don't
1147        // busy-wait too tightly.
1148        if last_res.is_err() {
1149            backoff.further(blocking_backoff).await;
1150        } else {
1151            backoff.reset();
1152        }
1153
1154        trace!("[scheduler] loop iteration {}", iter);
1155        if stop_after_iter.is_some() && iter > stop_after_iter.unwrap() {
1156            let sched = sched.lock().unwrap();
1157            tracing::warn!(
1158                "[scheduler] Early exit during sched loop iteration {} due to --stop-after-iter.  Summary:\n\n{}",
1159                iter,
1160                sched.full_summary()
1161            );
1162            immediate_fatal_exit(); // We don't want a backtrace of this thread.
1163        }
1164        iter += 1;
1165
1166        // If there are NO threads left in the system, then we're truly done:
1167        {
1168            let sched = sched.lock().unwrap();
1169            if sched.run_queue.is_empty()
1170                && sched.blocked.is_empty()
1171                && sched.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty()
1172                && sched.pending_run_queue_admissions.is_empty()
1173                && sched.pending_run_queue_removals.is_empty()
1174            {
1175                info!("[scheduler] run queue empty, exiting sched_loop.");
1176                if let Some(observer) = &observer {
1177                    observer("run queue empty; scheduler completed");
1178                }
1179                return;
1180            } else if let Some(stop) = sched.stop_after_turn
1181                && sched.turn > stop
1182            {
1183                tracing::warn!(
1184                    "[scheduler] Early exit during turn {} due to --stop-after-turn.  Summary:\n\n{}",
1185                    sched.turn,
1186                    sched.full_summary()
1187                );
1188                immediate_fatal_exit(); // We don't want a backtrace of this thread.
1189            }
1190        }
1191
1192        // Otherwise we trust the turn function to either choose a runnable thread or wait
1193        // until something blocked is ready to run again.
1194        last_res = do_a_turn_blocking(sched.clone(), timer.clone(), &last_res).await;
1195
1196        // A terminal deadlock ends the run here, alongside the two
1197        // `--stop-after-*` exits above, rather than by panicking out of the
1198        // scheduler task (see `report_terminal_deadlock`).
1199        //
1200        // Printed with `eprintln!` rather than `tracing::error!` on purpose: the
1201        // tracing writer prefixes a real wall-clock timestamp, and this report
1202        // is required to be byte-identical across runs of the same program.
1203        if let Some(report) = sched.lock().unwrap().take_terminal_deadlock() {
1204            eprintln!("{}", report);
1205            immediate_fatal_exit(); // We don't want a backtrace of this thread.
1206        }
1207
1208        if last_res.is_ok() && !observed_turn {
1209            if let Some(observer) = &observer {
1210                observer("completed a deterministic scheduling turn");
1211            }
1212            observed_turn = true;
1213        }
1214    }
1215}
1216
1217/// Not an error, but simply a turn that cannot do productive work.
1218#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
1219pub struct SkipTurn;
1220
1221/// Advance turn by 1 turn, blocking when necessary to make it happen.
1222/// Return the outcome of the turn as well as which resources were used, if any.
1223///
1224/// WARNING: this is duplicated with the non-blocking `step` function below.
1225/// TODO: this duplication is temporary and they should be either combined or one removed soon.
1226pub async fn do_a_turn_blocking(
1227    sched: Arc<Mutex<Scheduler>>,
1228    global_time: Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
1229    last_turn: &Result<Resources, SkipTurn>,
1230) -> Result<Resources, SkipTurn> {
1231    // Loop until all threads are parked, then proceed:
1232    //
1233    // TODO: First, this check can move after step2, before we commit the action. Second,
1234    // it can later grow more sophisticated to only check the completion of dependent
1235    // actions, not all outsanding guest actions.
1236    loop {
1237        // We must read the queue carefully, because it can grow in the background
1238        // everytime we await.  However, while it can *grow*, it cannot change order, as
1239        // only the scheduler thread (us) actually rotates entries from the front to the back.
1240        let req_ivar = {
1241            let mut mg = sched.lock().unwrap();
1242            let arc = global_time.clone();
1243
1244            let next_outstanding = mg.step1_check_quiescence(&arc, last_turn);
1245            match next_outstanding {
1246                None => {
1247                    trace!("Scheduler observed full quiescense, proceeding...");
1248                    break;
1249                }
1250                Some(iv) => iv.clone(),
1251            }
1252        };
1253        trace!("Scheduler wait for full quiescense, on {}...", req_ivar);
1254        let _ = req_ivar.await;
1255    }
1256
1257    // Here we copy some information while holding the sched lock, and then release it so
1258    // we can `.await` below:
1259    let (next_dtid, req, resp) = {
1260        let mut sched = sched.lock().unwrap();
1261        sched.step2_process_blocked(&global_time)?;
1262        sched.step3_peek().ok_or(SkipTurn)?
1263    };
1264
1265    // Step 1B: wait for the selected thread to make its request.
1266    trace!(
1267        "[sched-daemon] waiting for next thread (dtid {}) to park...",
1268        next_dtid
1269    );
1270    let rsrcs: Resources = match req.get().await {
1271        Err(ThreadExited) => {
1272            debug!(
1273                "[sched-daemon] woke up on request {}, but fizzling because next thread, {}, exited.",
1274                &req, &next_dtid
1275            );
1276            // The selected thread died while we awaited its request, so this turn
1277            // is skipped without reaching step4-6. `step3_peek` opened a
1278            // tentative_pop for `next_dtid`; close it here (undo, since the turn
1279            // did not commit) before returning. Otherwise the tentative selection
1280            // outlives the turn, and the next pass's step2 removal drain calls
1281            // `remove_tid` while `tentative_selection` is still `Some`, tripping
1282            // the run queue's transaction guard -- the "reconnect panic moved one
1283            // pass" defect. The dead thread's buffered removal drains
1284            // deterministically on the next pass, once this undo has closed the
1285            // window.
1286            sched.lock().unwrap().run_queue.undo_tentative_pop();
1287            return Err(SkipTurn);
1288        }
1289        Ok(r) => r,
1290    };
1291    trace!("[sched-daemon] daemon woke up on {}...", &req);
1292
1293    // Since the scheduler is asynchronous, we need to check our assumptions.  Polling is
1294    // sufficient here because the thread cannot be racing with us to exit since we know
1295    // it is *already* parked.
1296    let mut mg = sched.lock().unwrap();
1297    mg.abort_turn_if_thread_vanished(next_dtid)?;
1298
1299    // The logical COMMIT point for the turn is during step4:
1300    mg.step4_resource_block(next_dtid, &rsrcs, &resp)?;
1301    mg.step5_guest_unblock(next_dtid, &rsrcs, &resp)?;
1302    let sched_yield = rsrcs.resources.contains_key(&ResourceID::SchedYield);
1303    mg.step6_reenquue(next_dtid, sched_yield);
1304    if let Some(call) = rsrcs.as_exit_syscall() {
1305        mg.step7_simulate_exit_posthook(next_dtid, call, &global_time);
1306    }
1307    Ok(rsrcs)
1308}
1309
1310// A futex request contains only one resource request, for FutexWait.
1311fn assert_futex_request(nextturn: &ThreadNextTurn) {
1312    match nextturn.req.try_read() {
1313        Some(Ok(req)) => {
1314            if !(req.resources.contains_key(&ResourceID::FutexWait) && req.resources.len() == 1) {
1315                panic!(
1316                    "assert_empty_request({}): internal invariant broken, expected empty resource request, found: {:?}",
1317                    nextturn.dettid, req
1318                )
1319            }
1320        }
1321        _ => panic!(
1322            "assert_empty_request({}): internal invariant broken, expected request for zero resources, instead found no request.",
1323            nextturn.dettid
1324        ),
1325    }
1326}
1327
1328// Test if the request was from a futex_wait call.
1329fn is_futex_request(nextturn: &ThreadNextTurn) -> bool {
1330    match nextturn.req.try_read() {
1331        Some(Ok(req)) => Scheduler::is_x_turn(&req, &ResourceID::FutexWait),
1332        _ => false,
1333    }
1334}
1335
1336/// Until panics are escalated properly, this encapsulates a way to exit the hermit container
1337/// entirely.
1338pub fn immediate_fatal_exit() {
1339    std::process::exit(1);
1340}
1341
1342/// The result of consuming a SchedEvent during --replay-preemptions-from.  This represents some
1343/// decisions about what to do next, but are actions which we cannot implement inside
1344/// `consume_schedevent`.
1345pub struct ConsumeResult {
1346    /// Should we keep runnning this thread, if false we background the current thread after this schedevent to let the next thread run.
1347    pub keep_running: bool,
1348    /// A remaining (delta) timeslice this thread is required to run according to the replay schedule
1349    pub timeslice_remaining: Option<LogicalTime>,
1350    /// Should we print the stacktrace in the guest, as per --stacktrace-event
1351    pub print_stack: MaybePrintStack,
1352    /// The number of this event in the global total order of events.
1353    #[allow(dead_code)]
1354    pub event_ix: u64,
1355}
1356
1357/// Any non-None response means that the guest should print its stack trace before proceeding, and
1358/// a response that further includes a path means print to a file at that location.
1359pub type MaybePrintStack = Option<Option<PathBuf>>;
1360
1361enum ThreadStatus {
1362    // Not present in scheduler structures.
1363    Gone,
1364    Running,
1365    // Absent from run queue, but present in one of the blocked structures.
1366    NotRunning,
1367}
