1369impl Scheduler {
1370    /// Create a new scheduler based on the configuration.
1371    pub fn new(cfg: &Config) -> Self {
1372        let (replayer, m_vec) = match &cfg.replay_schedule_from {
1373            Some(path) => {
1374                trace!("Scheduler loading trace from path {}", path.display());
1375                let vec = read_trace(path);
1376                trace!("Trace loaded, length {}", vec.len());
1377
1378                let toprint = cfg
1379                    .stacktrace_event
1380                    .iter()
1381                    .map(|(ix, path)| (*ix, Some(vec[*ix as usize].clone()), path.clone()))
1382                    .collect();
1383                let mut replayer = Replayer::new(vec);
1384                replayer.replay_exhausted_panic = cfg.replay_exhausted_panic;
1385                replayer.die_on_desync = cfg.die_on_desync;
1386                (Some(replayer), Some(toprint))
1387            }
1388            None => (
1389                None,
1390                if cfg.stacktrace_event.is_empty() {
1391                    None
1392                } else {
1393                    let vec: Vec<_> = cfg
1394                        .stacktrace_event
1395                        .iter()
1396                        .map(|(ix, path)| (*ix, None, path.clone()))
1397                        .collect();
1398                    Some(vec)
1399                },
1400            ),
1401        };
1402
1403        let stacktrace_events: Option<StacktraceEventsIter> = m_vec.map(|mut v| {
1404            v.sort_by_key(|(ix, _, _)| *ix);
1405            v.into_iter().peekable()
1406        });
1407
1408        Self {
1409            preemption_writer: if cfg.record_preemptions {
1410                Some(PreemptionWriter::new(cfg.record_preemptions_to.clone()))
1411            } else {
1412                None
1413            },
1414            replayer,
1415            recorded_event_count: 0,
1416            stacktrace_events,
1417            stop_after_turn: cfg.stop_after_turn,
1418            stop_after_iter: cfg.stop_after_iter,
1419            recordreplay_modes: cfg.recordreplay_modes,
1420            run_queue: RunQueue::new(
1421                cfg.sched_heuristic,
1422                cfg.sched_seed(),
1423                cfg.sched_sticky_random_param,
1424            ),
1425            turn: 0,
1426            next_turns: Default::default(),
1427            physical_thread_pidfds: Default::default(),
1428            bg_action_pool: Default::default(),
1429            committed_time: Default::default(),
1430            blocked: Default::default(),
1431            vfork_barriers: Default::default(),
1432            pending_run_queue_admissions: Default::default(),
1433            pending_run_queue_removals: Default::default(),
1434            pending_cross_task_signals: Default::default(),
1435            cleared_child_tids: Default::default(),
1436            terminal_deadlock: None,
1437            cancel_killed_thread_rpcs: cfg.cancel_killed_thread_rpcs,
1438            backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals: cfg
1439                .backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals,
1440            backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption: cfg
1441                .backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption,
1442            logically_killed_threads: Default::default(),
1443            exec_incarnations: Default::default(),
1444            deregistration_accounted: Default::default(),
1445            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: cfg.backend_reports_physical_process_exits,
1446            pending_physical_process_exits: Default::default(),
1447            logically_exited_processes: Default::default(),
1448            backend_defers_vfork_child_registration: cfg.backend_defers_vfork_child_registration,
1449            completed_physical_process_exits: Default::default(),
1450            resources: Default::default(),
1451            started_up: Default::default(),
1452            thread_tree: Default::default(),
1453            priorities: Default::default(),
1454            timeslices: Default::default(),
1455            per_thread_timeslice: Default::default(),
1456            per_thread_syscalls: Default::default(),
1457            fuzz_futexes: cfg.fuzz_futexes,
1458            chaos_target_races: cfg.chaos_target_races,
1459            fuzz_prng: Pcg64Mcg::seed_from_u64(cfg.fuzz_seed()),
1460            post_fork_prng: Pcg64Mcg::seed_from_u64(cfg.sched_seed() ^ 0x706f_7374_666f_726b),
1461            happens_before: cfg.happens_before.clone().map(HbRuntime::new),
1462        }
1463    }
1464
1465    /// Record a newly created thread for happens-before `spawn_ordinal`
1466    /// resolution. A no-op unless a happens-before program is active.
1467    pub fn hb_note_spawn(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) {
1468        if let Some(hb) = self.happens_before.as_mut() {
1469            hb.note_spawn(dettid);
1470        }
1471    }
1472
1473    pub(crate) fn register_physical_thread(
1474        &mut self,
1475        dettid: DetTid,
1476        mm: MmId,
1477        physical_pid: i32,
1478        physical_tid: i32,
1479    ) -> std::io::Result<()> {
1480        if physical_pid <= 0 || physical_tid <= 0 {
1481            return Err(std::io::Error::new(
1482                std::io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput,
1483                "host process and thread IDs must be positive",
1484            ));
1485        }
1486        if let Some((known_mm, known_pid, known_tid, _)) = self.physical_thread_pidfds.get(&dettid)
1487        {
1488            if *known_mm == mm && *known_pid == physical_pid && *known_tid == physical_tid {
1489                return Ok(());
1490            }
1491            if *known_mm == mm {
1492                return Err(std::io::Error::new(
1493                    std::io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists,
1494                    format!(
1495                        "scheduler identity {dettid} already names host process {known_pid} thread {known_tid}"
1496                    ),
1497                ));
1498            }
1499        }
1500        let raw_fd = unsafe {
1501            libc::syscall(
1502                libc::SYS_pidfd_open,
1503                physical_tid,
1504                libc::O_EXCL as libc::c_uint,
1505            )
1506        };
1507        if raw_fd < 0 {
1508            return Err(std::io::Error::last_os_error());
1509        }
1510        // SAFETY: pidfd_open returned a new descriptor owned by this process.
1511        let pidfd = unsafe { OwnedFd::from_raw_fd(raw_fd as libc::c_int) };
1512        self.physical_thread_pidfds
1513            .insert(dettid, (mm, physical_pid, physical_tid, pidfd));
1514        Ok(())
1515    }
1516
1517    fn remove_physical_thread(&mut self, dettid: &DetTid, mm: MmId) {
1518        if self
1519            .physical_thread_pidfds
1520            .get(dettid)
1521            .is_some_and(|(registered_mm, ..)| *registered_mm == mm)
1522        {
1523            self.physical_thread_pidfds.remove(dettid);
1524        }
1525    }
1526
1527    #[cfg(test)]
1528    pub(crate) fn physical_thread_identity(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> Option<(MmId, i32, i32)> {
1529        self.physical_thread_pidfds
1530            .get(&dettid)
1531            .map(|(mm, pid, tid, _)| (*mm, *pid, *tid))
1532    }
1533
1534    pub(crate) fn note_process_sigkill(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, detpid: DetPid) {
1535        if !self.backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals {
1536            return;
1537        }
1538        let Some((mm, _, _, _)) = self.physical_thread_pidfds.get(&dettid) else {
1539            self.terminal_deadlock.get_or_insert_with(|| {
1540                format!(
1541                    "HERMIT_DEADLOCK: scheduler cannot complete SIGKILL for dettid {} without its host thread pidfd",
1542                    dettid,
1543                )
1544            });
1545            return;
1546        };
1547        let mm = *mm;
1548        self.logically_kill_thread(&dettid, &detpid, mm);
1549    }
1550
1551    fn should_synthesize_child_exit_signal(&self, parent: DetTid) -> bool {
1552        !self.physical_thread_pidfds.contains_key(&parent)
1553    }
1554
1555    /// Handle a happens-before checkpoint issued by `dettid` after its `count`th
1556    /// intercepted syscall (see `Detcore::handle_syscall_event`).
1557    ///
1558    /// Grants passage (firing every anchor at `SyscallCount(count)` on this
1559    /// thread and re-admitting any parked threads whose gate may now be open)
1560    /// unless a reached anchor is the AFTER endpoint of a Hard edge whose BEFORE
1561    /// anchor has not fired, in which case the thread is parked out of the run
1562    /// queue until a later firing wakes it. Mirrors the `SleepUntil` park/skip
1563    /// protocol: the request/response ivars are left intact so the re-admitted
1564    /// thread re-evaluates this same checkpoint on its next turn.
1565    fn hb_checkpoint(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, count: u64) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
1566        let (reached, blocked) = {
1567            let hb = self
1568                .happens_before
1569                .as_ref()
1570                .expect("hb checkpoint issued without a happens-before program");
1571            let reached = hb.anchors_at_syscall(dettid, count);
1572            let blocked = reached.iter().any(|name| hb.anchor_blocked(name));
1573            (reached, blocked)
1574        };
1575
1576        if reached.is_empty() {
1577            // No anchor addresses this (thread, count); nothing to gate or fire.
1578            return Ok(());
1579        }
1580
1581        if blocked {
1582            info!(
1583                "[scheduler] >>>>>>>\n\n NONCOMMIT turn {}, SKIP dettid {} held at happens-before \
1584                 anchor(s) {:?} (syscall count {}) awaiting a BEFORE anchor",
1585                self.turn, dettid, reached, count
1586            );
1587            self.happens_before.as_mut().unwrap().parked.insert(dettid);
1588            return self.skip_turn_blocked(dettid);
1589        }
1590
1591        // Grant passage: fire the reached anchors. Only wake parked threads when a
1592        // new anchor actually fired, so an idempotent re-grant causes no churn.
1593        let mut newly_fired = false;
1594        {
1595            let hb = self.happens_before.as_mut().unwrap();
1596            for name in &reached {
1597                if hb.fired.insert(name.clone()) {
1598                    newly_fired = true;
1599                }
1600            }
1601        }
1602        if newly_fired {
1603            debug!(
1604                "[happens-before] dettid {} fired anchor(s) {:?} at syscall count {}",
1605                dettid, reached, count
1606            );
1607            // Defer the actual re-admission: we are inside `block_for_one_resource`
1608            // with a `tentative_pop` selection live, and pushing to the run queue
1609            // now would trip the queue's transaction assertion. `step3` flushes.
1610            self.happens_before.as_mut().unwrap().wake_pending = true;
1611        }
1612        Ok(())
1613    }
1614
1615    /// If a happens-before anchor fired since the last check, re-admit every
1616    /// parked thread to the run queue so it re-evaluates its gate on its next
1617    /// turn. Threads still blocked re-park; the request/response ivars are
1618    /// untouched, so no request needs re-filling. Deterministic: parked threads
1619    /// are iterated in `DetTid` order.
1620    ///
1621    /// Called from `step3_peek` *before* the turn's `tentative_pop`, the only
1622    /// safe point to push to the run queue: anchors fire deep inside
1623    /// `block_for_one_resource` while a selection transaction is live, so the
1624    /// actual re-admission must be deferred to here.
1625    fn hb_flush_wakes(&mut self) {
1626        match self.happens_before.as_mut() {
1627            Some(hb) if hb.wake_pending => hb.wake_pending = false,
1628            _ => return,
1629        }
1630        let parked: Vec<DetTid> = self
1631            .happens_before
1632            .as_ref()
1633            .map(|hb| hb.parked.iter().copied().collect())
1634            .unwrap_or_default();
1635        for dettid in parked {
1636            self.happens_before.as_mut().unwrap().parked.remove(&dettid);
1637            if !self.run_queue.contains_tid(dettid) {
1638                let pos = self.runqueue_push_back(dettid);
1639                trace!(
1640                    "[happens-before] re-admitting parked dettid {} at queue position {}",
1641                    dettid, pos
1642                );
1643            }
1644        }
1645    }
1646
1647    /// Fill in a resource request, which is exactly what might make the next logical
1648    /// step become unblocked.
1649    pub fn request_put(
1650        &mut self,
1651        req: &Ivar<SchedRequest>,
1652        rs: Resources,
1653        _global_time: &Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
1654    ) {
1655        // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1656        // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1041): A guest resource-request RPC can race an
1657        // asynchronous signal delivery. `force_unblock_thread` replaces
1658        // `next_turns[tid].req` with an already-full Ivar carrying an
1659        // `InboundSignal` request (see the `Ivar::full` at the bottom of this
1660        // file). If the guest's own resource-request then lands on that same
1661        // turn, the previous unconditional `req.put()` panicked with "Ivar
1662        // multiple put" (observed intermittently on `timeout 5 echo hi` under
1663        // `--strict --verify`, where a 5s SIGALRM races the child's `wait4`).
1664        // Tolerate exactly that case: drop the late guest request and let the
1665        // signal turn win — the interrupted syscall (e.g. `rt_sigsuspend`)
1666        // restarts and re-issues a fresh request on the next turn. Any other
1667        // double-put still panics, preserving the write-once Ivar invariant.
1668        // Mirrors the `try_put` guard in `logically_kill_thread` (PR-845).
1669        if let Some(dropped) = req.try_put(Ok(rs)) {
1670            let tolerated = matches!(
1671                req.try_read(),
1672                Some(Ok(existing))
1673                    if existing
1674                        .resources
1675                        .keys()
1676                        .any(|r| {
1677                            matches!(
1678                                r,
1679                                ResourceID::InboundSignal(_) | ResourceID::WaitidSignals(_)
1680                            )
1681                        })
1682            );
1683            if tolerated {
1684                trace!(
1685                    "[request_put] dropping late guest request {:?}; an async inbound signal already filled the request for this turn (req {})",
1686                    dropped, req
1687                );
1688            } else {
1689                panic!(
1690                    "Ivar multiple put exception in request_put! Attempted to write {:?} to {}; existing content is not an inbound-signal request.",
1691                    dropped, req
1692                );
1693            }
1694        }
1695    }
1696
1697    /// Poll the resource request and *if* it is not currently observed to be full, return
1698    /// the IVar that *will* contain it in the future.
1699    fn check_request(&self, det_tid: &DetTid) -> Option<Ivar<SchedRequest>> {
1700        let nextturn = self.next_turns.get(det_tid).unwrap_or_else(|| {
1701            panic!(
1702                "[check_request] internal error: dettid {} queued but missing entry in next_turns",
1703                det_tid
1704            )
1705        });
1706        if nextturn.req.try_read().is_none() {
1707            Some(nextturn.req.clone())
1708        } else {
1709            None
1710        }
1711    }
1712
1713    /// Returns None if all are parked, otherwise the unfilled request of the next we're waiting on.
1714    fn are_all_quiesced(&self) -> Option<Ivar<SchedRequest>> {
1715        // Skip raw TIDs whose old run-queue incarnation is pending removal.
1716        // `Retire` targets have no `next_turns` entry, while
1717        // `ReplaceThenAdmit` targets have a fresh registration that must not be
1718        // waited on until the drain removes the old physical slot and admits
1719        // that replacement.
1720        self.run_queue
1721            .tids()
1722            .filter(|dt| !self.pending_run_queue_removals.contains_key(dt))
1723            .find_map(|dt| self.check_request(dt))
1724    }
1725
1726    /// Try to pop the next event from the sorted list of stacktrace_events, if it matches the given
1727    /// index.  This is idempotent, because subsequent attempts will just fizzle.
1728    fn try_pop_stacktrace_event(
1729        &mut self,
1730        current_ix: u64,
1731        observed: &SchedEvent,
1732    ) -> MaybePrintStack {
1733        let mut result = None;
1734        if let Some(iter) = &mut self.stacktrace_events
1735            && let Some((next_ix, event, m_path)) = iter.peek()
1736        {
1737            let go = if let Some(ev) = event {
1738                (*next_ix == current_ix && events_consistent(observed, ev))
1739                    || events_match(observed, ev)
1740            } else {
1741                *next_ix == current_ix
1742            };
1743            if go {
1744                info!(
1745                    "Now output stack trace for scheduled event #{} = {}:",
1746                    current_ix, observed,
1747                );
1748                if m_path.is_none() {
1749                    eprintln!(
1750                        "\nPrinting stack trace for scheduled event #{} = {}:",
1751                        current_ix, observed,
1752                    );
1753                }
1754                result = Some(m_path.clone());
1755                let _ = iter.next();
1756            }
1757        }
1758        result
1759    }
1760
1761    /// Verify that the event we're replaying matches what just happened.  Set up the next
1762    /// (replayed) event to run.  Return true if the current thread will keep running and false if
1763    /// it needs to be descheduled.
1764    ///
1765    /// PreReq: we're running under --replay-schedule-from
1766    pub fn consume_schedevent(&mut self, observed: &SchedEvent) -> ConsumeResult {
1767        debug_assert!(self.replayer.is_some());
1768        let mytid = observed.dettid;
1769
1770        if let Some((ix, action)) = self.replayer.as_mut().map(|r| {
1771            let current_ix = r.traced_event_count;
1772            (current_ix, r.observe_event(observed))
1773        }) {
1774            let print_stack = self.try_pop_stacktrace_event(ix, observed);
1775            debug!("Next ReplayAction = {:?}", action);
1776
1777            match action {
1778                replayer::ReplayAction::Continue(timeslice_remaining) => {
1779                    return ConsumeResult {
1780                        keep_running: true,
1781                        print_stack,
1782                        event_ix: ix,
1783                        timeslice_remaining,
1784                    };
1785                }
1786                replayer::ReplayAction::Stop(StopReason::FatalDesync) => immediate_fatal_exit(),
1787                replayer::ReplayAction::Stop(StopReason::ReplayExausted) => immediate_fatal_exit(),
1788                replayer::ReplayAction::ContextSwitch(is_now, new_tid, timeslice_remaining) => {
1789                    self.requeue_with_new_priority(mytid, REPLAY_DEFERRED_PRIORITY);
1790                    self.requeue_with_new_priority(new_tid, REPLAY_FOREGROUND_PRIORITY);
1791                    if !self.run_queue.contains_tid(new_tid) {
1792                        // If it is not yet in next_turns, that is because it was JUST spawned and
1793                        // hasn't showed up yet, but it will by the next scheduler turn.
1794                        if self.next_turns.contains_key(&new_tid) {
1795                            tracing::warn!(
1796                                "Attempted to context switch to tid {}, but it is not runnable atm. This could be legitimate if it is awoken by another thread exiting (futex wake).",
1797                                new_tid
1798                            );
1799                            // TODO(T138906107): make this a fatal error when RESYNC capability is robust enough.
1800                            // immediate_fatal_exit();
1801                        }
1802                    }
1803                    self.timeslices.insert(new_tid, timeslice_remaining);
1804                    return ConsumeResult {
1805                        keep_running: !is_now,
1806                        print_stack,
1807                        event_ix: ix,
1808                        timeslice_remaining,
1809                    };
1810                }
1811            };
1812        }
1813
1814        ConsumeResult {
1815            keep_running: true,
1816            print_stack: None,
1817            event_ix: 0,
1818            timeslice_remaining: None,
1819        }
1820    }
1821
1822    /// Remove a thread from the deterministic scheduler.  In order to call this, the precondition
1823    /// is that this thread will execute no further (visible) instructions.
1824    ///
1825    /// This is called while the guest is running, not in the middle of a scheduler turn.
1826    ///
1827    /// This is IDEMPOTENT, and it may indeed be called twice, both to proactively remove a thread,
1828    /// and then reactively in response to an exit hook.
1829    pub fn logically_kill_thread(&mut self, dtid: &DetTid, detpid: &DetPid, mm: MmId) {
1830        if self.cancel_killed_thread_rpcs {
1831            self.logically_killed_threads.insert(*dtid);
1832        }
1833        // Remove from the runnable queue at the next deterministic drain. This
1834        // is safe even if an asynchronous exec reconnect races a live
1835        // tentative_pop: the handler never reaches the run queue's mutation
1836        // guard and cannot poison the scheduler mutex.
1837        self.deschedule_or_defer(*dtid);
1838        // Remove from all non-runnable pools:
1839        self.remove_blocking_entries(dtid);
1840        self.remove_physical_thread(dtid, mm);
1841
1842        let _ = self.priorities.remove(dtid);
1843        match self.next_turns.remove(dtid) {
1844            None => {
1845                trace!(
1846                    "logically_kill_thread: thread already removed from scheduler: {}",
1847                    &dtid
1848                );
1849            }
1850            Some(nextturn) => {
1851                info!(
1852                    "logically_kill: Scheduler removing all knowledge of [det]tid {} in pid {}..",
1853                    dtid, detpid
1854                );
1855                // Put in a dummy request to unblock the scheduler that might be
1856                // waiting for the thread to park.
1857                //
1858                // WARNING: this try_put should potentially turn back into a put(), if we can narrow
1859                // down the exit scenarios and ensure that they happen when the guest is running and
1860                // has NOT filled its request to the scheduler yet.
1861                let request_was_pending = nextturn.req.try_put(Err(ThreadExited)).is_some();
1862                if request_was_pending && self.cancel_killed_thread_rpcs {
1863                    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1864                    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-845): Review killed-thread RPC cancellation.
1865                    nextturn.resp.try_put(SchedResponse::Signaled(None));
1866                }
1867                self.wake_futex_child_cleartid(
1868                    FutexID::private(mm, nextturn.child_tid_addr),
1869                    *dtid,
1870                );
1871            }
1872        }
1873
1874        // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1875        // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
1876        let live_process_thread = self
1877            .thread_tree
1878            .my_thread_group(detpid)
1879            .into_iter()
1880            .any(|tid| self.next_turns.contains_key(&tid));
1881        if !live_process_thread {
1882            let _ = self.begin_physical_process_exit(*detpid);
1883            self.logically_exited_processes.insert(*detpid);
1884            if let Some(parent) = self.thread_tree.parent_process(detpid) {
1885                self.wake_child_waiters(parent, *detpid);
1886            }
1887            self.blocked.timed_waiters.remove_process_timers(*detpid);
1888        }
1889    }
1890
1891    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1892    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1173): Review SaBRe exec incarnation reconciliation.
1893    /// Apply Linux's successful-exec rule when a backend reloads its tool.
1894    ///
1895    /// Every sibling disappears. If a non-leader called exec, Linux also changes
1896    /// that surviving task's TID to the process leader's TID. In that case the old
1897    /// caller registration is retired and a fresh leader registration is installed
1898    /// before it is removed, so process-exit barriers cannot observe a transiently
1899    /// empty thread group.
1900    pub fn reconnect_after_exec(&mut self, reconnect: ExecReconnect) -> Vec<DetTid> {
1901        let ExecReconnect {
1902            caller,
1903            new_leader,
1904            detpid,
1905            pre_exec_mm,
1906            post_exec_mm,
1907            child_tid_addr,
1908            reconnect_priority,
1909        } = reconnect;
1910        let group = self.thread_tree.my_thread_group(&detpid);
1911        assert!(group.contains(&caller));
1912        assert!(group.contains(&new_leader));
1913        self.exec_incarnations.insert(new_leader, post_exec_mm);
1914
1915        if caller == new_leader {
1916            let siblings: Vec<_> = group.into_iter().filter(|tid| *tid != caller).collect();
1917            for sibling in &siblings {
1918                self.logically_kill_thread(sibling, &detpid, pre_exec_mm);
1919                self.timeslices.remove(sibling);
1920            }
1921            self.remove_exec_vfork_barriers(&siblings);
1922            return siblings;
1923        }
1924
1925        let survivor_priority = self
1926            .priorities
1927            .get(&caller)
1928            .copied()
1929            .or(reconnect_priority)
1930            .expect("exec caller must have a scheduler priority");
1931        let mut retired = Vec::new();
1932        for old_tid in group.into_iter().filter(|tid| *tid != caller) {
1933            self.logically_kill_thread(&old_tid, &detpid, pre_exec_mm);
1934            self.timeslices.remove(&old_tid);
1935            retired.push(old_tid);
1936        }
1937
1938        // The leader identity was occupied by a thread the kernel destroyed as
1939        // part of this exec. This is the one intentional exception to permanent
1940        // raw-TID tombstones: the pending exec record proves why Linux reused it.
1941        self.logically_killed_threads.remove(&new_leader);
1942        self.deregistration_accounted.remove(&new_leader);
1943        self.pending_physical_process_exits.remove(&detpid);
1944        assert!(
1945            self.next_turns
1946                .insert(
1947                    new_leader,
1948                    ThreadNextTurn {
1949                        dettid: new_leader,
1950                        child_tid_addr,
1951                        req: Ivar::new(),
1952                        resp: Ivar::new(),
1953                    },
1954                )
1955                .is_none(),
1956            "retired exec leader still had a scheduler registration"
1957        );
1958        self.priorities.insert(new_leader, survivor_priority);
1959        if let Some(writer) = &mut self.preemption_writer {
1960            writer.set_current(new_leader, survivor_priority);
1961        }
1962        // Post-exec reconnection can arrive asynchronously on backends whose
1963        // exec-child self-bootstraps outside a scheduler turn (DBT), so route
1964        // the new leader's admission (a run-queue *push*) through the
1965        // tentative-safe buffer rather than pushing directly.
1966        //
1967        // The exec caller's fresh next-turn request is the causal anchor. Step5
1968        // installed that empty Ivar before the caller began executing exec, so
1969        // the daemon cannot pass step1 while the successful exec is in flight.
1970        // This handler records the complete old-leader removal/replacement
1971        // admission pair before `logically_kill_thread(caller)` resolves that
1972        // request. The scheduler mutex then prevents step2 from observing only
1973        // half of the handoff. Host delay can move the handler relative to the
1974        // daemon's wait, but cannot change first-drain membership.
1975        //
1976        // The *removals* in this handler (`logically_kill_thread` ->
1977        // `run_queue.remove_tid`, for the exec caller and its siblings, above
1978        // and below) are likewise tentative-safe: `logically_kill_thread` now
1979        // routes the run-queue removal through `deschedule_or_defer`, which
1980        // buffers it to the same deterministic `step2` drain. The old leader's
1981        // removal is explicitly classified as `ReplaceThenAdmit`, so the drain
1982        // removes its physical queue slot without cancelling the new
1983        // incarnation. `are_all_quiesced` filters every pending removal key;
1984        // ordinary targets are logically dead, while the replacement key is not
1985        // runnable until that old slot has been removed and its admission
1986        // applied. No handler mutates the queue inside a tentative window.
1987        self.replace_retired_run_queue_incarnation(new_leader, AdmitIntent::Fixed(AdmitSide::Back));
1988        self.started_up.try_put(());
1989
1990        self.logically_kill_thread(&caller, &detpid, pre_exec_mm);
1991        self.timeslices.remove(&caller);
1992        retired.push(caller);
1993        self.remove_exec_vfork_barriers(&retired);
1994        retired
1995    }
1996
1997    fn remove_exec_vfork_barriers(&mut self, retired: &[DetTid]) {
1998        self.vfork_barriers.retain(|parent, child| {
1999            !retired.contains(parent) && !child.is_some_and(|tid| retired.contains(&tid))
2000        });
2001    }
2002
2003    #[cfg(test)]
2004    pub(crate) fn vfork_barrier_mentions(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> bool {
2005        self.vfork_barriers
2006            .iter()
2007            .any(|(parent, child)| *parent == dettid || child == &Some(dettid))
2008    }

2011    pub(crate) fn install_test_vfork_barrier(&mut self, parent: DetTid, child: DetTid) {
2012        self.vfork_barriers.insert(parent, Some(child));
2013    }

2016    pub(crate) fn install_test_exec_incarnation(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, mm: MmId) {
2017        self.exec_incarnations.insert(dettid, mm);
2018    }

2020    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1023): Review fail-closed SaBRe thread tombstones.
2021    pub(crate) fn thread_is_logically_killed(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> bool {
2022        self.cancel_killed_thread_rpcs && self.logically_killed_threads.contains(&dettid)
2023    }
2024
2025    pub(crate) fn rpc_incarnation_matches(&self, dettid: DetTid, mm: MmId) -> bool {
2026        self.exec_incarnations
2027            .get(&dettid)
2028            .is_none_or(|expected| *expected == mm)
2029    }

2031    /// Mark a physical exit cleanup as accounted. Non-cancelling backends preserve their existing
2032    /// behavior; SaBRe teardown may deliver the cleanup after an earlier logical tombstone.
2033    pub(crate) fn note_deregistration_accounted(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) -> bool {
2034        !self.cancel_killed_thread_rpcs || self.deregistration_accounted.insert(dettid)
2035    }
2036
2037    /// Install a barrier between SaBRe's logical process-leader exit hook and the final ptrace
2038    /// wait status. Other backends retain their existing lifecycle behavior.
2039    pub(crate) fn begin_physical_process_exit(&mut self, detpid: DetPid) -> bool {
2040        if self.backend_reports_physical_process_exits {
2041            self.completed_physical_process_exits.remove(&detpid);
2042            let inserted = self.pending_physical_process_exits.insert(detpid);
2043            if inserted {
2044                trace!(
2045                    "[detcore, dpid {}] waiting for final physical process exit",
2046                    detpid
2047                );
2048            }
2049            inserted
2050        } else {
2051            false
2052        }
2053    }
2054
2055    /// Release the exact process barrier when the ptrace supervisor receives its final `Exited`
2056    /// or `Signaled` wait status. At that lifecycle point the process is physically waitable.
2057    pub(crate) fn complete_physical_process_exit(&mut self, detpid: DetPid) -> bool {
2058        let removed = self.pending_physical_process_exits.remove(&detpid);
2059        if removed {
2060            self.completed_physical_process_exits.insert(detpid);
2061            self.wake_physical_child_waiters(detpid);
2062        }
2063        removed
2064    }
2065
2066    /// Release every physical-exit barrier after the backend supervisor has drained all tracees.
2067    pub(crate) fn release_all_physical_process_exits(&mut self) -> usize {
2068        let children = std::mem::take(&mut self.pending_physical_process_exits);
2069        let released = children.len();
2070        for child in children {
2071            self.completed_physical_process_exits.insert(child);
2072            self.wake_physical_child_waiters(child);
2073        }
2074        released
2075    }
2076
2077    /// Remove entries from everywhere that non-runnable threads lurk.
2078    fn remove_blocking_entries(&mut self, dtid: &DetTid) {
2079        self.blocked.timed_waiters.remove(*dtid);
2080        let _ = self.blocked.external_io_blockers.remove(dtid);
2081        let _ = self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.remove(dtid);
2082        self.blocked.timed_out_futex_waiters.remove(dtid);
2083        self.blocked.sigchld_deferred.remove(dtid);
2084        self.blocked.sigchld_ready.remove(dtid);
2085        self.blocked.child_waiters.remove(dtid);
2086        self.blocked.physical_child_ready.remove(dtid);
2087        self.blocked.physical_child_waiters.retain(|_, waiters| {
2088            waiters.remove(dtid);
2089            !waiters.is_empty()
2090        });
2091        self.pending_run_queue_admissions.remove(dtid);
2092        let _ = self.remove_futex_waiter(dtid);
2093    }
2094
2095    fn remove_futex_waiter(&mut self, dettid: &DetTid) -> bool {
2096        let mut removed = 0;
2097        self.blocked.futex_waiters.retain(|_, waiters| {
2098            let before = waiters.len();
2099            waiters.retain(|waiter| &waiter.dettid != dettid);
2100            removed += before - waiters.len();
2101            !waiters.is_empty()
2102        });
2103        assert!(removed <= 1, "thread was registered on multiple futexes");
2104        removed == 1
2105    }
2106
2107    /// Put a Futex waiter to sleep, to be awoken by `wake_futex_waiter`.
2108    pub fn sleep_futex_waiter(
2109        &mut self,
2110        dettid: &DetTid,
2111        futexid: FutexID,
2112        maybe_timeout: Option<LogicalTime>,
2113        bitset: u32,
2114    ) {
2115        let nxt = self
2116            .next_turns
2117            .get(dettid)
2118            .expect("Missing next_turns entry");
2119        let entry: &mut Vec<_> = self.blocked.futex_waiters.entry(futexid).or_default();
2120        entry.push(FutexWaiter {
2121            dettid: *dettid,
2122            response: nxt.resp.clone(),
2123            bitset,
2124        });
2125        // When we park, we use a resource request to signal WHAT we're blocking on.  But this is
2126        // not quite the same as when an active thread in the runqueue blocks on a resource, because
2127        // we're not actually waiting on the scheduler giving us the resource.  We're waiting in the
2128        // futex_waiters pool until a waker comes along.
2129        let mut rsrc = Resources::new(*dettid);
2130        rsrc.insert(ResourceID::FutexWait, Permission::R);
2131        nxt.req.put(Ok(rsrc));
2132        trace!(
2133            "[dtid {}] Waiter blocking on futex {:?}, now {} waiters, on {}",
2134            &dettid,
2135            &futexid,
2136            entry.len(),
2137            nxt.resp,
2138        );
2139        // A futex with timeout waits in both the futex_waiters and timed_events structures:
2140        if let Some(target_time) = maybe_timeout {
2141            self.blocked.timed_waiters.insert(target_time, *dettid);
2142        }
2143    }
2144
2145    /// Reschedule a single thread that has been blocked on futex.
2146    pub fn wake_futex_waiter(&mut self, waiter: FutexWaiter) {
2147        let waiterid = waiter.dettid;
2148        let waiter_ivar = waiter.response;
2149        debug_assert!(!self.run_queue.contains_tid(waiterid));
2150
2151        // If it was registered as a waiter-with-timeout, remove it:
2152        self.blocked.timed_waiters.remove(waiterid);
2153
2154        // Put the woken thread back into circulation:
2155        let pos = self.runqueue_push_back(waiterid);
2156        trace!(
2157            "[detcore] Woke one thread, dtid: {}, ivar {}, scheduled at position {}",
2158            &waiterid, &waiter_ivar, pos,
2159        );
2160        let nxt = self
2161            .next_turns
2162            .get_mut(&waiterid)
2163            .expect("Thread must have an entry in next_turns");
2164        assert_futex_request(nxt);
2165        // N.B. We don't write the response here.  That's for the scheduler to do.
2166        // But with a place in the queue, and a request filled, this thread
2167        // is ready to run in normal order.
2168    }
2169
2170    fn choose_futex_wakees(
2171        &mut self,
2172        vec: &mut Vec<FutexWaiter>,
2173        num_woken: usize,
2174    ) -> Vec<FutexWaiter> {
2175        if self.fuzz_futexes {
2176            let rng = &mut self.fuzz_prng;
2177            debug!(
2178                "[fuzz-futexes] selecting {} tids, pre shuffle: {:?}",
2179                num_woken,
2180                vec.iter().map(|x| x.dettid).collect::<Vec<DetTid>>()
2181            );
2182
2183            // No need to actually use the results here since vec was mutated:
2184            let (_extracted, _remain) = &vec[..].partial_shuffle(rng, num_woken);
2185
2186            info!(
2187                "[fuzz-futexes] selecting {} tids, post shuffle: {:?}",
2188                num_woken,
2189                vec.iter().map(|x| x.dettid).collect::<Vec<DetTid>>()
2190            );
2191        }
2192        // just take the first N, in whatever deterministic order they are in:
2193        vec.split_off(vec.len() - num_woken)
2194    }
2195
2196    fn take_futex_wakees(
2197        &mut self,
2198        futexid: FutexID,
2199        max_to_wake: i32,
2200        wake_mask: u32,
2201    ) -> Vec<FutexWaiter> {
2202        if max_to_wake == 0 {
2203            trace!("[detcore] Futex wake of 0 waiters necessarily fizzles...");
2204            return Vec::new();
2205        }
2206        let mut vec: Vec<FutexWaiter> = {
2207            match self.blocked.futex_waiters.get_mut(&futexid) {
2208                None => {
2209                    trace!(
2210                        "[detcore] Futex wake {} waiters FIZZLED -- none waiting",
2211                        max_to_wake
2212                    );
2213                    return Vec::new();
2214                }
2215                Some(r) => std::mem::take(r),
2216            }
2217        };
2218        trace!(
2219            "Waking up to {} Futex waiters, out of {} waiting.",
2220            max_to_wake,
2221            vec.len(),
2222        );
2223        let mut matching = take_matching_futex_waiters(&mut vec, wake_mask);
2224        let num_woken: usize = std::cmp::min(matching.len(), max_to_wake.try_into().unwrap());
2225        let to_wake = self.choose_futex_wakees(&mut matching, num_woken);
2226
2227        assert_eq!(to_wake.len(), num_woken);
2228        vec.extend(matching);
2229        // Put back what wasn't woken up:
2230        if !vec.is_empty() {
2231            let junk = self.blocked.futex_waiters.insert(futexid, vec);
2232            assert!(junk.unwrap().is_empty());
2233        }
2234        to_wake
2235    }
2236
2237    /// Reschedule all threads blocked on a particular futex.
2238    pub fn wake_futex_waiters(
2239        &mut self,
2240        _waker_dettid: DetTid,
2241        futexid: FutexID,
2242        max_to_wake: i32,
2243        wake_mask: u32,
2244    ) -> u64 {
2245        let to_wake = self.take_futex_wakees(futexid, max_to_wake, wake_mask);
2246        let num_woken = to_wake.len();
2247        for waiter in to_wake {
2248            self.wake_futex_waiter(waiter);
2249        }
2250        num_woken as u64
2251    }
2252
2253    /// Record futex wakes delivered by a physical-exit callback for the next
2254    /// deterministic run-queue drain.
2255    pub(crate) fn wake_futex_waiters_after_exit(
2256        &mut self,
2257        wakes: &[(DetTid, FutexID)],
2258    ) -> Vec<u64> {
2259        wakes
2260            .iter()
2261            .map(|(_owner, futexid)| {
2262                let to_wake = self.take_futex_wakees(*futexid, 1, u32::MAX);
2263                let num_woken = to_wake.len();
2264                for waiter in to_wake {
2265                    let waiterid = waiter.dettid;
2266                    self.blocked.timed_waiters.remove(waiterid);
2267                    let next_turn = self
2268                        .next_turns
2269                        .get_mut(&waiterid)
2270                        .expect("Thread must have an entry in next_turns");
2271                    assert_futex_request(next_turn);
2272                    self.admit_to_run_queue(waiterid, AdmitIntent::Fixed(AdmitSide::Back));
2273                }
2274                num_woken as u64
2275            })
2276            .collect()
2277    }
2278
2279    /// Simulate the effect of CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID.
2280    pub fn wake_futex_child_cleartid(&mut self, futid: FutexID, dettid: DetTid) {
2281        self.cleared_child_tids.insert(futid, dettid);
2282        debug!(
2283            "simulate CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID on futex {:?}, wake one",
2284            futid
2285        );
2286        // Wakes only one thread, as per:
2287        // https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/set_tid_address.2.html
2288        self.wake_futex_waiters(dettid, futid, 1, u32::MAX);
2289    }
2290
2291    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2292    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-845): Review late CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID wait recovery.
2293    /// Whether a futex word still names the child that was logically cleared.
2294    pub(crate) fn child_tid_was_cleared(&self, futid: FutexID, observed: i32) -> bool {
2295        self.cleared_child_tids
2296            .get(&futid)
2297            .is_some_and(|dettid| dettid.as_raw() == observed)
2298    }
2299
2300    /// Step: Before we select which thread to run, first we check if some internal data
2301    /// structure maintenance is necessary, i.e. moving timed events from the waiting pool
2302    /// to the run queue. It manipulates scheduler data structures accordingly.
2303    fn step2_process_blocked(
2304        &mut self,
2305        global_time: &Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
2306    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
2307        // Apply run-queue mutations deferred by asynchronous global-request
2308        // handlers first, at this fixed deterministic point, before any early
2309        // return below and before step3 opens a tentative-pop window. Removals
2310        // drain before admissions so a thread killed while an admission was
2311        // still buffered is not re-enqueued.
2312        self.drain_pending_run_queue_removals();
2313        self.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
2314        self.drain_pending_run_queue_admissions();
2315        if self
2316            .blocked
2317            .physical_child_waiters
2318            .keys()
2319            .any(|child| self.pending_physical_process_exits.contains(child))
2320        {
2321            // A reaper has reached the deterministic logical-exit boundary.
2322            // Do not let runnable siblings advance while the backend catches
2323            // up to physical waitability; completion admits the waiter through
2324            // the next deterministic drain.
2325            std::thread::yield_now();
2326            return Err(SkipTurn);
2327        }
2328        self.step2a_wait_for_vfork_barrier()?;
2329        self.step2b_process_timed(); // May populate run_queue.
2330        self.step2c_process_io_blockers()?;
2331        self.step2e_process_signal_deferred(); // May populate run_queue.
2332        self.step2d_handle_empty_queue(global_time)?;
2333        Ok(())
2334    }
2335
2336    /// Re-admit parents whose host-async `SIGCHLD` was parked in
2337    /// `blocked.sigchld_deferred` (see `block_for_one_resource`). Uses the same
2338    /// deterministic-work-first gate as `step2c_process_io_blockers`: a deferred
2339    /// signal is delivered only once the run queue holds no ordinary (non-poller)
2340    /// guest work, so its commit order is fixed by the scheduler rather than by
2341    /// host signal-arrival timing. Runs after external-IO harvesting so a ready
2342    /// IO continuation is always ordered ahead of a deferred signal.
2343    fn step2e_process_signal_deferred(&mut self) {
2344        if self.blocked.sigchld_deferred.is_empty() {
2345            return;
2346        }
2347        let only_pollers = match self.run_queue.first_priority() {
2348            Some(fp) => fp >= LAST_PRIORITY,
2349            None => true,
2350        };
2351        if !self.run_queue.is_empty() && !only_pollers {
2352            return;
2353        }
2354        // BTreeSet drains in sorted DetTid order, giving a canonical admission
2355        // order when several parents are owed a signal at the same quiescence.
2356        let ready = std::mem::take(&mut self.blocked.sigchld_deferred);
2357        for dtid in ready {
2358            info!("[step2] Re-admit deferred SIGCHLD for dtid {:?}", dtid);
2359            self.blocked.sigchld_ready.insert(dtid);
2360            self.run_queue.push_eager_io_repoll(dtid);
2361        }
2362    }
2363
2364    /// Keep scheduling inside an active vfork until the parent can continue.
2365    /// Before child registration no guest may run; afterward step 3 admits only
2366    /// the child. A failed clone reaches the parent continuation without a child.
2367    ///
2368    /// On the ptrace backend the kernel keeps the vfork parent blocked inside the injected
2369    /// `clone(2)` until the child execs or exits, so a registered child (barrier `Some`) is always
2370    /// present by the time the parent posts its continuation; an unfulfilled barrier (`None`) at
2371    /// that point therefore means the clone failed and the barrier must be dropped. On a backend
2372    /// that defers the child spawn (see `backend_defers_vfork_child_registration`, e.g. KVM) the
2373    /// child registers only *after* the parent posts its continuation, so an unfulfilled barrier at
2374    /// parent continuation means the child is still on its way and the barrier must be kept.
2375    fn step2a_wait_for_vfork_barrier(&mut self) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
2376        // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2377        // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1152): Review deferred vfork child registration.
2378        let defers_registration = self.backend_defers_vfork_child_registration;
2379        let completed_parents: Vec<_> = self
2380            .vfork_barriers
2381            .iter()
2382            .filter_map(|(parent, registered_child)| {
2383                let child_registered = registered_child.is_some();
2384                let remove = match self
2385                    .next_turns
2386                    .get(parent)
2387                    .and_then(|turn| turn.req.try_read())
2388                {
2389                    // The parent exited: there will be no child; drop the barrier.
2390                    Some(Err(ThreadExited)) => true,
2391                    Some(Ok(resources)) => {
2392                        let vfork_failed = resources
2393                            .resources
2394                            .keys()
2395                            .any(|resource| matches!(resource, ResourceID::VforkFailed(_)));
2396                        let at_continue = resources.resources.keys().any(|resource| {
2397                            matches!(resource, ResourceID::BlockedExternalContinue(_))
2398                        });
2399                        // A failed injected clone is an explicit deterministic outcome: no child
2400                        // can ever register, so cancel the barrier on every backend. Successful
2401                        // deferred spawns retain the ordinary continuation and keep waiting.
2402                        // At parent continuation, drop a fulfilled barrier as normal cleanup. An
2403                        // unfulfilled barrier is a failed clone only when the backend kept the
2404                        // parent blocked until the child registered; when the backend defers child
2405                        // registration the child is still coming, so keep waiting.
2406                        vfork_failed || (at_continue && (child_registered || !defers_registration))
2407                    }
2408                    _ => false,
2409                };
2410                remove.then_some(*parent)
2411            })
2412            .collect();
2413        for parent in completed_parents {
2414            self.vfork_barriers.remove(&parent);
2415        }
2416
2417        if self.vfork_barriers.values().all(Option::is_some) {
2418            Ok(())
2419        } else {
2420            trace!(
2421                "waiting for vfork child registration from parents {:?}",
2422                self.vfork_barriers
2423            );
2424            Err(SkipTurn)
2425        }
2426    }
2427
2428    /// Check whether it is time for the *earliest* time-based event to execute INSTEAD of
2429    /// dispatching from the normal run queue.  Manipulates scheduler data structures
2430    /// accordingly.
2431    fn step2b_process_timed(&mut self) {
2432        if let Some((time_ns, evt)) = self
2433            .blocked
2434            .timed_waiters
2435            .pop_if_before(self.committed_time)
2436        {
2437            match evt {
2438                TimedEvent::ThreadEvt(dtid) => self.wake_timed_event(time_ns, dtid),
2439                TimedEvent::SignalEvt(
2440                    timed_waiters::SignalTimerId::ChildExit { parent, .. },
2441                    dtid,
2442                    sig,
2443                ) => {
2444                    // Deterministic child-exit SIGCHLD. If the host-async signal
2445                    // already arrived and was parked by the InboundSignal deferral
2446                    // gate, release it now at this logical time — that real signal
2447                    // is sufficient, so do not also synthesize one (avoids a
2448                    // duplicate delivery). Otherwise synthesize the delivery so the
2449                    // parent is notified deterministically regardless of host
2450                    // signal latency. Either way mark the parent `sigchld_ready` so
2451                    // its InboundSignal turn is granted here rather than deferred a
2452                    // second time. Releasing the deferred signal at a logical
2453                    // deadline (rather than only at run-queue quiescence, as
2454                    // step2e does) is what breaks the redis_deep starvation
2455                    // deadlock: a busy sibling can no longer starve the reaper.
2456                    self.blocked.sigchld_ready.insert(parent);
2457                    if self.blocked.sigchld_deferred.remove(&parent) {
2458                        self.run_queue.push_eager_io_repoll(parent);
2459                    } else {
2460                        self.fire_alarm(parent, dtid, sig);
2461                    }
2462                }
2463                TimedEvent::SignalEvt(id, dtid, sig) => self.fire_alarm(id.process(), dtid, sig),
2464            }
2465        }
2466    }
2467
2468    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2469    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
2470    fn fire_alarm(&mut self, dpid: DetPid, dtid: DetTid, sig: Signal) {
2471        let Some(target) = self.select_signal_target(dpid, Some(dtid)) else {
2472            info!(
2473                "[dpid {}] Alarm expired after its target exited; ignoring.",
2474                dpid
2475            );
2476            return;
2477        };
2478        info!(
2479            "[dtid {}] Alarm fired, delivering signal {} to guest.",
2480            target, sig
2481        );
2482        self.signal_guest(target, sig);
2483    }
2484
2485    // Follow Linux semantics for delivering a signal to a thread within a process group.
2486    // Optionally take a hint on which tid detcore would *like* to deliver to, if it is available.
2487    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2488    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
2489    fn select_signal_target(&mut self, detpid: DetPid, m_dettid: Option<DetTid>) -> Option<DetTid> {
2490        if !self.thread_tree.thread_group_leaders.contains(&detpid) {
2491            return None;
2492        }
2493
2494        // Targeted chaos (T137242449): a process-directed signal may legally be
2495        // handled by any thread in the group that does not block it. Instead of
2496        // always steering it to the hinted/leader thread, pick a random eligible
2497        // thread to surface signal-timing races. This stays reproducible under a
2498        // fixed `--fuzz-seed`.
2499        if self.chaos_target_races {
2500            let group = self.thread_tree.my_thread_group(&detpid);
2501            let eligible: Vec<DetTid> = group
2502                .into_iter()
2503                .filter(|t| !matches!(self.thread_status(*t), ThreadStatus::Gone))
2504                .collect();
2505            if let Some(chosen) = chaos_pick(&mut self.fuzz_prng, &eligible) {
2506                info!(
2507                    "[targeted-chaos] delivering process-directed signal to random group thread {} (of {:?})",
2508                    chosen, eligible
2509                );
2510                return Some(chosen);
2511            }
2512        }
2513
2514        if let Some(dettid) = m_dettid {
2515            match self.thread_status(dettid) {
2516                ThreadStatus::Gone => {}
2517                ThreadStatus::Running | ThreadStatus::NotRunning => {
2518                    return Some(dettid);
2519                }
2520            }
2521        }
2522        match self.thread_status(detpid) {
2523            ThreadStatus::Gone => self.process_signal_targets(detpid).into_iter().next(),
2524            ThreadStatus::Running | ThreadStatus::NotRunning => Some(detpid),
2525        }
2526    }
2527
2528    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2529    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#663)
2530    /// Return the scheduler's live threads for a positive process ID.
2531    /// Is `dettid` a live thread, leader or not?
2532    ///
2533    /// `process_signal_targets` answers a DIFFERENT question and must not be
2534    /// reused for this one: it models `kill(2)`, so it returns nothing unless
2535    /// the id names a thread-group LEADER. `sched_setattr(2)` and its relatives
2536    /// resolve through `find_task_by_vpid`, which finds any live task, so a
2537    /// non-leader thread's tid is a perfectly good target there. Answering that
2538    /// question with the kill resolver reports ESRCH for a thread that exists.
2539    ///
2540    /// Liveness is `next_turns`, the same membership `process_signal_targets`
2541    /// filters its own result by, so the two agree about what "live" means.
2542    pub fn thread_is_live(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> bool {
2543        self.next_turns.contains_key(&dettid)
2544    }
2545
2546    /// Return scheduler-owned lifecycle state for an exact child-process wait.
2547    ///
2548    /// This deliberately models direct parentage and process liveness rather
2549    /// than `kill(2)` target resolution. It lets wait syscalls stop exposing
2550    /// the backend-dependent interval between logical exit and host waitability.
2551    pub fn exact_child_wait_state(&mut self, parent: DetPid, child: DetPid) -> ExactChildWaitState {
2552        if self.thread_tree.parent_process(&child) != Some(parent) {
2553            return ExactChildWaitState::Unknown;
2554        }
2555
2556        let live = self
2557            .thread_tree
2558            .my_thread_group(&child)
2559            .into_iter()
2560            .any(|tid| self.next_turns.contains_key(&tid));
2561        if live {
2562            return ExactChildWaitState::Running;
2563        }
2564
2565        if !self.logically_exited_processes.contains(&child) {
2566            return ExactChildWaitState::Unknown;
2567        }
2568
2569        if !self.backend_reports_physical_process_exits {
2570            ExactChildWaitState::LogicallyExited
2571        } else if self.pending_physical_process_exits.contains(&child) {
2572            ExactChildWaitState::PhysicalExitPending
2573        } else {
2574            ExactChildWaitState::PhysicallyExited
2575        }
2576    }
2577
2578    fn child_matches_wait(&self, parent: DetPid, child: DetPid, spec: ChildWaitSpec) -> bool {
2579        let Some(metadata) = self.thread_tree.process_wait.get(&child) else {
2580            return false;
2581        };
2582        metadata.wait_parent == Some(parent)
2583            && spec.owner.is_none_or(|owner| metadata.wait_owner == owner)
2584            && match spec.exit_class {
2585                ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld => metadata.exit_signal == libc::SIGCHLD,
2586                ChildWaitExitClass::Clone => metadata.exit_signal != libc::SIGCHLD,
2587                ChildWaitExitClass::Any => true,
2588            }
2589            && match spec.selector {
2590                ChildWaitSelector::Exact(expected) => child == expected,
2591                ChildWaitSelector::Any => true,
2592                ChildWaitSelector::ProcessGroup(group) => metadata.process_group == group,
2593            }
2594    }
2595
2596    pub fn ready_child_wait(&self, parent: DetPid, spec: ChildWaitSpec) -> Option<DetPid> {
2597        self.logically_exited_processes
2598            .iter()
2599            .copied()
2600            .find(|child| self.child_matches_wait(parent, *child, spec))
2601    }
2602
2603    pub fn has_child_wait_target(&self, parent: DetPid, spec: ChildWaitSpec) -> bool {
2604        self.thread_tree
2605            .process_wait
2606            .keys()
2607            .copied()
2608            .any(|child| self.child_matches_wait(parent, child, spec))
2609    }
2610
2611    pub fn consume_child_wait(&mut self, parent: DetPid, child: DetPid) -> bool {
2612        if self.thread_tree.parent_process(&child) != Some(parent) {
2613            return false;
2614        }
2615        self.wake_child_waiters(parent, child);
2616        self.completed_physical_process_exits.remove(&child);
2617        self.thread_tree.process_parent.remove(&child);
2618        self.thread_tree.process_wait.remove(&child);
2619        self.logically_exited_processes.remove(&child)
2620    }
2621
2622    fn wake_child_waiters(&mut self, parent: DetPid, child: DetPid) {
2623        let waiters: Vec<DetTid> = self
2624            .blocked
2625            .child_waiters
2626            .iter()
2627            .filter_map(|(dettid, (wait_parent, spec))| {
2628                (*wait_parent == parent && self.child_matches_wait(parent, child, *spec))
2629                    .then_some(*dettid)
2630            })
2631            .collect();
2632        for dettid in waiters {
2633            self.blocked.child_waiters.remove(&dettid);
2634            debug_assert!(!self.run_queue.contains_tid(dettid));
2635            self.admit_to_run_queue(dettid, AdmitIntent::Fixed(AdmitSide::Back));
2636        }
2637    }
2638
2639    fn wake_physical_child_waiters(&mut self, child: DetPid) {
2640        if let Some(waiters) = self.blocked.physical_child_waiters.remove(&child) {
2641            for dettid in waiters {
2642                self.blocked.physical_child_ready.insert(dettid);
2643                if self.next_turns.contains_key(&dettid) && !self.run_queue.contains_tid(dettid) {
2644                    self.admit_to_run_queue(dettid, AdmitIntent::Fixed(AdmitSide::Back));
2645                }
2646            }
2647        }
2648    }
2649
2650    pub fn process_signal_targets(&mut self, detpid: DetPid) -> Vec<DetTid> {
2651        if !self.thread_tree.thread_group_leaders.contains(&detpid) {
2652            return Vec::new();
2653        }
2654        let mut targets = self.thread_tree.my_thread_group(&detpid);
2655        targets.retain(|tid| self.next_turns.contains_key(tid));
2656        targets.sort();
2657        targets
2658    }
2659
2660    fn wake_timed_event(&mut self, time_ns: LogicalTime, dettid: DetTid) {
2661        let futex_timed_out = {
2662            let next_turn = self
2663                .next_turns
2664                .get(&dettid)
2665                .expect("internal invariant broken");
2666            is_futex_request(next_turn)
2667        };
2668        if futex_timed_out {
2669            assert!(self.remove_futex_waiter(&dettid));
2670            assert!(self.blocked.timed_out_futex_waiters.insert(dettid));
2671        }
2672
2673        if enabled!(Level::TRACE) {
2674            if futex_timed_out {
2675                info!(
2676                    "[sched-step2] Time-based event on thread {} (time {}, committed time {}) - futex wait timed out!",
2677                    dettid, time_ns, self.committed_time
2678                );
2679            } else {
2680                info!(
2681                    "[sched-step2] Time-based event on thread {} (time {}) jumping back to the head of it's priority at global(committed) time {}",
2682                    dettid, time_ns, self.committed_time
2683                );
2684            }
2685        }
2686        self.runqueue_push_front(dettid);
2687    }
2688
2689    /// Send a signal to the guest. A scheduler-parked thread is made runnable immediately. SaBRe
2690    /// external syscalls remain blocked until the signal interrupts them and their real
2691    /// continuation RPC becomes visible; other backends retain their existing immediate requeue.
2692    fn signal_guest(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, signal: Signal) {
2693        debug!(
2694            "[dtid {}] deliver signal {} physically to guest thread.",
2695            dettid, signal
2696        );
2697        let result = if let Some((_, _, _, pidfd)) = self.physical_thread_pidfds.get(&dettid) {
2698            let rc = unsafe {
2699                libc::syscall(
2700                    libc::SYS_pidfd_send_signal,
2701                    pidfd.as_raw_fd(),
2702                    signal as libc::c_int,
2703                    std::ptr::null::<libc::siginfo_t>(),
2704                    0,
2705                )
2706            };
2707            if rc < 0 {
2708                Err(nix::errno::Errno::last())
2709            } else {
2710                Ok(())
2711            }
2712        } else if self.backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals {
2713            self.terminal_deadlock.get_or_insert_with(|| {
2714                format!(
2715                    "HERMIT_DEADLOCK: scheduler cannot deliver signal {} to dettid {} without its host thread pidfd",
2716                    signal, dettid,
2717                )
2718            });
2719            return;
2720        } else {
2721            let pid = Pid::from_raw(dettid.as_raw()); // TODO(T78538674): virtualize pid/tid:
2722            signal::kill(pid, signal)
2723        };
2724        match result {
2725            Ok(()) => {}
2726            // ⚠️ ESRCH IS AN EXPECTED OUTCOME HERE, NOT AN ERROR. The target
2727            // chose to exit between the moment it was selected and the moment
2728            // the signal was sent; that window is inherent and cannot be closed
2729            // by locking, because the thread's exit is the guest's decision.
2730            // There is no one left to signal and nothing left to wake, so the
2731            // correct behaviour is to say so and carry on.
2732            //
2733            // This is NOT a retry and NOT a widened catch: exactly ESRCH is
2734            // absorbed, and every other errno still panics, because a signal
2735            // rejected for any other reason means the scheduler's model of the
2736            // guest is wrong and continuing would compound it.
2737            //
2738            // WHY IT MATTERED. Before this, the unconditional `expect` turned
2739            // that ordinary race into a panic on the scheduler task -- and a
2740            // panicking scheduler does not fail the run, it HANGS it, because
2741            // every guest thread is waiting on a task that no longer exists.
2742            // Measured 2026-08-25: `hermit run --backend kvm --strict --verify`
2743            // on a bash process-substitution pipeline ran 420 s with empty
2744            // stdout and never exited. The pipeline's short-lived subshells hit
2745            // this window repeatedly. Found only once the 22 `run_kvm_` cli
2746            // tests were scheduled; nothing had ever run them.
2747            Err(nix::errno::Errno::ESRCH) => {
2748                info!(
2749                    "[dtid {}] signal {} not delivered: the thread exited before it landed. \
2750                     Expected race; nothing to deliver and nothing to wake.",
2751                    dettid, signal
2752                );
2753                return;
2754            }
2755            Err(errno) => panic!("signal::kill to go through, got {errno}"),
2756        }
2757        self.wake_signaled_guest(dettid, signal);
2758    }
2759
2760    /// Wake a scheduler-parked target after another guest has successfully
2761    /// queued a physical signal for it. The signal itself is not sent here;
2762    /// this only prevents an internal polling request from hiding a pending
2763    /// signal indefinitely.
2764    fn wake_signaled_guest(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, signal: Signal) {
2765        debug!(
2766            "[dtid {}] make pending signal {} visible to the scheduler.",
2767            dettid, signal
2768        );
2769        // `rt_sigsuspend_blockers` joins `external_io_blockers` here per main's
2770        // rt_sigsuspend work; both mean the thread is parked outside the
2771        // scheduler and must await its own continuation.
2772        let has_external_blocker = self.blocked.external_io_blockers.contains_key(&dettid)
2773            || self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.contains_key(&dettid);
2774        let await_external_continuation =
2775            self.backend_reports_physical_process_exits && has_external_blocker;
2776        if cfg!(debug_assertions) && !await_external_continuation {
2777            let nxtturn = self
2778                .next_turns
2779                .get(&dettid)
2780                .expect("internal invariant broken");
2781            assert!(
2782                nxtturn.req.try_read().is_some(),
2783                "signal_guest: thread should be parked in the scheduler"
2784            );
2785        }
2786        if await_external_continuation {
2787            return;
2788        }
2789
2790        // Now that the thread is signaled, it needs to be runnable for the scheduler to continue it.
2791        match self.thread_status(dettid) {
2792            ThreadStatus::Gone => {
2793                panic!(
2794                    "signal_guest: should not have just delivered a signal to a nonexistent thread..."
2795                );
2796            }
2797            ThreadStatus::Running => {
2798                let is_internal_io_polling = self
2799                    .next_turns
2800                    .get(&dettid)
2801                    .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
2802                    .is_some_and(|request| {
2803                        request.is_ok_and(|resources| {
2804                            resources
2805                                .resources
2806                                .contains_key(&ResourceID::InternalIOPolling)
2807                        })
2808                    });
2809
2810                if is_internal_io_polling {
2811                    assert!(self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid));
2812                    let mut rsrcs = Resources::new(dettid);
2813                    rsrcs.insert(
2814                        ResourceID::InboundSignal(SigWrapper::from(signal)),
2815                        Permission::W,
2816                    );
2817                    self.force_unblock_thread(dettid, rsrcs);
2818                }
2819                // TODO(T137242449): other runnable requests could be reprioritized to run
2820                // sooner, but for now we leave their priorities alone.
2821            }
2822            ThreadStatus::NotRunning => {
2823                let mut rsrcs = Resources::new(dettid);
2824                rsrcs.insert(
2825                    ResourceID::InboundSignal(SigWrapper::from(signal)),
2826                    Permission::W,
2827                );
2828                self.force_unblock_thread(dettid, rsrcs);
2829            }
2830        }
2831    }
2832
2833    /// Classify a request that still represents the same in-flight waitid.
2834    /// A signal may arrive before its polling request is rewritten or after a prior
2835    /// notification has already materialized its scheduler wakeup.
2836    fn waitid_signal_request(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> Option<WaitidSignalRequest> {
2837        let resources = self
2838            .next_turns
2839            .get(&dettid)
2840            .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
2841            .and_then(Result::ok)?;
2842        // The two ways to be parked inside wait4/waitid: the legacy kernel
2843        // polling loop, and the scheduler-managed child-wait lifecycle resource
2844        // added by the typed WaitChild work. Both must be woken; neither implies
2845        // anything about run-queue residency, which the drain decides.
2846        let waiting_on_child = resources
2847            .resources
2848            .keys()
2849            .any(|resource| matches!(resource, ResourceID::WaitChild { .. }));
2850        let legacy_polling = resources.fyi == "waitid"
2851            && resources
2852                .resources
2853                .contains_key(&ResourceID::InternalIOPolling);
2854        if legacy_polling || waiting_on_child {
2855            return Some(WaitidSignalRequest::Parked);
2856        }
2857        // Signals already materialized on this waitid request by an earlier
2858        // drain. A marked `WaitidSignals` resource instead belongs to an
2859        // interrupted InternalIOPolling request and is classified below.
2860        if resources.signal_interrupt_errno().is_some() {
2861            return None;
2862        }
2863        resources
2864            .resources
2865            .keys()
2866            .find_map(|resource| match resource {
2867                ResourceID::WaitidSignals(signals) => {
2868                    Some(WaitidSignalRequest::Pending(signals.clone()))
2869                }
2870                _ => None,
2871            })
2872    }
2873
2874    fn restartable_internal_io_signals(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> Option<Vec<SigWrapper>> {
2875        if !self.backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption {
2876            return None;
2877        }
2878        self.next_turns
2879            .get(&dettid)
2880            .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
2881            .and_then(Result::ok)
2882            .and_then(|resources| {
2883                if resources.resources.len() != 1
2884                    || resources.signal_interrupt_errno() != Some(Errno::ERESTARTSYS.into_raw())
2885                {
2886                    return None;
2887                }
2888                match resources.resources.keys().next() {
2889                    Some(ResourceID::InternalIOPolling) => Some(Vec::new()),
2890                    Some(ResourceID::WaitidSignals(signals)) => Some(signals.clone()),
2891                    _ => None,
2892                }
2893            })
2894    }
2895
2896    /// Record an unambiguous cross-task signal that was physically queued while
2897    /// its target was parked in waitid or restartable internal IO polling. The
2898    /// request rewrite is deferred to step2 so an asynchronous backend cannot
2899    /// mutate beneath a tentative selection.
2900    pub(crate) fn notify_signal_pending(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, signal: SigWrapper) {
2901        if self.waitid_signal_request(dettid).is_some()
2902            || self.restartable_internal_io_signals(dettid).is_some()
2903        {
2904            let signals = self.pending_cross_task_signals.entry(dettid).or_default();
2905            if !signals.contains(&signal) {
2906                signals.push(signal);
2907            }
2908        }
2909    }
2910
2911    // Force a thread out blocking and into the runnable state, replacing its resource request.
2912    fn force_unblock_thread(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, rsrcs: Resources) {
2913        info!(
2914            "[dtid {}] removing blocking entries and requeuing thread",
2915            dettid
2916        );
2917        self.remove_blocking_entries(&dettid);
2918
2919        if let Some(nxt) = self.next_turns.get_mut(&dettid) {
2920            // Counterfeit the entry as though the thread had requested this resource from the start:
2921            nxt.req = Ivar::full(Ok(rsrcs));
2922        }
2923
2924        // Targeted chaos (T137242449): a force-unblocked thread (e.g. woken by a
2925        // signal or ready I/O) is normally requeued at the back of its priority
2926        // level, so it runs after everything already queued. Randomizing whether
2927        // it jumps to the front instead varies the order in which a just-woken
2928        // thread races the threads it was contending with -- surfacing
2929        // lock-ordering / wakeup-ordering races. Reproducible under `--fuzz-seed`.
2930        let to_front = self.chaos_target_races
2931            && chaos_pick(&mut self.fuzz_prng, &[true, false]).unwrap_or(false);
2932        if to_front {
2933            self.runqueue_push_front(dettid);
2934        } else {
2935            self.runqueue_push_back(dettid);
2936        }
2937    }
2938
2939    /// Check on threads executing blocking syscalls outside the runnable set.
2940    fn step2c_process_io_blockers(&mut self) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
2941        if !self.blocked.external_io_blockers.is_empty()
2942            || !self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty()
2943        {
2944            // A nondeterministic snapshot of which backgrounded actions are ready right now:
2945            let mut blockers: Vec<(DetTid, ExternalOpId, bool)> = self
2946                .blocked
2947                .external_io_blockers
2948                .iter()
2949                .map(|(dtid, op_id)| (*dtid, *op_id, !self.vfork_barriers.contains_key(dtid)))
2950                .chain(
2951                    self.blocked
2952                        .rt_sigsuspend_blockers
2953                        .iter()
2954                        .map(|(dtid, op_id)| (*dtid, *op_id, true)),
2955                )
2956                .collect();
2957            blockers.sort_by_key(|(dtid, _, _)| *dtid);
2958            let ready: Vec<DetTid> = blockers
2959                .iter()
2960                .filter(|(dtid, op_id, signal_can_complete)| {
2961                    let nt = self
2962                        .next_turns
2963                        .get(dtid)
2964                        .expect("internal invariant broken");
2965                    if let Some(Ok(req)) = nt.req.try_read() {
2966                        blocking_request_is_ready(&req, *op_id, *signal_can_complete)
2967                    } else {
2968                        false
2969                    }
2970                })
2971                .map(|(dtid, _, _)| *dtid)
2972                .collect();
2973            debug!(
2974                "Nondeterministic status of backgrounded operations: out of {}, completed on {}, dtids: {:?}",
2975                blockers.len(),
2976                ready.len(),
2977                ready
2978            );
2979
2980            let requeue_ready = |scheduler: &mut Self, ready: &[DetTid]| {
2981                for ready_dtid in ready {
2982                    info!(
2983                        "[step2] Reschedule formerly backgrounded dtid {:?}",
2984                        ready_dtid
2985                    );
2986                    let external = scheduler.blocked.external_io_blockers.remove(ready_dtid);
2987                    let sigsuspend = scheduler.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.remove(ready_dtid);
2988                    assert!(
2989                        external.is_some() ^ sigsuspend.is_some(),
2990                        "ready thread must belong to exactly one blocking pool"
2991                    );
2992                    scheduler.run_queue.push_eager_io_repoll(*ready_dtid);
2993                }
2994            };
2995
2996            // FIXME TODO (T137183027): for record/replay to work properly, we need to ALLOW the
2997            // "Nondeterminstic algorithm" below, but record & replay those scheduler events. In
2998            // the meantime, use a deterministic eager policy once there is no other runnable work.
2999            if self.recordreplay_modes {
3000                // Only *real* deterministic work should defer external-IO harvesting.
3001                // Internal pollers sit at LAST_PRIORITY and are frequently spinning on
3002                // the very result an external-IO blocker will produce (e.g. the reader
3003                // side of `echo hi | cat`, where one stage uses InternalIOPolling while
3004                // its peer is backgrounded on BlockingExternalIO). Treat a run queue
3005                // that holds only pollers as "no deterministic work" so we still harvest
3006                // completed IO below; a queued poller must not starve a ready blocker.
3007                let only_pollers = if let Some(fp) = self.run_queue.first_priority() {
3008                    fp >= LAST_PRIORITY
3009                } else {
3010                    true
3011                };
3012
3013                // Deterministic work is runnable: let it proceed. Waiting here while such
3014                // work exists can deadlock thread creation -- the parent and new child
3015                // cannot complete clone while an existing worker blocks indefinitely in
3016                // epoll_wait.
3017                if !self.run_queue.is_empty() && !only_pollers {
3018                    return Ok(());
3019                }
3020
