3021                // Reschedule every blocker whose IO has completed so its continuation can
3022                // consume the recorded result. Harvesting all ready blockers (not just the
3023                // first) and doing so even when pollers are queued is what breaks the
3024                // record-mode pipe livelock: previously a queued poller made this branch
3025                // return early forever, so the completed read/write was never rescheduled
3026                // and the poller spun on data that never arrived.
3027                if !ready.is_empty() {
3028                    requeue_ready(self, &ready);
3029                    return Ok(());
3030                }
3031
3032                // `rt_sigsuspend` has no spontaneous completion: with no real
3033                // external IO left, continue to step2d so a finite timer can
3034                // fire or the signal-less wait can receive a terminal verdict.
3035                if self.blocked.external_io_blockers.is_empty() {
3036                    return Ok(());
3037                }
3038
3039                // No completed IO yet, and nothing but pollers (or nothing) to run. The
3040                // blocking syscalls are executing in the host kernel; go around the loop
3041                // and re-check readiness. (Still a busy-wait; see T137183027 for the
3042                // record-the-nondeterministic-event fix.)
3043                trace!(
3044                    "[step2] eagerly waiting on external IO for dtids {:?}. spinning.",
3045                    &self.blocked.external_io_blockers
3046                );
3047                std::thread::yield_now();
3048                return Err(SkipTurn);
3049            } // End region which should be deleted.
3050
3051            // Use the same deterministic-work-first policy as record/replay. Host
3052            // completion timing must not decide whether a ready continuation overtakes
3053            // guest work that was already runnable. Pollers are excluded because they
3054            // commonly wait for the completed operation and would otherwise starve it.
3055            let only_pollers = if let Some(fp) = self.run_queue.first_priority() {
3056                fp >= LAST_PRIORITY
3057            } else {
3058                true
3059            };
3060            if !self.run_queue.is_empty() && !only_pollers {
3061                return Ok(());
3062            }
3063
3064            if !ready.is_empty() {
3065                requeue_ready(self, &ready);
3066            }
3067            if self.run_queue.is_empty()
3068                && self.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty()
3069                && !self.blocked.external_io_blockers.is_empty()
3070            {
3071                // TODO (T137184765): for now we just WAIT eagerly whenever there is blocking
3072                // external IO and else to do. We implement a busy-wait by going around the
3073                // scheduler loop again.
3074                trace!(
3075                    "[step2] TEMPORARY2: eagerly blocking on external IO for dtids {:?}.  SPINNING!",
3076                    &self.blocked.external_io_blockers
3077                );
3078                std::thread::yield_now();
3079                Err(SkipTurn)
3080            } else {
3081                // Productive work to do, irrespcetive of what's blocked, so let's get to it.
3082                Ok(())
3083            }
3084        } else {
3085            Ok(())
3086        }
3087    }
3088
3089    /// Render one blocked thread's pending request, with a stable resource order.
3090    ///
3091    /// `Resources::resources` is a `HashMap`, so its iteration order is not
3092    /// reproducible; sort the rendered entries. Reads the `Ivar`'s contents
3093    /// rather than `Debug`-printing the `Ivar`, whose parked `Waker` carries raw
3094    /// host pointers.
3095    fn render_pending_request(req: &Ivar<SchedRequest>) -> String {
3096        match req.try_read() {
3097            None => "<no request pending>".to_owned(),
3098            Some(Err(ThreadExited)) => "<thread exited>".to_owned(),
3099            Some(Ok(rsrc)) => {
3100                let mut held: Vec<String> = rsrc
3101                    .resources
3102                    .iter()
3103                    .map(|(rid, perm)| match rid {
3104                        // Spell the sentinel out. Its derived `Debug` is
3105                        // `SleepUntil(LogicalTime(18446744073709551615))`, and a
3106                        // reader who has to decode that number by hand is
3107                        // exactly the reader this report failed.
3108                        ResourceID::SleepUntil(deadline) if deadline.is_indefinite() => {
3109                            format!("SleepUntil(INDEFINITE): {:?}", perm)
3110                        }
3111                        _ => format!("{:?}: {:?}", rid, perm),
3112                    })
3113                    .collect();
3114                held.sort();
3115                let mut rendered = if held.is_empty() {
3116                    "<no resources>".to_owned()
3117                } else {
3118                    held.join(", ")
3119                };
3120                if rsrc.poll_attempt != 0 {
3121                    let _ = write!(rendered, " (poll_attempt {})", rsrc.poll_attempt);
3122                }
3123                if !rsrc.fyi.is_empty() {
3124                    let _ = write!(rendered, " (fyi {:?})", rsrc.fyi);
3125                }
3126                rendered
3127            }
3128        }
3129    }
3130
3131    /// Render a futex key with its guest address in hex.
3132    ///
3133    /// `FutexID`'s derived `Debug` prints the address in decimal, which is
3134    /// deterministic but unusable: a reader has to match it against a guest
3135    /// symbol or a `futex(...)` line in the DETLOG, and both are hex.
3136    fn render_futex_id(futex_id: &FutexID) -> String {
3137        match futex_id {
3138            FutexID::Private { mm, address } => {
3139                format!("private {:?} address {:#x}", mm, address)
3140            }
3141            FutexID::Shared { object, offset } => {
3142                format!("shared {:?} offset {:#x}", object, offset)
3143            }
3144        }
3145    }
3146
3147    /// Render a `LogicalTime` deadline, naming the no-deadline sentinel.
3148    fn render_deadline(deadline: LogicalTime) -> String {
3149        if deadline.is_indefinite() {
3150            "INDEFINITE (no deadline)".to_owned()
3151        } else {
3152            deadline.to_string()
3153        }
3154    }
3155
3156    /// Name every blocked class that is actually holding the run.
3157    ///
3158    /// Derived from state rather than passed in by the caller, so the headline
3159    /// cannot contradict the body it sits above. Branch order alone would
3160    /// mis-file a genuine futex deadlock that merely has a saturated timer
3161    /// registered: `step2d_handle_empty_queue` reaches the indefinite arm
3162    /// whenever *any* timed waiter exists, so a caller-supplied headline would
3163    /// read "waiting indefinitely" while the futex pool is the real story. The
3164    /// body always listed both; the headline is what lands in a bug report.
3165    fn describe_terminal_waiters(&self) -> String {
3166        let mut classes: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
3167        if !self.blocked.no_futex_waiters() {
3168            classes.push("thread(s) waiting on futex");
3169        }
3170        if !self.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty() {
3171            classes.push(
3172                "thread(s) waiting indefinitely (pause, or a timer beyond the end of logical time)",
3173            );
3174        }
3175        if !self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty() {
3176            classes.push("thread(s) waiting in rt_sigsuspend with no possible signal");
3177        }
3178        if classes.is_empty() {
3179            // Defensive: every caller fires with at least one class present.
3180            "thread(s) blocked with no possible wake".to_owned()
3181        } else {
3182            classes.join(", and ")
3183        }
3184    }
3185
3186    /// Build the deadlock report. **Must be byte-identical across runs of the
3187    /// same program**: this text reaches stderr, and emitting a
3188    /// host-dependent string from a deterministic execution engine is a defect
3189    /// in its own right, whether or not any comparator currently catches it.
3190    ///
3191    /// The obvious implementation -- `{:?}` of `run_queue`, `next_turns` and
3192    /// `blocked` -- is *not* reproducible. Three independent sources of
3193    /// run-to-run variation hide in those structures:
3194    ///
3195    /// 1. `ThreadNextTurn` and `FutexWaiter` hold [`Ivar`]s whose `Debug` prints
3196    ///    the parked waker as `Waker { data: 0x55.., vtable: 0x55.. }` -- raw
3197    ///    host pointers that move with ASLR on every run.
3198    /// 2. `BlockedPool::futex_waiters` is a `HashMap` and
3199    ///    `timed_out_futex_waiters` a `HashSet`, so both iterate in a
3200    ///    randomized order whenever they hold more than one entry.
3201    /// 3. `Resources::resources` is itself a `HashMap`, so even a single
3202    ///    thread's resource list is unordered.
3203    ///
3204    /// So every collection below is sorted on a stable key, and only
3205    /// guest-level identities are printed: dettids, resource ids, futex keys and
3206    /// logical deadlines, all of which Detcore already guarantees to be
3207    /// deterministic. No host pointer is emitted.
3208    fn format_terminal_deadlock(&self) -> String {
3209        // ⚠️ ADDING A SECTION HERE? RENDER `dtid` OR A STABLE ORDINAL, NEVER A
3210        // DEBUG-FORMATTED ID. The rule is stated in full in the doc comment above
3211        // and repeated here because that is not where authors edit: the doc
3212        // comment already warned that the obvious `{:?}` implementation is not
3213        // reproducible, and a later change added a section that did it anyway.
3214        //
3215        // A host-influenced counter is the failure that survives review, because
3216        // it LOOKS like a small integer. `{:?}` of a wrapper type, a raw thread
3217        // id, an allocation counter or anything seeded from host state will differ
3218        // run to run while reading like an ordinary index.
3219        //
3220        // Print only identities Detcore already determinizes: dettids, resource
3221        // ids, futex keys, logical deadlines, or a position computed by sorting on
3222        // one of those.
3223        //
3224        // ⚠️ AND EXTEND THE FIXTURE. The determinism test cannot see a section its
3225        // fixture never renders, so a section that only appears under state
3226        // `deadlocked_scheduler` does not build is UNGUARDED however good the
3227        // banned list is. If your section needs different scheduler state, add
3228        // that state to the fixture in the same change.
3229        let mut out = format!(
3230            "Deadlock detected: {}, but no runnable threads left.\n",
3231            self.describe_terminal_waiters()
3232        );
3233        let _ = writeln!(
3234            out,
3235            "  turn {}, committed time {}",
3236            self.turn, self.committed_time
3237        );
3238        let _ = writeln!(out, "  run queue: {} runnable", self.run_queue.len());
3239
3240        let _ = writeln!(out, "  threads ({}), by dettid:", self.next_turns.len());
3241        // `next_turns` is a BTreeMap, so this walk is already ordered.
3242        for (dettid, next_turn) in self.next_turns.iter() {
3243            let _ = writeln!(
3244                out,
3245                "    dtid {}: {}",
3246                dettid,
3247                Self::render_pending_request(&next_turn.req)
3248            );
3249        }
3250
3251        let mut futexes: Vec<String> = self
3252            .blocked
3253            .futex_waiters
3254            .iter()
3255            .filter(|(_, waiters)| !waiters.is_empty())
3256            .map(|(futex_id, waiters)| {
3257                let mut parked: Vec<String> = waiters
3258                    .iter()
3259                    .map(|w| format!("dtid {} (bitset {:#010x})", w.dettid, w.bitset))
3260                    .collect();
3261                parked.sort();
3262                format!(
3263                    "    {}: {}",
3264                    Self::render_futex_id(futex_id),
3265                    parked.join(", ")
3266                )
3267            })
3268            .collect();
3269        futexes.sort();
3270        if futexes.is_empty() {
3271            let _ = writeln!(out, "  futex waiters: none");
3272        } else {
3273            let _ = writeln!(out, "  futex waiters ({}), by futex:", futexes.len());
3274            for line in futexes {
3275                let _ = writeln!(out, "{}", line);
3276            }
3277        }
3278
3279        let mut timed_out: Vec<DetTid> = self
3280            .blocked
3281            .timed_out_futex_waiters
3282            .iter()
3283            .copied()
3284            .collect();
3285        timed_out.sort();
3286        let _ = writeln!(
3287            out,
3288            "  timed-out futex waiters: {}",
3289            render_tid_list(&timed_out)
3290        );
3291
3292        let timed: Vec<(LogicalTime, TimedEvent)> = self.blocked.timed_waiters.iter().collect();
3293        if timed.is_empty() {
3294            let _ = writeln!(out, "  timed waiters: none");
3295        } else {
3296            // `TimedEvents` is backed by a BTreeMap keyed on the deadline, so
3297            // this is already in deadline order.
3298            let _ = writeln!(out, "  timed waiters ({}), by deadline:", timed.len());
3299            for (deadline, evt) in timed {
3300                let _ = writeln!(out, "    {}: {}", Self::render_deadline(deadline), evt);
3301            }
3302        }
3303
3304        if self.blocked.external_io_blockers.is_empty() {
3305            let _ = writeln!(out, "  external IO blockers: none");
3306        } else {
3307            // BTreeMap: already ordered by dettid.
3308            let _ = writeln!(
3309                out,
3310                "  external IO blockers ({}), by dettid:",
3311                self.blocked.external_io_blockers.len()
3312            );
3313            for (dettid, op) in self.blocked.external_io_blockers.iter() {
3314                let _ = writeln!(out, "    dtid {}: {:?}", dettid, op);
3315            }
3316        }
3317
3318        if self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty() {
3319            let _ = writeln!(out, "  rt_sigsuspend blockers: none");
3320        } else {
3321            let _ = writeln!(
3322                out,
3323                "  rt_sigsuspend blockers ({}), by dettid:",
3324                self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.len()
3325            );
3326            for (dettid, op) in self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.iter() {
3327                let _ = writeln!(out, "    dtid {}: {:?}", dettid, op);
3328            }
3329        }
3330
3331        // Both are BTreeSets, so already ordered.
3332        let deferred: Vec<DetTid> = self.blocked.sigchld_deferred.iter().copied().collect();
3333        let ready: Vec<DetTid> = self.blocked.sigchld_ready.iter().copied().collect();
3334        let _ = writeln!(
3335            out,
3336            "  sigchld deferred: {}; sigchld ready: {}",
3337            render_tid_list(&deferred),
3338            render_tid_list(&ready)
3339        );
3340        out
3341    }
3342
3343    /// Report a permanently blocked guest and abort the run.
3344    ///
3345    /// Reached only when the run queue is empty *and* every remaining thread is
3346    /// blocked on something no future scheduler turn can supply. In a
3347    /// sequentialized deterministic run the scheduler is the only source of
3348    /// wakeups, so "no thread can be woken now" is also "no thread can ever be
3349    /// woken": the condition is permanent, not transient. Linux would leave such
3350    /// a process hung forever; reporting it is strictly more useful than
3351    /// reproducing the hang, and it is the established Detcore behaviour for the
3352    /// futex case (`docs/ERROR_CATALOG.md`).
3353    ///
3354    /// `waiters` names *what* is blocked; everything else is shared, so every
3355    /// deadlock class prints the same reproducible shape.
3356    ///
3357    /// Records the report and returns [`SkipTurn`] rather than panicking.
3358    /// `sched_loop_inner` picks it up and exits the container, next to the two
3359    /// existing `--stop-after-*` fatal exits. A panic here would NOT end the
3360    /// run: the scheduler is a `tokio::spawn`ed task whose panic the harness
3361    /// captures, leaving every guest thread parked forever on an
3362    /// `Ivar<SchedResponse>` only the scheduler could fill, so the process hangs
3363    /// until an external timeout kills it. Unwinding out of
3364    /// `do_a_turn_blocking` would also poison the scheduler mutex.
3365    fn report_terminal_deadlock(&mut self) -> SkipTurn {
3366        let report = self.format_terminal_deadlock();
3367        // Keep the first verdict: it names the state that actually wedged.
3368        self.terminal_deadlock.get_or_insert(report);
3369        SkipTurn
3370    }
3371
3372    /// Take the pending terminal-deadlock report, if the scheduler produced one.
3373    fn take_terminal_deadlock(&mut self) -> Option<String> {
3374        self.terminal_deadlock.take()
3375    }
3376
3377    fn step2d_handle_empty_queue(
3378        &mut self,
3379        global_time: &Arc<Mutex<GlobalTime>>,
3380    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3381        let timed_empty = self.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty();
3382        let external_waits_empty = self.blocked.external_io_blockers.is_empty()
3383            && self.blocked.child_waiters.is_empty()
3384            && self.blocked.physical_child_waiters.is_empty();
3385        let rt_sigsuspend_empty = self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.is_empty();
3386        let futex_empty = self.blocked.no_futex_waiters();
3387
3388        if self.run_queue.is_empty() {
3389            if !self.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty() {
3390                // The SaBRe plugin has run the child process's logical exit hook, but the ptrace
3391                // supervisor has not received its final wait status. Fast-forwarding the next
3392                // timer here can fire a parent's timeout before the child becomes waitable.
3393                trace!(
3394                    "waiting for physical process exits before empty-queue timer fast-forward: {:?}",
3395                    self.pending_physical_process_exits
3396                );
3397                std::thread::yield_now();
3398                return Err(SkipTurn);
3399            }
3400            // When the run queue is empty, we sometimes need to give things a kick.
3401            if futex_empty && timed_empty && external_waits_empty && rt_sigsuspend_empty {
3402                // `info!`, and that level is load-bearing. Restored from `trace!`
3403                // by owner ruling after 08ff51a33e demoted it.
3404                //
3405                // THE DEMOTION'S ARGUMENT AND WHY IT WAS REJECTED. It ran: how
3406                // many times this branch is reached depends on when the host
3407                // delivers a child's final wait status, that is an external
3408                // real-time event, an INFO line driven by one is a determinism
3409                // hazard by construction, so keep the loop sub-INFO. The
3410                // measurement offered with it was real -- over 312 concurrent
3411                // SaBRe cell-runs, 4 diverged, and in each the whole difference
3412                // was this line plus one unrelated WARN.
3413                //
3414                // The owner's ruling: this fizzle is part of the deterministic
3415                // scheduling model, not record/replay external-IO terrain, so it
3416                // SHOULD be a deterministic function of detcore scheduling. If it
3417                // is not, that is a hermit DEFECT, and demoting the line hid the
3418                // defect instead of fixing it. The previous note ended "do not
3419                // promote this back without making the host's wait-status
3420                // delivery deterministic, which is not possible" -- but making it
3421                // deterministic is exactly the work owed, not a reason to stop
3422                // reporting it.
3423                //
3424                // AND THE DEFECT IS REAL AND LARGER THAN THIS LINE. Measured
3425                // 2026-08-24 on the CI-staged SaBRe artifact: a guest that forks
3426                // and reaps its child with `wait4(WNOHANG)` diverges 36 of 50
3427                // under `--verify-strict`, while ptrace is 0 of 50 on the same
3428                // guest. The divergence is not confined to logging -- the guest
3429                // ITSELF executes different work, 355 syscalls with 4 `wait4`
3430                // calls in one run against 354 with 3 in the other. The
3431                // scheduler's committed non-poll decisions are byte-identical
3432                // (68 in both), so what varies is how many times the parent is
3433                // told "not yet". Silencing this line would have left that
3434                // unreported.
3435                //
3436                // If this line is noisy again, the fix is to make a child's exit
3437                // become observable to its parent at a deterministic point, not
3438                // to lower the level.
3439                if enabled!(Level::INFO) {
3440                    let record_suffix = crate::detlog::record_suffix(
3441                        crate::detlog::DetLogEvent::SchedulerEmptyQueueKick,
3442                    );
3443                    info!(
3444                        "scheduler (step2_process_blocked): zero threads left anywhere, fizzling.{}",
3445                        record_suffix
3446                    );
3447                }
3448                return Err(SkipTurn);
3449            } else if timed_empty && external_waits_empty && (!futex_empty || !rt_sigsuspend_empty)
3450            {
3451                return Err(self.report_terminal_deadlock());
3452            } else if !timed_empty {
3453                // Only a *reachable* deadline justifies fast-forwarding the clock.
3454                // `LogicalTime::INDEFINITE` is the sentinel a `pause(2)` (or a
3455                // far-future timer that saturated the clock) registers to mean "no
3456                // deadline; wake me on a signal". Because the map is ordered, an
3457                // indefinite front entry means every pending timed event is
3458                // indefinite, so no amount of virtual time can fire any of them.
3459                // Popping it anyway would jump global virtual time by ~584 years and
3460                // grant the sleep as a `ResumeStatus::Normal` wake, which is exactly
3461                // the internal violation `Detcore::handle_pause` refuses to accept.
3462                let next_deadline = self
3463                    .blocked
3464                    .timed_waiters
3465                    .next_deadline()
3466                    .expect("internal error: no timed events found");
3467                if next_deadline.is_indefinite() {
3468                    if !external_waits_empty {
3469                        // Blocking external IO may still complete and produce the
3470                        // signal/wake the indefinite waiter needs, so this is not a
3471                        // deadlock yet. Spin as step2c does for the same reason.
3472                        trace!(
3473                            "[scheduler] empty run-queue with only indefinite waiters, but external IO is outstanding for dtids {:?}. SPINNING!",
3474                            &self.blocked.external_io_blockers
3475                        );
3476                        std::thread::yield_now();
3477                        return Err(SkipTurn);
3478                    }
3479                    return Err(self.report_terminal_deadlock());
3480                }
3481                debug!(
3482                    "[scheduler] Deadlock avoidance! Empty run-queue, so waking next timed event."
3483                );
3484                let (event_ns, evt) = self
3485                    .blocked
3486                    .timed_waiters
3487                    .pop()
3488                    .expect("internal error: no timed events found");
3489                debug_assert_eq!(event_ns, next_deadline);
3490                info!("[scheduler] Skipping global time ahead to {}.", event_ns);
3491                {
3492                    let mut gt = global_time.lock().unwrap();
3493                    let gt_now_ns = gt.as_nanos();
3494                    let delta = event_ns.duration_since(gt_now_ns);
3495                    detlog_debug!(
3496                        "[sched] add extra global time for deadlock avoidance {:?} on current time {}",
3497                        delta,
3498                        gt_now_ns,
3499                    );
3500                    gt.add_extra_time(delta);
3501                }
3502
3503                match evt {
3504                    TimedEvent::ThreadEvt(dtid) => self.wake_timed_event(event_ns, dtid),
3505                    TimedEvent::SignalEvt(id, dtid, sig) => {
3506                        self.fire_alarm(id.process(), dtid, sig)
3507                    }
3508                }
3509                return Err(SkipTurn);
3510            }
3511        }
3512        Ok(())
3513    }
3514
3515    /// Step: Find the next thread to run for this scheduling run.
3516    /// Sometimes the next thread is from the run queue, but it can also be a timed event.
3517    /// Return `None` if the queue is empty.
3518    ///
3519    /// This is a "peek" in the sense that it leaves the thread in the run queue.
3520    fn step3_peek(&mut self) -> Option<(DetTid, Ivar<SchedRequest>, Ivar<SchedResponse>)> {
3521        // Re-admit any happens-before threads whose gate opened since last turn.
3522        // Must precede `tentative_pop_next`: the run queue forbids pushes while a
3523        // selection transaction is live.
3524        self.hb_flush_wakes();
3525        debug!(
3526            "[sched-step3] Stepping scheduler, queue len {}, current turn {}, committed_time {}",
3527            self.run_queue.len(),
3528            self.turn,
3529            self.committed_time
3530        );
3531
3532        // Enable for FULL detail:
3533        {
3534            trace!(
3535                "[sched-step3] queue {:?}, io-blocked {:?}, next_turns: ",
3536                &self.run_queue, self.blocked.external_io_blockers
3537            );
3538            for (dtid, nxt) in self.next_turns.iter() {
3539                trace!(" ==> dtid {}, req {}, resp {}", dtid, nxt.req, nxt.resp);
3540            }
3541            if !self.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty() {
3542                trace!("Timed events: {:?}", self.blocked.timed_waiters);
3543            }
3544        }
3545
3546        if self.run_queue.is_empty() {
3547            None
3548        } else {
3549            let next_dtid = if self.vfork_barriers.is_empty() {
3550                self.run_queue.tentative_pop_next().expect("impossible")
3551            } else {
3552                let child = self
3553                    .vfork_barriers
3554                    .values()
3555                    .flatten()
3556                    .find(|child| self.run_queue.contains_tid(**child))
3557                    .copied()?;
3558                self.run_queue
3559                    .tentative_pop_tid(child)
3560                    .expect("vfork child disappeared from run queue")
3561            };
3562            let nextturn = self.next_turns.get(&next_dtid).unwrap_or_else(|| {
3563                panic!(
3564                "[sched-step3] internal error: dettid {} queued but missing entry in next_turns",
3565                    next_dtid
3566            )
3567            });
3568            Some((next_dtid, nextturn.req.clone(), nextturn.resp.clone()))
3569        }
3570    }
3571
3572    /// Deschedule, but do not clear request/response. This should be used when
3573    /// the turn was skipped because the blocked-on resource is still blocking.
3574    fn skip_turn_blocked(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3575        self.run_queue.undo_tentative_pop(); // Started in step3.
3576        assert!(self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid)); // Deschedule while we wait.
3577        trace!(
3578            "[dtid {}] after removal, run queue: {:?}",
3579            dettid, &self.run_queue
3580        );
3581        self.skip_turn()
3582    }
3583
3584    /// Post-await re-check: the selected thread parked (its request resolved
3585    /// `Ok`), but did its `next_turns` entry survive until the daemon got the
3586    /// lock back? If not, the turn must be abandoned.
3587    ///
3588    /// Returns `Err(SkipTurn)` for the abandoned case, after closing the
3589    /// tentative window `step3_peek` opened. Two things hang on that `Err`:
3590    ///
3591    /// * The tentative pop is undone rather than committed, so the selection
3592    ///   does not outlive the turn and the next pass's step2 removal drain does
3593    ///   not call `remove_tid` against a live `tentative_selection` (the same
3594    ///   hygiene the `Err(ThreadExited)` arm needs).
3595    /// * The caller reports a SKIP, not a completed turn. This branch bypasses
3596    ///   steps 4-7, so nothing is blocked, unblocked or re-enqueued -- yet
3597    ///   `bump_global_time` suppresses its advance only on `last_turn.is_err()`
3598    ///   ("if the last turn was a skip, it shouldn't really have time-bumped").
3599    ///   Reporting `Ok` here therefore added a DETLOG-visible virtual-time tick
3600    ///   for work that never happened, and whether this branch is reached at all
3601    ///   depends on whether teardown cleared `next_turns` inside the host-timed
3602    ///   gap between the await resolving and the re-lock -- so the same logical
3603    ///   execution could gain that tick in one run and not the next.
3604    fn abort_turn_if_thread_vanished(&mut self, next_dtid: DetTid) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3605        if self.next_turns.contains_key(&next_dtid) {
3606            return Ok(());
3607        }
3608        info!(
3609            "[sched-daemon] thread {} exited, skipping over...",
3610            &next_dtid
3611        );
3612        self.run_queue.undo_tentative_pop();
3613        Err(SkipTurn)
3614    }
3615
3616    /// Simply advance the turn. This does NOT remove any threads from the
3617    /// runqueue; callers must maintain `run_queue`/`blocking` invariants.
3618    fn skip_turn(&mut self) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3619        self.turn += 1; // Skipping the turn advances the turn.
3620        Err(SkipTurn)
3621    }
3622
3623    /// Step: Determine if action will block based on current information.  E.g. will it block
3624    /// on a pipe read with no writer? If so, register it in the blocked_pool and issue a "skip".
3625    /// We can go ahead and take resource locks and physically issue the blocking effect if we
3626    /// like.  It's immaterial whether we do that now or later.
3627    ///
3628    /// Postcondition:
3629    ///  - If returning SkipTurn, this function ENDS the Scheduler turn, advancing to the
3630    ///    next (skipping subsequent steps within theturn).  Otherwise, it waits for a
3631    ///    later step end the turn.
3632    #[allow(clippy::unnecessary_wraps)]
3633    fn step4_resource_block(
3634        &mut self,
3635        dettid: DetTid,
3636        rs: &Resources,
3637        resp: &Ivar<SchedResponse>,
3638    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3639        if rs.poll_attempt > 0 {
3640            // The thread is polling and hasn't been "remade" as runnable yet.
3641            info!(
3642                "[scheduler] >>>>>>>\n\n NONCOMMIT turn {}, SKIP dettid {} polling resource {:?}",
3643                self.turn, dettid, rs
3644            );
3645            // Requeue the thread as a poller
3646            let popped = self.run_queue.commit_tentative_pop();
3647            assert_eq!(dettid, popped);
3648            self.run_queue
3649                .push_poller(dettid, self.get_priority(dettid), rs.poll_attempt);
3650            trace!(
3651                "[dtid {}] after deprioritizing polling request, run queue: {:?}",
3652                dettid, &self.run_queue
3653            );
3654            self.upgrade_polled_to_runnable(dettid, rs); // Indicate the thread gets to run next time
3655            self.skip_turn()
3656        } else {
3657            match rs.resources.len() {
3658                0 => Ok(()),
3659                1 => {
3660                    let (rid, perm) = rs.resources.iter().next().unwrap();
3661                    self.block_for_one_resource(
3662                        dettid,
3663                        rid,
3664                        perm,
3665                        rs.signal_interrupt_errno(),
3666                        resp,
3667                    )
3668                }
3669                _ => {
3670                    panic!(
3671                        "Requests for more than one resource at a time are not supported yet: {:?}",
3672                        rs
3673                    )
3674                }
3675            }
3676        }
3677    }
3678
3679    /// Replace the request Ivar for `dettid` with a copy with `poll_attempt = 0`,
3680    /// indicating the poll request is runnable on the next trip through the run queue.
3681    ///
3682    /// Precondition: The guest is stopped, so that no one is potentially using the request Ivar.
3683    /// The request Ivar should also be full with the passed resources
3684    fn upgrade_polled_to_runnable(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, rs: &Resources) {
3685        let mut retry_rs = rs.clone();
3686        retry_rs.poll_attempt = 0;
3687        let runnable_req = Ivar::full(Ok(retry_rs));
3688        let req = &mut self
3689            .next_turns
3690            .get_mut(&dettid)
3691            .expect("nextturn present")
3692            .req;
3693        debug_assert!(req.try_read().unwrap().is_ok()); // Ivar should be full
3694        trace!(
3695            "[dtid {}] Upgrading polled resource request in {} to runnable non-polled in {}",
3696            dettid, req, runnable_req
3697        );
3698        *req = runnable_req;
3699    }
3700
3701    /// Helper function. Same postcondition as step4_resource_block
3702    fn block_for_one_resource(
3703        &mut self,
3704        dettid: DetTid,
3705        rid: &ResourceID,
3706        _perm: &Permission,
3707        signal_interrupt_errno: Option<i32>,
3708        resp: &Ivar<SchedResponse>,
3709    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3710        match rid {
3711            ResourceID::SleepUntil(target_ns) => {
3712                if *target_ns <= self.committed_time {
3713                    trace!(
3714                        "[dtid {}] time-based action ready to execute, target time {} is before committed global time {}",
3715                        dettid, target_ns, self.committed_time
3716                    );
3717                    Ok(())
3718                } else {
3719                    trace!(
3720                        "[dtid {}] time-based action not ready yet, registering waiter at future time {}. Current time is {}",
3721                        dettid, target_ns, self.committed_time
3722                    );
3723                    info!(
3724                        "[scheduler] >>>>>>>\n\n NONCOMMIT turn {}, SKIP dettid {} which wanted resource {:?} (blocking)",
3725                        self.turn, dettid, rid
3726                    );
3727                    self.blocked.timed_waiters.insert(*target_ns, dettid);
3728                    self.skip_turn_blocked(dettid)
3729                }
3730            }
3731
3732            // Thread BEGINS a blocking syscall outside the runnable set.
3733            ResourceID::BlockingExternalIO(op_id)
3734            | ResourceID::BlockingVfork(op_id)
3735            | ResourceID::BlockingRtSigsuspend(op_id) => {
3736                if matches!(rid, ResourceID::BlockingVfork(_)) {
3737                    assert!(self.vfork_barriers.insert(dettid, None).is_none());
3738                }
3739                if enabled!(Level::INFO) {
3740                    let record_suffix = scheduler_commit_record_suffix(
3741                        self.turn,
3742                        self.committed_time.as_nanos(),
3743                        false,
3744                        false,
3745                    );
3746                    info!(
3747                        "[scheduler] >>>>>>>\n\n COMMIT turn {}, BACKGROUND dettid {} (maybe-blocking){}",
3748                        self.turn, dettid, record_suffix
3749                    );
3750                }
3751                // Here we allow the action to execute asynchrounously, in the
3752                // background. The protocol is that it must:
3753                //   (1) not interfere with other internal/external actions (independence),
3754                //   (2) Request a BlockedExternalContinue as the first thing after the external IO is complete.
3755                self.run_queue.undo_tentative_pop(); // Begun in step3
3756                assert!(self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid)); // Deschedule while in background.
3757
3758                // TODO: Register the action that is occuring in the background:
3759                // let act = self.new_action(Ivar::new());
3760                // self.bg_action_pool.insert(act.action_id, act);
3761
3762                // Unblock guest so that potentially-blocking IO action can get
3763                // started. This intentionally races with subsequent turns the
3764                // scheduler commits, and thus it leans on an assumption of
3765                // non-interference, or on interference *only* affecting the external
3766                // actions that will be recorded anyway.
3767                self.run_queue.consume_yield_exclusion();
3768                self.unblock_guest(dettid, resp);
3769
3770                // Only once the ivars are cleared and the guest is ready to issue
3771                // BlockedExternalContinue do we record which blocked pool owns it.
3772                let old = if matches!(rid, ResourceID::BlockingRtSigsuspend(_)) {
3773                    self.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.insert(dettid, *op_id)
3774                } else {
3775                    self.blocked.external_io_blockers.insert(dettid, *op_id)
3776                };
3777                assert!(old.is_none(), "thread started a second external operation");
3778                Err(SkipTurn)
3779            }
3780
3781            // Thread CONTINUES after completing [potentially] blocking IO.
3782            ResourceID::BlockedExternalContinue(_) | ResourceID::VforkFailed(_) => {
3783                // We leave the thread out of the run-queue.  At the point we put it back
3784                // in, this resource request is immediately granted.
3785                Ok(())
3786            }
3787
3788            ResourceID::WaitChild { parent, spec } => {
3789                if self.ready_child_wait(*parent, *spec).is_some()
3790                    || !self.has_child_wait_target(*parent, *spec)
3791                {
3792                    Ok(())
3793                } else {
3794                    info!(
3795                        "[scheduler] NONCOMMIT turn {}, parking dettid {} for child {:?}",
3796                        self.turn, dettid, spec
3797                    );
3798                    assert!(
3799                        self.blocked
3800                            .child_waiters
3801                            .insert(dettid, (*parent, *spec))
3802                            .is_none()
3803                    );
3804                    self.skip_turn_blocked(dettid)
3805                }
3806            }
3807
3808            ResourceID::WaitPhysicalChild(child) => {
3809                if self.blocked.physical_child_ready.remove(&dettid) {
3810                    Ok(())
3811                } else {
3812                    info!(
3813                        "[scheduler] NONCOMMIT turn {}, parking dettid {} for physical child {}",
3814                        self.turn, dettid, child
3815                    );
3816                    let completion_already_observed =
3817                        self.completed_physical_process_exits.contains(child);
3818                    assert!(
3819                        self.blocked
3820                            .physical_child_waiters
3821                            .entry(*child)
3822                            .or_default()
3823                            .insert(dettid)
3824                    );
3825                    let skipped = self.skip_turn_blocked(dettid);
3826                    if completion_already_observed {
3827                        self.wake_physical_child_waiters(*child);
3828                    }
3829                    skipped
3830                }
3831            }
3832
3833            // Thread requests change in priority
3834            ResourceID::PriorityChangePoint(prio, change_time, rcbs, epochs) => {
3835                self.perform_priority_changepoint(dettid, *prio, *change_time, *rcbs, epochs)
3836            }
3837
3838            // For now, all other resource types are immediately granted.
3839            // (TODO/FIXME: handle the entire set of resource requests.)
3840            ResourceID::FileContents(_) => Ok(()),
3841            ResourceID::FileMetadata(_) => Ok(()),
3842            ResourceID::DirectoryContents(_) => Ok(()),
3843            ResourceID::MemAddrSpace(_) => Ok(()),
3844            ResourceID::Path(_) => Ok(()),
3845            ResourceID::PathsTransitive(_) => Ok(()),
3846            ResourceID::Device(_) => Ok(()),
3847            // The scheduler-ordered `Exit` grant is the deterministic moment a
3848            // child process leaves the run set. Register a one-shot child-exit
3849            // `SIGCHLD` for the reaping parent, to be delivered at a deterministic
3850            // logical time by `step2b_process_timed`, instead of relying on the
3851            // host-async kernel `SIGCHLD` whose arrival time is host-timed (the
3852            // `make -jN` / redis `--strict --verify` nondeterminism source).
3853            // A backend that registered a PIDFD_THREAD already preserves the
3854            // kernel's native child-exit signal. Sending another SIGCHLD through
3855            // that pidfd would make the application observe both CLD_EXITED and
3856            // SI_TKILL for one child, so only the scheduler wait readiness is
3857            // synthesized on that path.
3858            ResourceID::Exit { group, process, .. } => {
3859                if *group
3860                    && let Some(parent) = self.thread_tree.parent_process(process)
3861                    && self.should_synthesize_child_exit_signal(parent)
3862                {
3863                    // Fire strictly after the current committed time so the event
3864                    // is dispatched on a subsequent scheduler pass (DetTid == DetPid
3865                    // for a group leader, so `parent` is also the parent thread id).
3866                    let deadline = self.committed_time + LogicalTime::from_nanos(1);
3867                    self.blocked
3868                        .timed_waiters
3869                        .insert_child_exit(deadline, *process, parent, parent);
3870                }
3871                Ok(())
3872            }
3873            ResourceID::ParentContinue { .. } => Ok(()),
3874            ResourceID::InternalIOPolling => Ok(()),
3875            ResourceID::FutexWait => Ok(()),
3876            ResourceID::TraceReplay => Ok(()),
3877            ResourceID::SchedYield => Ok(()),
3878
3879            // A guest thread checking in at a happens-before anchor point. Delegate
3880            // to the enforcement logic, which either grants passage (firing anchors)
3881            // or parks the thread until its gating BEFORE anchor fires.
3882            ResourceID::HappensBeforeCheckpoint(count) => self.hb_checkpoint(dettid, *count),
3883
3884            // A host-async SIGCHLD (a guest child process exited) is delivered to
3885            // the parent at a moment decided by host timing. Committing that turn
3886            // immediately makes the signal race whatever guest work was already
3887            // runnable (e.g. a `make -jN` jobserver `pselect6` continuation),
3888            // which diverges under `--strict --verify`. Defer it deterministic-
3889            // work-first: park the parent out of the run queue and let
3890            // `step2e_process_signal_deferred` re-admit it once no ordinary guest
3891            // work remains, mirroring the `external_io_blockers` policy. Signals
3892            // that the scheduler itself synthesizes deterministically (timers via
3893            // `fire_alarm`) are never SIGCHLD and are unaffected.
3894            ResourceID::WaitidSignals(_) => Ok(()),
3895            ResourceID::InboundSignal(sig) => {
3896                // `sigchld_ready` marks a parent step2e has already re-admitted;
3897                // grant it now rather than deferring it a second time.
3898                let already_readmitted = self.blocked.sigchld_ready.remove(&dettid);
3899                if sig.signal() == Some(Signal::SIGCHLD)
3900                    && signal_interrupt_errno.is_none()
3901                    && !already_readmitted
3902                    && self.run_queue.has_runnable_besides(dettid)
3903                {
3904                    self.run_queue.undo_tentative_pop(); // Begun in step3.
3905                    assert!(self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid));
3906                    self.blocked.sigchld_deferred.insert(dettid);
3907                    Err(SkipTurn)
3908                } else {
3909                    Ok(())
3910                }
3911            }
3912        }
3913    }
3914
3915    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-868): Review the vfork registration scheduler barrier.
3916    pub(crate) fn complete_vfork_registration(&mut self, parent: DetTid, child: DetTid) {
3917        let registered_child = self
3918            .vfork_barriers
3919            .get_mut(&parent)
3920            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("vfork child registered without a pending parent {parent}"));
3921        assert!(registered_child.replace(child).is_none());
3922    }
3923
3924    /// Inner helper for just the core priority changing.
3925    fn requeue_with_new_priority(&mut self, dettid: DetTid, new_priority: Priority) {
3926        // TODO: do we want to record in preemption_writer if we are in schedule-trace-replay mode?
3927        assert!(runqueue::is_ordinary_priority(new_priority));
3928        // Alter the threads priority and requeue.
3929        let _old_priority = self.priorities.insert(dettid, new_priority);
3930        let present = self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid);
3931        if present {
3932            self.runqueue_push_back(dettid); // Repush with new priority
3933        }
3934        trace!(
3935            "[dettid {}] requeue: Priority mapping after change to priority {}: {:?}",
3936            dettid, new_priority, self.priorities
3937        );
3938    }
3939
3940    /// Helper for priority changepoint logic
3941    ///
3942    /// Precondition: guest is stopped so that there is no chance the ivars are being used
3943    /// concurrently while they are being cleared.
3944    ///
3945    /// Postcondition: Same as block_for_one_resource. However, always returns SkipTurn, because the
3946    /// priority changepoint may not allow the current thread to continue in a regular turn (i.e.
3947    /// doing actual work).
3948    fn perform_priority_changepoint(
3949        &mut self,
3950        dettid: DetTid,
3951
3952        new_priority: Priority,
3953        guest_time: LogicalTime,
3954        guest_rcbs: u64,
3955        // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
3956        // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1151)
3957        chaos_epochs: &[crate::resources::ChaosEpochTransition],
3958    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
3959        assert!(runqueue::is_ordinary_priority(new_priority));
3960        // Alter the threads priority and requeue.
3961        let old_priority = self.priorities.insert(dettid, new_priority);
3962
3963        // Do not attempt to record preemptions/priorities when we're dictated by a raw schedule replay.
3964        if self.replayer.is_none()
3965            && let Some(pw) = &mut self.preemption_writer
3966        {
3967            let old_prio = old_priority.unwrap();
3968            debug!(
3969                "[dtid {}] Recording preemption point, current time {} prior priority {} (next priority {})",
3970                dettid, guest_time, old_prio, new_priority
3971            );
3972            for transition in chaos_epochs {
3973                pw.insert_chaos_epoch(dettid, *transition);
3974            }
3975            pw.insert_reprioritization(dettid, guest_time, guest_rcbs, old_prio, new_priority);
3976            pw.set_current(dettid, new_priority);
3977        }
3978
3979        let popped = self.run_queue.commit_tentative_pop(); // Begun in step3.
3980        assert_eq!(dettid, popped);
3981        self.runqueue_push_back(dettid); // Repush with new priority
3982        trace!(
3983            "[dettid {}] changepoint: Priority mapping after change to priority {}: {:?}",
3984            dettid, new_priority, self.priorities
3985        );
3986
3987        // Update request to be empty so the thread is unconditionally
3988        // runnable when it next comes up in the queue.
3989        let empty_req = Ivar::full(Ok(Resources::new(dettid)));
3990        trace!(
3991            "[dettid {}] Priority change point emplaced empty resource request at new {}",
3992            dettid, empty_req
3993        );
3994        self.next_turns
3995            .get_mut(&dettid)
3996            .expect("nextturn present")
3997            .req = empty_req;
3998        info!(
3999            "[scheduler] >>>>>>>\n\n NONCOMMIT turn {}, dettid {} changed priority to {}",
4000            self.turn, dettid, new_priority
4001        );
4002        self.skip_turn() // The thread shouldn't run.
4003    }
4004
4005    /// Step1: Wait till threads park. Also tick global logical time due to the scheduler itself.
4006    ///
4007    /// N.B. Currently, as an overapproximation, we check for full quiescence!
4008    ///
4009    /// N.B. This was formerly "step 3" and has been temporarily moved earlier to make
4010    /// things easier for the time being.
4011    fn step1_check_quiescence(
4012        &mut self,
4013        global_time: &Mutex<GlobalTime>,
4014        last_turn: &Result<Resources, SkipTurn>,
4015    ) -> Option<Ivar<SchedRequest>> {
4016        // TODO: actually check resource availability to enable asynchronous background activities!
4017        let outstanding = self.are_all_quiesced();
4018        if outstanding.is_none() {
4019            self.bump_global_time(global_time, last_turn);
4020        }
4021        outstanding
4022    }
4023
4024    fn is_internal_turn(rsrcs: &Resources) -> bool {
4025        Self::is_x_turn(rsrcs, &ResourceID::TraceReplay)
4026    }
4027
4028    /// A turn that only grants `InternalIOPolling`, i.e. a retry of a nonblocking
4029    /// poll/epoll/select/futex/recv/... injected by the blocking-via-polling machinery
4030    /// (see `retry_nonblocking_syscall_helper`). The *number* of such retries before a
4031    /// file descriptor becomes ready is wall-clock dependent when the readiness is driven
4032    /// by an external actor (e.g. a child linker process draining a pipe), so it varies
4033    /// between otherwise-identical runs.
4034    fn is_polling_turn(rsrcs: &Resources) -> bool {
4035        Self::is_x_turn(rsrcs, &ResourceID::InternalIOPolling)
4036    }
4037
4038    /// SaBRe discovers an inherited stdio pipe as a device resource before the inner
4039    /// `InternalIOPolling` request. Both turns belong to one host-timing-sensitive pipe
4040    /// operation, so their logical-time logging must use the same retry normalization.
4041    fn is_sabre_internal_pipe_io_turn(&self, rsrcs: &Resources) -> bool {
4042        rsrcs.fyi == SABRE_INTERNAL_PIPE_IO_FYI
4043    }
4044
4045    /// A strong yield issued by a SaBRe task before a zero-timeout poll while it owns a
4046    /// loopback connection. Its count is kernel-readiness timing, not guest-visible progress.
4047    fn is_sabre_loopback_poll_yield_turn(&self, rsrcs: &Resources) -> bool {
4048        rsrcs.fyi == SABRE_LOOPBACK_POLL_YIELD_FYI
4049    }
4050
4051    fn is_x_turn(rsrcs: &Resources, x: &ResourceID) -> bool {
4052        if rsrcs.resources.contains_key(x) {
4053            if rsrcs.resources.len() > 1 {
4054                panic!(
4055                    "is_x_turn: not expecting an {:?} mixed in with other resource requests: {:?}",
4056                    x, rsrcs
4057                );
4058            }
4059            true
4060        } else {
4061            false
4062        }
4063    }
4064
4065    /// Tick global logical time due to represent the work of the scheduler itself.
4066    /// Also, update committed time.
4067    /// Prerequisite: all threads are parked, with their time contributions frozen.
4068    fn bump_global_time(
4069        &mut self,
4070        global_time: &Mutex<GlobalTime>,
4071        last_turn: &Result<Resources, SkipTurn>,
4072    ) {
4073        // An internal IO-polling retry (see `is_polling_turn`) must still advance logical
4074        // time -- finite poll/epoll/select/futex timeouts are enforced by comparing observed
4075        // logical time against the deadline in `retry_nonblocking_syscall_helper`, so freezing
4076        // time here would turn a timed wait into an infinite spin. But because the *count* of
4077        // these retries is host-timing nondeterministic, we keep their time-advance out of the
4078        // determinism log (DETLOG). This makes the `--verify` deterministic comparison
4079        // insensitive to retry count; the matching `{InternalIOPolling: ...}` COMMIT turn is
4080        // likewise excluded in `logdiff::is_internal_io_poll_commit`. Time values still shift
4081        // between runs, but those are numerically normalized before comparison.
4082        let last_turn_was_polling = last_turn
4083            .as_ref()
4084            .map(|resources| {
4085                Self::is_polling_turn(resources)
4086                    || self.is_sabre_internal_pipe_io_turn(resources)
4087                    || self.is_sabre_loopback_poll_yield_turn(resources)
4088            })
4089            .unwrap_or(false);
4090
4091        // At this moment, when threads are parked, we know that the global_time is
4092        // frozen and we can read it without any race.
4093        let snapshot: LogicalTime = {
4094            let mut gtime = global_time.lock().unwrap();
4095
4096            if self.run_queue.is_empty() && self.blocked.only_external_blocked() {
4097                // TODO(T112017687): rationalize the occurence of
4098                // BlockingExternalIO in strict runs. For example, we should
4099                // probably inject nanosleep and actually wait the intervening
4100                // time, so we don't appear too fast to external observers.
4101                trace!(
4102                    "[scheduler] skipping scheduler time advance because we're ONLY waiting for external events"
4103                );
4104            } else if last_turn.is_err() {
4105                // Note: if the last turn was a skip, it shouldn't really have time-bumped. But since we
4106                // can't see the future, we just cancel out the bump by not doing a bump this turn.
4107                trace!(
4108                    "[scheduler] skipping scheduler time advance because just-finished turn did not progress (i.e. SkipTurn)"
4109                );
4110            } else if last_turn
4111                .as_ref()
4112                .map(Self::is_internal_turn)
4113                .unwrap_or(false)
4114            {
4115                trace!(
4116                    "[scheduler] skipping scheduler time advance because just-finished turn was an internal book-keeping one"
4117                );
4118            } else {
4119                let newtime = gtime.add_scheduler_time();
4120                if last_turn_was_polling {
4121                    // Advance time (needed for timeout enforcement) but keep it off the DETLOG.
4122                    trace!(
4123                        "[sched] advance global time for internal IO-polling retry (suppressed from detlog), new time {:?}",
4124                        newtime,
4125                    );
4126                } else {
4127                    detlog_debug!(
4128                        "[sched] advance global time for scheduler turn, new time {:?}",
4129                        newtime,
4130                    );
4131                }
4132            }
4133            gtime.as_nanos()
4134        };
4135
4136        match snapshot.cmp(&self.committed_time) {
4137            std::cmp::Ordering::Less => {
4138                panic!(
4139                    "bump_global_time: invariant broken, global time went backwards from {} to {}",
4140                    self.committed_time, snapshot
4141                );
4142            }
4143            std::cmp::Ordering::Equal => {}
4144            std::cmp::Ordering::Greater => {
4145                // NB: `committed_time` still tracks the (host-timing-perturbed) global clock,
4146                // including the time advanced by suppressed IO-polling retries above, so this
4147                // line's presence is retry-count sensitive. It is therefore excluded from the
4148                // deterministic `--verify` comparison in `logdiff::is_scheduler_committed_time`
4149                // (it is redundant with the per-turn "advance global time" DETLOG anyway).
4150                detlog_debug!(
4151                    event = crate::detlog::DetLogEvent::SchedulerCommittedTime;
4152                    "[sched-step1] advancing committed_time from {} to {}",
4153                    self.committed_time,
4154                    snapshot
4155                );
4156                self.committed_time = snapshot;
4157            }
4158        }
4159    }
4160
4161    /// Step 4: unblock enabled actions to actually, physically run.
4162    fn step5_guest_unblock(
4163        &mut self,
4164        next_dtid: DetTid,
4165        rsrcs: &Resources,
4166        resp: &Ivar<SchedResponse>,
4167    ) -> Result<(), SkipTurn> {
4168        match self.next_turns.get(&next_dtid) {
4169            None => {
4170                info!(
4171                    "Scheduler was about to schedule {} for a turn (resources {:?}), but it died first.",
4172                    &next_dtid, rsrcs.resources
4173                );
4174                Err(SkipTurn)
4175            }
4176            Some(nxt) => {
4177                assert_eq!(resp, &nxt.resp);
4178                // N.B.: these prints themselves should be deterministic between
4179                // runs.  They are part of the "detlog".
4180                let normalization_marker = if self.is_sabre_internal_pipe_io_turn(rsrcs) {
4181                    " [sabre-internal-pipe-io]"
4182                } else if self.is_sabre_loopback_poll_yield_turn(rsrcs) {
4183                    " [sabre-loopback-poll-zero-timeout]"
4184                } else {
4185                    ""
4186                };
4187                if enabled!(Level::INFO) {
4188                    let internal_io_poll =
4189                        rsrcs.resources.contains_key(&ResourceID::InternalIOPolling)
4190                            || self.is_sabre_internal_pipe_io_turn(rsrcs)
4191                            || self.is_sabre_loopback_poll_yield_turn(rsrcs);
4192                    let runtime_maps_read = rsrcs.resources.keys().any(|resource| {
4193                        matches!(
4194                            resource,
4195                            ResourceID::Path(path)
4196                                if path.as_path() == std::path::Path::new("/proc/self/maps")
4197                        )
4198                    });
4199                    let record_suffix = scheduler_commit_record_suffix(
4200                        self.turn,
4201                        self.committed_time.as_nanos(),
4202                        internal_io_poll,
4203                        runtime_maps_read,
4204                    );
4205                    info!(
4206                        "[sched-step5] >>>>>>>\n\n COMMIT turn {}, dettid {} using resources {:?}, on previously committed {}{}{}",
4207                        self.turn,
4208                        next_dtid,
4209                        rsrcs.resources,
4210                        self.committed_time,
4211                        normalization_marker,
4212                        record_suffix,
4213                    );
4214                }
4215                self.unblock_guest(next_dtid, resp);
4216                Ok(())
4217            }
4218        }
4219    }
4220
4221    /// Unblock the guest to run, clear its ivars for the next turn, and increment the turn counter.
4222    ///
4223    /// Precondition: guest is stopped.
4224    /// Postcondition: guest is running concurrently with this scheduler/tracer thread.
4225    fn unblock_guest(&mut self, dtid: DetTid, resp: &Ivar<SchedResponse>) {
4226        self.turn += 1;
4227        trace!(
4228            "[sched-step5] Guest unblocking (via {}); clear ivars for the next turn on dettid {}",
4229            &resp, &dtid
4230        );
4231        let signals = self.inbound_signals(dtid); // Peek before we clear the ivars.
4232        let futex_timed_out = self.blocked.timed_out_futex_waiters.remove(&dtid);
4233        self.clear_nextturn(dtid);
4234        let answer = if !signals.is_empty() {
4235            SchedResponse::Signaled(Some(signals))
4236        } else if futex_timed_out {
4237            SchedResponse::Go(Some(SchedValue::TimeOut))
4238        } else {
4239            let timeslice = self.timeslices.remove(&dtid).flatten();
4240            // TODO(T137799529): use a more strongly typed representation rather than reusing
4241            // SchedValue/u64:
4242            let as_schedvalue = timeslice
4243                .as_ref()
4244                .map(LogicalTime::as_nanos)
4245                .map(SchedValue::Value);
4246            SchedResponse::Go(as_schedvalue)
4247        };
4248        resp.put(answer);
4249    }
4250
4251    fn inbound_signals(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> Vec<SigWrapper> {
4252        let req = &self.next_turns.get(&dettid).unwrap().req;
4253        let mut signals = Vec::new();
4254        if let Some(Ok(resources)) = req.try_read() {
4255            for resource in resources.resources.keys() {
4256                match resource {
4257                    ResourceID::InboundSignal(signal) => signals.push(*signal),
4258                    ResourceID::WaitidSignals(batch) => signals.extend(batch.iter().copied()),
4259                    _ => {}
4260                }
4261            }
4262        }
4263        // A signal can legitimately appear in both an `InboundSignal` and a
4264        // merged `WaitidSignals` batch. Sort then dedup so the reported set is a
4265        // canonical function of WHICH signals are pending, not of how many
4266        // resources happen to carry each one or of the order they arrived in.
4267        signals.sort_by_key(|signal| signal.0 as libc::c_int);
4268        signals.dedup_by_key(|signal| signal.0 as libc::c_int);
4269        signals
4270    }
4271
4272    /// Clear the thread's nextturn, installing fresh ivars.
4273    ///
4274    /// Precondition: guest is stopped so that there is no chance the ivars are being used
4275    /// concurrently while they are being cleared.
4276    fn clear_nextturn(&mut self, dtid: DetTid) {
4277        let nextturn = self
4278            .next_turns
4279            .get_mut(&dtid)
4280            .expect("clear_nextturn: Thread should be available in next_turns");
4281        nextturn.req = Ivar::new();
4282        nextturn.resp = Ivar::new();
4283    }
4284
4285    /// Step: reenqueue the thread that just had a turn.
4286    fn step6_reenquue(&mut self, next_dtid: DetTid, sched_yield: bool) {
4287        // We delay popping till here, so while holding the lock we "atomically" move the
4288        // thread from the front to the back of the queue.
4289        let dt2 = self.run_queue.commit_tentative_pop_completed_turn();
4290        assert_eq!(next_dtid, dt2);
4291        // SchedYield is emitted in normal execution and non-chaos preemption replay. Its
4292        // queue placement is transient, so persistent priorities remain unchanged.
4293        let pos = if sched_yield {
4294            let priority = self.get_priority(next_dtid);
4295            self.run_queue.push_yielded(next_dtid, priority)
4296        } else {
4297            self.runqueue_push_back(next_dtid)
4298        };
4299        debug!(
4300            "[sched-step6] dettid {} going back into queue at position {}.",
4301            next_dtid, pos
4302        );
4303    }
4304
4305    /// Add a simulated "post hook" for exit calls which we're about to let through.
4306    /// ALTERNATIVE: this could happen later when the thread_exit hook comes through.
4307    fn step7_simulate_exit_posthook(
4308        &mut self,
4309        dettid: DetTid,
4310        placeholder_syscall: Syscall,
4311        global_time: &Mutex<GlobalTime>,
4312    ) {
4313        let replay = self.replayer.is_some();
4314        let record = self.preemption_writer.is_some();
4315        if !(replay || record) {
4316            return;
4317        }
4318        let thread_duration = global_time.lock().unwrap().threads_duration(dettid);
4319        debug!(
4320            "simulate exit posthook on tid {}, thread time {}: {:?}",
4321            dettid, thread_duration, placeholder_syscall
4322        );
4323
4324        let ev = SchedEvent::syscall(dettid, placeholder_syscall.number(), SyscallPhase::Posthook)
4325            .with_time(thread_duration);
4326        let print_stack1 = if replay {
4327            let ConsumeResult {
4328                keep_running,
4329                print_stack,
4330                event_ix: _,
4331                timeslice_remaining: _,
4332            } = self.consume_schedevent(&ev);
4333            // We should not ever need to background the thread when it is going to exit anyway.
4334            if !keep_running {
4335                tracing::warn!(
4336                    "simulate_exit_posthook: unexpectedly asked to background the current, exiting thread {}",
4337                    dettid
4338                );
4339            }
4340            print_stack
4341        } else {
4342            None
4343        };
4344        let print_stack2 = if record { self.record_event(&ev) } else { None };
4345        if print_stack1.is_some() || print_stack2.is_some() {
4346            eprintln!(
4347                ":: Guest tid {}, at thread time {}, backtrace requested but not available post-exit!\n",
4348                dettid, thread_duration
4349            );
4350        }
4351    }
4352
4353    /// Get the priority for a thread; panic if absent.
4354    fn get_priority(&self, dettid: DetTid) -> Priority {
4355        *self
4356            .priorities
4357            .get(&dettid)
4358            .expect("get_priority: all threads should have a persistent priority")
4359    }
4360
4361    /// Push_back a thread onto the runqueue, respecting its persistent priority
4362    /// value. This should be the ordinary way threads are pushed onto the queue.
4363    pub fn runqueue_push_back(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) -> PrioritizedOrder {
4364        let priority = self.get_priority(dettid);
4365        self.run_queue.push_back(dettid, priority)
4366    }
4367
4368    /// Push a thread to the front of its persistent priority band.
4369    ///
4370    /// This is reserved for protocol handoffs where the queued thread must run
4371    /// before an equal-priority peer, such as ordinary clone child startup.
4372    pub(crate) fn runqueue_push_front(&mut self, dettid: DetTid) -> PrioritizedOrder {
4373        let priority = self.get_priority(dettid);
4374        self.run_queue.push_front(dettid, priority)
4375    }
4376
4377    /// Record an intent to admit `dtid` to the run queue, applied by the daemon
4378    /// at the next deterministic drain point ([`step2`](Self::step2_process_blocked)).
4379    ///
4380    /// Global-request handlers (`recv_create_child_thread`,
4381    /// `reconnect_after_exec`) hold the scheduler lock but run on whichever
4382    /// backend worker fielded the RPC, not on the scheduler daemon's turn. The
4383    /// point in the daemon's loop at which such a handler acquires the lock is
4384    /// host-timing-dependent on asynchronous backends (e.g. DBT): it may land
4385    /// inside the tentative-pop window (between `step3_peek` and `step4`'s
4386    /// commit, where the lock is released across `req.get().await`) *or* outside
4387    /// it (during the quiescence-wait / backoff awaits at the top of
4388    /// `do_a_turn_blocking`, where `tentative_selection` is `None`). A design
4389    /// that pushed directly whenever the window happened to be closed would make
4390    /// the *admission order* — and, under `RunsPostFork::Random`, the PRNG draw
4391    /// order — a function of that host timing: two equal-priority admissions
4392    /// could enter the queue in either relative order across otherwise-identical
4393    /// runs, and a fixed seed could explore different schedules.
4394    ///
4395    /// So admission is *always* deferred, never applied directly here. Handlers
4396    /// only record the unresolved [`AdmitIntent`]; the daemon resolves the side
4397    /// (drawing any `RunsPostFork::Random` value) and pushes the run queue at the
4398    /// single `step2` drain, in canonical `DetTid` order, before `step3` opens a
4399    /// tentative window. Draining is also the only place `remove_tid`'s tentative
4400    /// guard is guaranteed to hold.
4401    ///
4402    /// # What this does and does not make deterministic
4403    ///
4404    /// **Synchronous backends (ptrace): fully deterministic, and byte-identical
4405    /// to the pre-deferral behavior.** Handlers run post-commit, one per turn, so
4406    /// at most one admission is buffered per turn and it drains at the next
4407    /// `step2` — before that turn's `step3` selection — yielding the same
4408    /// selection sequence and the same one-draw-per-fork PRNG order as an
4409    /// immediate push.
4410    ///
4411    /// **Asynchronous backends: every production admission site has a causal or
4412    /// explicit barrier that fixes its drain, and order within that drain is
4413    /// canonical.** A bare off-turn handler would still be insufficient: a
4414    /// `BTreeMap` only canonicalizes items already in one snapshot. The current
4415    /// sites additionally bind snapshot membership to deterministic scheduler
4416    /// state:
4417    ///
4418    /// * **Ordinary clone — anchored, causally.** `CreateChildThread` issues the
4419    ///   parent's `ParentContinue` request only *after* buffering the child's
4420    ///   admission, so no thread can run between the two and the admission
4421    ///   cannot straddle a drain boundary.
4422    /// * **`vfork` — anchored, by barrier.** `vfork_barriers` /
4423    ///   [`Scheduler::step2a_wait_for_vfork_barrier`] hold the parent until the
4424    ///   child has registered, which fixes the drain.
4425    /// * **Multi-threaded exec reconnect — anchored, causally.** Step5 installs
4426    ///   the caller's empty next-turn request before it executes exec. The
4427    ///   reconnect handler atomically buffers the old-leader removal and new
4428    ///   incarnation admission, then retires the caller and resolves that
4429    ///   request. Step1 therefore cannot release step2 before the complete pair
4430    ///   exists. [`Scheduler::replace_retired_run_queue_incarnation`] binds the
4431    ///   same-raw-TID handoff explicitly.
4432    ///
4433    /// Thus both membership and within-drain resolution are functions of
4434    /// deterministic state for all current sites. The exec regression test
4435    /// forces the daemon to wait before reconnect, varies host yields, and
4436    /// compares the exact first-drain queue plus the next post-fork PRNG draw.
4437    pub(crate) fn admit_to_run_queue(&mut self, dtid: DetTid, intent: AdmitIntent) {
4438        let prev = self.pending_run_queue_admissions.insert(dtid, intent);
4439        debug_assert!(
4440            prev.is_none(),
4441            "thread {:?} recorded for run-queue admission twice before draining",
4442            dtid
4443        );
4444    }
4445
4446    /// Atomically classify a same-raw-TID exec handoff and record its fresh
4447    /// admission. The scheduler mutex serializes this method with `step2`, so a
4448    /// drain can never observe only one half of the handoff.
4449    fn replace_retired_run_queue_incarnation(&mut self, dtid: DetTid, intent: AdmitIntent) {
4450        let disposition = self
4451            .pending_run_queue_removals
4452            .get_mut(&dtid)
4453            .unwrap_or_else(|| {
4454                panic!(
4455                    "exec replacement {:?} has no retired run-queue incarnation",
4456                    dtid
4457                )
4458            });
4459        assert_eq!(
4460            *disposition,
4461            RemovalDisposition::Retire,
4462            "exec replacement {:?} was classified more than once",
4463            dtid
4464        );
4465        *disposition = RemovalDisposition::ReplaceThenAdmit;
4466        assert!(
4467            self.pending_run_queue_admissions
4468                .insert(dtid, intent)
4469                .is_none(),
4470            "exec replacement {:?} already had a pending admission",
4471            dtid
4472        );
4473    }
4474
4475    /// Resolve an [`AdmitIntent`] to a concrete [`AdmitSide`], consuming the
4476    /// post-fork PRNG draw for `RunsPostFork::Random`.
4477    ///
4478    /// Called at the drain rather than in the handler, so the draw is never
4479    /// consumed in host *RPC arrival* order, and the draws taken within one
4480    /// drain follow canonical `DetTid` order. This function only canonicalizes
4481    /// draws within a fixed drain; each admission site must separately bind its
4482    /// drain membership to deterministic scheduler state. See
4483    /// [`Scheduler::admit_to_run_queue`] for the causal and explicit barriers
4484    /// that provide that binding for all current production sites.
4485    fn resolve_admit_intent(&mut self, intent: AdmitIntent) -> AdmitSide {
4486        match intent {
4487            AdmitIntent::Fixed(side) => side,
4488            AdmitIntent::PostFork(mode) => {
4489                if self.child_runs_first_post_fork(mode) {
4490                    AdmitSide::Front
4491                } else {
4492                    AdmitSide::Back
4493                }
4494            }
4495        }
4496    }
4497
4498    /// Record an intent to remove `dtid` from the run queue, applied by the
4499    /// daemon at the next deterministic drain point ([`step2`](Self::step2_process_blocked)).
4500    ///
4501    /// The mirror of [`Scheduler::admit_to_run_queue`] for the removal side, and
4502    /// deferred for the same reason: a global-request handler
4503    /// (`reconnect_after_exec` -> `logically_kill_thread`) runs on a backend
4504    /// worker and may hold the lock inside the daemon's tentative-pop window,
4505    /// where `RunQueue::remove_tid`'s `tentative_selection.is_none()` assert
4506    /// would trip and poison the scheduler mutex. Recording the removal and
4507    /// applying it at `step2` (window closed, guard holds) avoids that. The
4508    /// caller has already made the thread logically dead (cleared `next_turns`,
4509    /// resolved its request to `ThreadExited`), so leaving its stale run-queue
4510    /// entry in place until the drain is inert: the daemon skips it for any
4511    /// intervening turn (`step3`'s pick is validated against `next_turns`) and
4512    /// `are_all_quiesced` filters it out. On ptrace the drain runs at the next
4513    /// `step2`, before that turn's `step3` selection, so the removal is
4514    /// observationally immediate — the dead thread is never selected.
4515    fn deschedule_or_defer(&mut self, dtid: DetTid) {
4516        // A later logical death of a not-yet-drained exec replacement must
4517        // override `ReplaceThenAdmit`: `remove_blocking_entries` clears its
4518        // admission and this `Retire` disposition prevents resurrection.
4519        self.pending_run_queue_removals
4520            .insert(dtid, RemovalDisposition::Retire);
4521    }
4522
4523    /// Apply queued cross-task signal notifications at the deterministic
4524    /// run-queue mutation point. Re-check the request because a target may have
4525    /// exited or completed its wait before this drain.
4526    fn drain_pending_cross_task_signals(&mut self) {
4527        let pending = std::mem::take(&mut self.pending_cross_task_signals);
4528        for (dettid, mut signals) in pending {
4529            match self.waitid_signal_request(dettid) {
4530                Some(WaitidSignalRequest::Parked) => {
4531                    // Decide run-queue residency HERE, by asking the queue, and
4532                    // never by inferring it from which resource the thread
4533                    // holds. A thread can hold either park request while already
4534                    // queued: `wake_child_waiters` re-admits a waiter without
4535                    // clearing its `WaitChild` request, and `step6_reenqueue`
4536                    // pushes a completed turn back before the guest issues its
4537                    // next request. `force_unblock_thread` ends in
4538                    // `runqueue_push_*`, so pushing an already-queued thread
4539                    // trips the run-queue invariant under `debug_assertions`
4540                    // and, worse, SILENTLY double-enqueues in release — one
4541                    // thread selected twice. This mirrors `wake_signaled_guest`,
4542                    // which likewise consults `thread_status` before removing.
4543                    if self.run_queue.contains_tid(dettid) {
4544                        let removed = self.run_queue.remove_tid(dettid);
4545                        debug_assert!(
4546                            removed,
4547                            "run_queue.contains_tid disagreed with remove_tid for {dettid}"
4548                        );
4549                    }
4550                    signals.sort_by_key(SigWrapper::raw);
4551                    signals.dedup();
