6552    #[test]
6553    fn unsupported_backend_does_not_rewrite_a_marked_internal_poller() {
6554        let config = Config {
6555            backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption: false,
6556            ..Config::default()
6557        };
6558        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
6559        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6560        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6561        let mut polling = Resources::new(target);
6562        polling.insert(ResourceID::InternalIOPolling, Permission::W);
6563        polling.set_signal_interrupt_errno(Errno::ERESTARTSYS);
6564        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(polling));
6565        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6566
6567        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1));
6568        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6569
6570        assert!(scheduler.pending_cross_task_signals.is_empty());
6571        assert!(scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target));
6572        assert!(scheduler.inbound_signals(target).is_empty());
6573        assert!(scheduler.take_terminal_deadlock().is_none());
6574        let resources = scheduler
6575            .next_turns
6576            .get(&target)
6577            .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
6578            .and_then(Result::ok)
6579            .expect("the target should retain its polling request");
6580        assert!(
6581            resources
6582                .resources
6583                .contains_key(&ResourceID::InternalIOPolling)
6584        );
6585        assert_eq!(
6586            resources.signal_interrupt_errno(),
6587            Some(Errno::ERESTARTSYS.into_raw())
6588        );
6589    }
6590
6591    #[test]
6592    fn pending_signals_preserve_a_restartable_internal_write_pollers_signal_set() {
6593        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6594        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6595        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6596        let mut polling = Resources::new(target);
6597        polling.insert(ResourceID::InternalIOPolling, Permission::W);
6598        polling.set_signal_interrupt_errno(Errno::ERESTARTSYS);
6599        polling.fyi("writev");
6600        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(polling));
6601        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6602
6603        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2));
6604        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1));
6605        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6606
6607        assert!(scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target));
6608        assert_eq!(
6609            scheduler.inbound_signals(target),
6610            vec![
6611                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1),
6612                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2)
6613            ]
6614        );
6615        let resources = scheduler
6616            .next_turns
6617            .get(&target)
6618            .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
6619            .and_then(Result::ok)
6620            .expect("the target should hold its signal interruption request");
6621        assert_eq!(resources.resources.len(), 1);
6622        assert!(
6623            resources
6624                .resources
6625                .contains_key(&ResourceID::WaitidSignals(vec![
6626                    SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1),
6627                    SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2)
6628                ]))
6629        );
6630        assert_eq!(
6631            resources.signal_interrupt_errno(),
6632            Some(Errno::ERESTARTSYS.into_raw())
6633        );
6634
6635        // A signal arriving after the request was rewritten must remain part
6636        // of the same one-resource response rather than being dropped.
6637        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGTERM));
6638        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6639        assert_eq!(
6640            scheduler.inbound_signals(target),
6641            vec![
6642                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1),
6643                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2),
6644                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGTERM),
6645            ]
6646        );
6647
6648        let response = scheduler.next_turns[&target].resp.clone();
6649        let (selected, _, selected_response) = scheduler.step3_peek().unwrap();
6650        assert_eq!(selected, target);
6651        assert!(
6652            scheduler
6653                .step4_resource_block(target, &resources, &selected_response)
6654                .is_ok()
6655        );
6656        assert!(
6657            scheduler
6658                .step5_guest_unblock(target, &resources, &selected_response)
6659                .is_ok()
6660        );
6661        assert!(matches!(
6662            response.try_read(),
6663            Some(SchedResponse::Signaled(Some(signals)))
6664                if signals == vec![
6665                    SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1),
6666                    SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2),
6667                    SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGTERM),
6668                ]
6669        ));
6670    }

6675        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);

6683        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6684        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(sibling);

6712
6713    /// `step4_resource_block` and `blocking_request_is_ready` both assert that a
6714    /// request carries exactly one resource. The drain must never violate that.
6715    ///
6716    /// An earlier revision merged a pending signal into a request that already
6717    /// held an `InboundSignal`, producing a two-resource request. The scheduler
6718    /// daemon then panicked and the container hung — on a guest containing no
6719    /// `waitid` at all, because `{InboundSignal}` is exactly what `signal_guest`
6720    /// installs for an ordinary cross-thread signal.
6721    #[test]
6722    fn drain_never_builds_a_multi_resource_request() {
6723        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6724        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6725        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6726        // The shape that crashed: an ordinary delivered signal, nothing to do
6727        // with waitid.
6728        let mut inbound = Resources::new(target);
6729        inbound.insert(
6730            ResourceID::InboundSignal(SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1)),
6731            Permission::W,
6732        );
6733        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(inbound));
6734        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6735
6736        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2));
6737        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6738
6739        let resources = scheduler
6740            .next_turns
6741            .get(&target)
6742            .and_then(|next_turn| next_turn.req.try_read())
6743            .and_then(Result::ok)
6744            .expect("the target should still hold a request");
6745        assert!(
6746            resources.resources.len() <= 1,
6747            "the drain produced a {}-resource request, which step4 asserts against: {:?}",
6748            resources.resources.len(),
6749            resources.resources,
6750        );
6751    }
6752
6753    /// A thread can hold a `WaitChild` request while ALREADY in the run queue:
6754    /// `wake_child_waiters` re-admits a waiter without clearing its request, and
6755    /// `step6_reenqueue` pushes a completed turn back before the guest issues
6756    /// its next request. An earlier revision inferred "blocked" from the
6757    /// resource and skipped the run-queue removal, so the drain pushed a
6758    /// second copy of the thread.
6759    ///
6760    /// That failure is worse than it looks: `RunQueue`'s duplicate check is
6761    /// `cfg!(debug_assertions)`, so debug builds panic with "Invariant
6762    /// violation! Tried to add ... already present" while RELEASE builds
6763    /// silently enqueue the thread twice and select it twice.
6764    #[test]
6765    fn pending_signal_does_not_double_queue_a_runnable_child_waiter() {
6766        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6767        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6768        let parent = DetPid::from_raw(100);
6769        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6770        let spec = ChildWaitSpec {
6771            selector: ChildWaitSelector::Any,
6772            owner: None,
6773            exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
6774        };
6775        let mut waiting = Resources::new(target);
6776        waiting.insert(ResourceID::WaitChild { parent, spec }, Permission::R);
6777        waiting.fyi("wait-child-lifecycle");
6778        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(waiting));
6779        // The distinguishing state: holds WaitChild AND is runnable, with no
6780        // `child_waiters` entry -- exactly what a just-woken waiter looks like.
6781        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6782        assert!(scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target));
6783        assert!(!scheduler.blocked.child_waiters.contains_key(&target));
6784
6785        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1));
6786        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6787
6788        let occurrences = scheduler
6789            .run_queue
6790            .tids()
6791            .filter(|tid| **tid == target)
6792            .count();
6793        assert_eq!(
6794            occurrences, 1,
6795            "the signalled child waiter must appear in the run queue exactly once"
6796        );
6797    }
6798
6799    /// A thread parked on a scheduler-managed child wait is blocked in
6800    /// `child_waiters`, never in the run queue, so the run-queue removal the
6801    /// polling disposition performs does not apply to it. Before this case was
6802    /// handled, a signal aimed at such a thread was recorded nowhere and a wait
6803    /// on a child that never exits could not be interrupted at all.
6804    #[test]
6805    fn pending_signal_unblocks_a_managed_child_waiter() {
6806        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6807        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6808        let parent = DetPid::from_raw(100);
6809        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6810        let spec = ChildWaitSpec {
6811            selector: ChildWaitSelector::Any,
6812            owner: None,
6813            exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
6814        };
6815        let mut waiting = Resources::new(target);
6816        waiting.insert(ResourceID::WaitChild { parent, spec }, Permission::R);
6817        waiting.fyi("wait-child-lifecycle");
6818        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(waiting));
6819        // Parked, not runnable: exactly how `WaitChild` leaves a thread.
6820        scheduler
6821            .blocked
6822            .child_waiters
6823            .insert(target, (parent, spec));
6824        assert!(!scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target));
6825
6826        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1));
6827        assert_eq!(scheduler.pending_cross_task_signals[&target].len(), 1);
6828
6829        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6830
6831        assert!(
6832            scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target),
6833            "the signalled child waiter must be requeued"
6834        );
6835        assert!(
6836            !scheduler.blocked.child_waiters.contains_key(&target),
6837            "its child-wait blocking entry must be cleared"
6838        );
6839        assert_eq!(
6840            scheduler.inbound_signals(target),
6841            vec![SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1)],
6842            "the resume must name the signal that woke it"
6843        );
6844    }

6846    #[test]
6847    fn pending_signal_replaces_an_internal_poller_request() {
6848        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6849        let target = DetTid::from_raw(100);
6850        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, target);
6851        let mut polling = Resources::new(target);
6852        polling.insert(ResourceID::InternalIOPolling, Permission::W);
6853        polling.fyi("waitid");
6854        scheduler.next_turns.get_mut(&target).unwrap().req = Ivar::full(Ok(polling));
6855        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(target);
6856
6857        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2));
6858
6859        assert_eq!(scheduler.pending_cross_task_signals[&target].len(), 1);
6860        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6861        assert!(scheduler.run_queue.contains_tid(target));
6862        assert_eq!(
6863            scheduler.inbound_signals(target),
6864            vec![SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2)]
6865        );
6866
6867        // A later signal must merge into the already-materialized waitid wakeup.
6868        scheduler.notify_signal_pending(target, SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1));
6869        assert_eq!(scheduler.pending_cross_task_signals[&target].len(), 1);
6870        scheduler.drain_pending_cross_task_signals();
6871        assert_eq!(
6872            scheduler.inbound_signals(target),
6873            vec![
6874                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR1),
6875                SigWrapper::from(Signal::SIGUSR2)
6876            ]
6877        );
6878    }

6959    #[test]
6960    fn alarm_deadline_uses_observed_logical_time() {
6961        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
6962        let detpid = DetPid::from_raw(100);
6963        let dettid = DetTid::from_raw(101);
6964        let now = LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000);
6965        let duration = LogicalTime::from_nanos(250);
6966
6967        assert_eq!(
6968            scheduler.register_alarm(
6969                detpid,
6970                dettid,
6971                now,
6972                duration,
6973                LogicalTime::ZERO,
6974                Signal::SIGALRM,
6975            ),
6976            (LogicalTime::ZERO, LogicalTime::ZERO)
6977        );
6978        assert_eq!(
6979            scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.iter().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
6980            vec![(
6981                LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_250),
6982                TimedEvent::SignalEvt(
6983                    timed_waiters::SignalTimerId::Alarm(detpid),
6984                    dettid,
6985                    Signal::SIGALRM,
6986                )
6987            )]
6988        );
6989        assert_eq!(
6990            scheduler.alarm_remaining(detpid, LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_100)),
6991            LogicalTime::from_nanos(150)
6992        );
6993        assert_eq!(
6994            scheduler.alarm_remaining(detpid, LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_300)),
6995            LogicalTime::ZERO
6996        );
6997
6998        let cancel_time = LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_100);
6999        assert_eq!(
7000            scheduler.register_alarm(
7001                detpid,
7002                dettid,
7003                cancel_time,
7004                LogicalTime::ZERO,
7005                LogicalTime::ZERO,
7006                Signal::SIGALRM
7007            ),
7008            (LogicalTime::from_nanos(150), LogicalTime::ZERO)
7009        );
7010        assert!(scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty());
7011    }

7035
7036    #[test]
7037    fn physical_exit_barrier_precedes_empty_queue_timer_fast_forward() {
7038        let config = Config {
7039            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: true,
7040            ..Config::default()
7041        };
7042        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7043        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7044        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7045        let exit_deadline = initial_time + LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000);
7046        let first_process = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7047        let second_process = DetPid::from_raw(200);
7048        let unrelated_process = DetPid::from_raw(300);
7049
7050        assert!(scheduler.begin_physical_process_exit(first_process));
7051        assert!(!scheduler.begin_physical_process_exit(first_process));
7052        assert!(scheduler.begin_physical_process_exit(second_process));
7053        scheduler.register_alarm(
7054            first_process,
7055            first_process,
7056            initial_time,
7057            LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000),
7058            LogicalTime::ZERO,
7059            Signal::SIGALRM,
7060        );
7061
7062        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7063        assert_eq!(
7064            scheduler
7065                .blocked
7066                .timed_waiters
7067                .iter()
7068                .map(|(time, _)| time)
7069                .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
7070            vec![exit_deadline]
7071        );
7072        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7073
7074        assert!(!scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(unrelated_process));
7075        assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(first_process));
7076        assert!(!scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(first_process));
7077        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7078        assert!(!scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty());
7079        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7080
7081        assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(second_process));
7082        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7083        assert!(scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty());
7084        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), exit_deadline);
7085    }
7086
7087    fn physical_wait_handoff_queue(completion_before_wait: bool) -> Vec<DetTid> {
7088        let config = Config {
7089            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: true,
7090            ..Config::default()
7091        };
7092        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7093        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7094        let waiter = DetTid::from_raw(100);
7095        let sibling = DetTid::from_raw(200);
7096        let child = DetPid::from_raw(300);
7097        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, waiter);
7098        register_known_thread(&mut scheduler, sibling);
7099        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(waiter);
7100        scheduler.runqueue_push_back(sibling);
7101
7102        assert!(scheduler.begin_physical_process_exit(child));
7103        if completion_before_wait {
7104            assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(child));
7105        }
7106
7107        assert_eq!(scheduler.run_queue.tentative_pop_tid(waiter), Some(waiter));
7108        assert!(
7109            scheduler
7110                .block_for_one_resource(
7111                    waiter,
7112                    &ResourceID::WaitPhysicalChild(child),
7113                    &Permission::W,
7114                    None,
7115                    &Ivar::new(),
7116                )
7117                .is_err()
7118        );
7119        assert_eq!(
7120            scheduler.run_queue.tids().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
7121            vec![sibling]
7122        );
7123
7124        if !completion_before_wait {
7125            assert!(
7126                scheduler.step2_process_blocked(&global_time).is_err(),
7127                "a runnable sibling must not pass the pending physical-exit barrier"
7128            );
7129            assert_eq!(
7130                scheduler.run_queue.tids().copied().collect::<Vec<_>>(),
7131                vec![sibling]
7132            );
7133            assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(child));
7134        }
7135
7136        assert!(scheduler.step2_process_blocked(&global_time).is_ok());
7137        assert!(scheduler.pending_run_queue_admissions.is_empty());
7138        assert!(scheduler.blocked.physical_child_ready.contains(&waiter));
7139        scheduler.run_queue.tids().copied().collect()
7140    }

7177    #[test]
7178    fn terminal_wait_selects_group_owner_and_clone_class() {
7179        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&Config::default());
7180        let parent = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7181        let owner_a = DetTid::from_raw(101);
7182        let owner_b = DetTid::from_raw(102);
7183        let normal = DetPid::from_raw(200);
7184        let clone_child = DetPid::from_raw(300);
7185        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(parent, parent, true);
7186        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(parent, owner_a, false);
7187        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(parent, owner_b, false);
7188        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child_with_wait_metadata(
7189            owner_a,
7190            normal,
7191            true,
7192            false,
7193            libc::SIGCHLD,
7194        );
7195        scheduler
7196            .thread_tree
7197            .add_child_with_wait_metadata(owner_b, clone_child, true, false, 0);
7198        let group = DetPid::from_raw(77);
7199        assert!(scheduler.thread_tree.set_process_group(normal, group));
7200        assert!(scheduler.thread_tree.set_process_group(clone_child, group));
7201        scheduler.logically_exited_processes.insert(normal);
7202        scheduler.logically_exited_processes.insert(clone_child);
7203
7204        assert_eq!(
7205            scheduler.ready_child_wait(
7206                parent,
7207                ChildWaitSpec {
7208                    selector: ChildWaitSelector::ProcessGroup(DetPid::from_raw(78)),
7209                    owner: Some(owner_a),
7210                    exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
7211                },
7212            ),
7213            None,
7214            "a different process group must not match"
7215        );
7216        assert_eq!(
7217            scheduler.ready_child_wait(
7218                parent,
7219                ChildWaitSpec {
7220                    selector: ChildWaitSelector::Exact(normal),
7221                    owner: Some(owner_b),
7222                    exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
7223                },
7224            ),
7225            None,
7226            "__WNOTHREAD must exclude a child created by another task"
7227        );
7228        assert_eq!(
7229            scheduler.ready_child_wait(
7230                parent,
7231                ChildWaitSpec {
7232                    selector: ChildWaitSelector::Exact(clone_child),
7233                    owner: Some(owner_b),
7234                    exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
7235                },
7236            ),
7237            None,
7238            "a clone child must not enter the ordinary SIGCHLD population"
7239        );
7240        assert_eq!(
7241            scheduler.ready_child_wait(
7242                parent,
7243                ChildWaitSpec {
7244                    selector: ChildWaitSelector::ProcessGroup(group),
7245                    owner: Some(owner_b),
7246                    exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Clone,
7247                },
7248            ),
7249            Some(clone_child)
7250        );
7251        assert_eq!(
7252            scheduler.ready_child_wait(
7253                parent,
7254                ChildWaitSpec {
7255                    selector: ChildWaitSelector::Any,
7256                    owner: Some(owner_a),
7257                    exit_class: ChildWaitExitClass::Sigchld,
7258                },
7259            ),
7260            Some(normal)
7261        );
7262    }

7294
7295    /// Populate a scheduler with a deadlock's worth of blocked state, inserting
7296    /// each collection in the caller's chosen order.
7297    ///
7298    /// `reverse` exists so the same logical state can be built two ways: the
7299    /// report must not depend on insertion order, because `futex_waiters` is a
7300    /// `HashMap`, `timed_out_futex_waiters` a `HashSet`, and
7301    /// `Resources::resources` a `HashMap`.
7302    fn deadlocked_scheduler(config: &Config, reverse: bool) -> Scheduler {
7303        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(config);
7304        let mm = MmId::initial(DetTid::from_raw(3));
7305        let mut futexes = vec![
7306            (FutexID::private(mm, 0x404100), 5),
7307            (FutexID::private(mm, 0x404200), 7),
7308            (FutexID::private(mm, 0x404300), 9),
7309        ];
7310        let mut tids = vec![3, 5, 7, 9];
7311        if reverse {
7312            futexes.reverse();
7313            tids.reverse();
7314        }
7315
7316        for (futex_id, dettid) in futexes {
7317            scheduler
7318                .blocked
7319                .futex_waiters
7320                .entry(futex_id)
7321                .or_default()
7322                .push(futex_waiter(dettid, u32::MAX));
7323        }
7324        for dettid in tids {
7325            let dettid = DetTid::from_raw(dettid);
7326            // A request carrying several resources: `Resources::resources` is a
7327            // HashMap, so an unsorted render would vary here too.
7328            let mut resources = Resources::new(dettid);
7329            resources.insert(ResourceID::FutexWait, Permission::R);
7330            resources.insert(ResourceID::MemAddrSpace(dettid), Permission::RW);
7331            scheduler.next_turns.insert(
7332                dettid,
7333                ThreadNextTurn {
7334                    dettid,
7335                    child_tid_addr: 0,
7336                    // A FULL ivar: `Debug` on this prints the parked waker's raw
7337                    // host pointers, which is exactly what must not reach stderr.
7338                    req: Ivar::full(Ok(resources)),
7339                    resp: Ivar::new(),
7340                },
7341            );
7342            scheduler.blocked.timed_out_futex_waiters.insert(dettid);
7343        }
7344        scheduler
7345            .blocked
7346            .timed_waiters
7347            .insert(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, DetTid::from_raw(11));
7348        scheduler
7349    }
7350
7351    /// The deadlock report reaches stderr, so it must be byte-identical across
7352    /// runs of the same program. Codex measured the unguarded `{:?}` dump
7353    /// emitting `Waker { data: 0x557e90a74680, .. }` in one run and
7354    /// `Waker { data: 0x5654e2b08680, .. }` in the next: raw host pointers that
7355    /// move with ASLR. Assert the render carries no host pointer and no
7356    /// insertion-order dependence.
7357    #[test]
7358    fn deadlock_report_is_deterministic_and_carries_no_host_pointer() {
7359        let config = Config::default();
7360        let forward = deadlocked_scheduler(&config, false).format_terminal_deadlock();
7361        let reversed = deadlocked_scheduler(&config, true).format_terminal_deadlock();
7362
7363        // Same logical state built in two insertion orders must render
7364        // identically: no HashMap/HashSet iteration order may leak.
7365        assert_eq!(forward, reversed);
7366
7367        // No `Debug` of an Ivar/Waker, and so no host pointer.
7368        //
7369        // ⚠️ THIS LIST CANNOT SEE A SECTION THE FIXTURE NEVER RENDERS. It only
7370        // inspects the text `deadlocked_scheduler` happens to produce. When you
7371        // add a section to `format_terminal_deadlock`, add the state that triggers
7372        // it to the fixture in the same change, or this test will pass and say
7373        // nothing about your section.
7374        //
7375        // These tokens catch host POINTERS and raw thread ids. The subtler failure
7376        // is a host-influenced COUNTER, which looks like an ordinary small integer
7377        // and matches no banned word -- which is why the byte-identical assertion
7378        // above is the load-bearing check and this list is only a backstop.
7379        for banned in [
7380            "Waker", "Ivar", "vtable", "Mutex", "poisoned", "ThreadId", "0x7f",
7381        ] {
7382            assert!(
7383                !forward.contains(banned),
7384                "deadlock report leaked {banned:?}; it must print only guest-level \
7385                 identities.\n{forward}"
7386            );
7387        }
7388
7389        // The report is still substantive: it names every blocked thread, the
7390        // futex keys, and the indefinite deadline.
7391        for expected in [
7392            // This fixture holds BOTH pools, so the state-derived headline must
7393            // name both rather than whichever branch happened to fire.
7394            "Deadlock detected: thread(s) waiting on futex, and thread(s) waiting \
7395             indefinitely (pause, or a timer beyond the end of logical time), but no \
7396             runnable threads left.",
7397            "dtid 3",
7398            "dtid 9",
7399            "0x404100",
7400            "0x404300",
7401            "INDEFINITE (no deadline)",
7402            "FutexWait: R, MemAddrSpace(DetPid(3)): RW",
7403        ] {
7404            assert!(
7405                forward.contains(expected),
7406                "deadlock report lost {expected:?}:\n{forward}"
7407            );
7408        }
7409    }
7410
7411    /// A `pause(2)` registers `LogicalTime::INDEFINITE`, which is a sentinel for
7412    /// "no deadline", not a deadline. With nothing else left to run, the guest is
7413    /// permanently blocked and must be reported as such -- never woken.
7414    #[test]
7415    fn indefinite_waiter_alone_is_reported_as_a_deadlock() {
7416        let config = Config::default();
7417        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7418        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7419        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7420
7421        scheduler
7422            .blocked
7423            .timed_waiters
7424            .insert(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, DetTid::from_raw(100));
7425
7426        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7427
7428        // Asserting the verdict by value, not by catching a panic: the report is
7429        // recorded for `sched_loop_inner` to print and exit on, so the text is
7430        // checkable here rather than only through `#[should_panic]`.
7431        let report = scheduler
7432            .take_terminal_deadlock()
7433            .expect("an indefinite waiter with nothing runnable is a terminal deadlock");
7434        assert!(
7435            report.contains("thread(s) waiting indefinitely"),
7436            "unexpected report:\n{report}"
7437        );
7438        // Taking it is destructive, and the indefinite waiter is never woken.
7439        assert!(scheduler.take_terminal_deadlock().is_none());
7440        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7441        assert!(!scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty());
7442    }

7509    #[test]
7510    fn genuine_external_io_defers_rt_sigsuspend_deadlock_verdict() {

7512        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7513        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7514        let waiter = DetTid::from_raw(100);
7515        let io_thread = DetTid::from_raw(101);

7517            .blocked
7518            .rt_sigsuspend_blockers
7519            .insert(waiter, ExternalOpId::new(waiter, 7));
7520        scheduler

7522            .external_io_blockers

7524        for dettid in [waiter, io_thread] {

7537        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_ok());
7538        assert!(scheduler.take_terminal_deadlock().is_none());
7539        assert_eq!(scheduler.blocked.external_io_blockers.len(), 1);
7540        assert_eq!(scheduler.blocked.rt_sigsuspend_blockers.len(), 1);

7542
7543    /// The `SignalEvt` arm is the sub-case whose behaviour this change actually
7544    /// alters, and it is reachable with no `pause` anywhere.
7545    ///
7546    /// `handle_timer_settime` adds a guest-supplied duration to now, and
7547    /// `LogicalTime`'s `Add` saturates, so an absurdly far-future POSIX timer
7548    /// (issue #219, the Java case) lands on `LogicalTime::INDEFINITE` and
7549    /// `register_posix_timer` inserts it verbatim. Previously `step2d` popped it,
7550    /// fast-forwarded the clock ~584 years, and dispatched to `fire_alarm`, which
7551    /// -- unlike the `ThreadEvt` arm -- does NOT abort: it delivered a bogus
7552    /// year-2554 signal and the guest carried on. That is now a terminal
7553    /// deadlock, which is the faithful answer, because Linux will not fire that
7554    /// timer either and those threads genuinely hang.
7555    #[test]
7556    fn saturated_posix_timer_alone_is_a_deadlock_not_a_bogus_signal() {
7557        let config = Config::default();
7558        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7559        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7560        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7561        let process = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7562
7563        // The real production entry point, with the deadline a saturating add
7564        // produces. No `pause`, no `ThreadEvt`.
7565        scheduler.register_posix_timer(
7566            process,
7567            process,
7568            7,
7569            Some(LogicalTime::MAX - LogicalTime::from_nanos(0)),
7570            LogicalTime::ZERO,
7571            Signal::SIGALRM,
7572        );
7573        assert_eq!(
7574            scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.next_deadline(),
7575            Some(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE),
7576            "a saturated timer must land on the no-deadline sentinel"
7577        );
7578
7579        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7580
7581        let report = scheduler
7582            .take_terminal_deadlock()
7583            .expect("a saturated timer with nothing runnable is a terminal deadlock");
7584        assert!(
7585            report.contains("thread(s) waiting indefinitely"),
7586            "unexpected report:\n{report}"
7587        );
7588        // The signal was NOT delivered and the clock did NOT jump to the end of
7589        // logical time: both are what the old fast-forward did here.
7590        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7591        assert!(scheduler.run_queue.is_empty());
7592        assert!(!scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.is_empty());
7593    }
7594
7595    /// The headline is derived from state, not from the branch that fired, so a
7596    /// genuine futex deadlock that merely has a saturated timer registered names
7597    /// both classes rather than being filed as an indefinite-wait bug.
7598    #[test]
7599    fn a_futex_deadlock_holding_a_saturated_timer_names_both_classes() {
7600        let config = Config::default();
7601        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7602        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7603        let mm = MmId::initial(DetTid::from_raw(3));
7604        scheduler
7605            .blocked
7606            .futex_waiters
7607            .entry(FutexID::private(mm, 0x404100))
7608            .or_default()
7609            .push(futex_waiter(3, u32::MAX));
7610        scheduler.register_posix_timer(
7611            DetPid::from_raw(100),
7612            DetPid::from_raw(100),
7613            7,
7614            Some(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE),
7615            LogicalTime::ZERO,
7616            Signal::SIGALRM,
7617        );
7618
7619        // Branch order sends this to the indefinite arm, because a timed waiter
7620        // exists; the headline must still say the futex pool is blocked.
7621        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7622        let report = scheduler.take_terminal_deadlock().expect("terminal");
7623        let headline = report.lines().next().expect("a headline");
7624        assert_eq!(
7625            headline,
7626            "Deadlock detected: thread(s) waiting on futex, and thread(s) waiting \
7627             indefinitely (pause, or a timer beyond the end of logical time), but no \
7628             runnable threads left.",
7629            "full report:\n{report}"
7630        );
7631    }
7632
7633    /// The futex class shares `report_terminal_deadlock`, so it must record a
7634    /// verdict too -- otherwise fixing the teardown for one class silently
7635    /// leaves the other wedged.
7636    #[test]
7637    fn futex_deadlock_records_the_same_terminal_verdict() {
7638        let config = Config::default();
7639        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7640        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7641        let mm = MmId::initial(DetTid::from_raw(3));
7642        scheduler
7643            .blocked
7644            .futex_waiters
7645            .entry(FutexID::private(mm, 0x404100))
7646            .or_default()
7647            .push(futex_waiter(3, u32::MAX));
7648
7649        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7650
7651        let report = scheduler
7652            .take_terminal_deadlock()
7653            .expect("futex waiters with nothing runnable is a terminal deadlock");
7654        assert!(
7655            report.contains("thread(s) waiting on futex"),
7656            "unexpected report:\n{report}"
7657        );
7658    }
7659
7660    /// The positive half of the bracket: refusing to fast-forward onto an
7661    /// indefinite waiter must not make the fast-forward machinery inert. A real
7662    /// deadline still fires, the clock still advances to exactly that deadline
7663    /// (not to the end of logical time), and the indefinite waiter stays parked.
7664    #[test]
7665    fn finite_deadline_still_fast_forwards_alongside_an_indefinite_waiter() {
7666        let config = Config::default();
7667        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7668        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7669        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7670        let alarm_deadline = initial_time + LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000);
7671        let process = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7672        let pauser = DetTid::from_raw(200);
7673
7674        scheduler
7675            .blocked
7676            .timed_waiters
7677            .insert(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, pauser);
7678        scheduler.register_alarm(
7679            process,
7680            process,
7681            initial_time,
7682            LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000),
7683            LogicalTime::ZERO,
7684            Signal::SIGALRM,
7685        );
7686
7687        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7688        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), alarm_deadline);
7689        assert_eq!(
7690            scheduler
7691                .blocked
7692                .timed_waiters
7693                .iter()
7694                .collect::<Vec<(LogicalTime, TimedEvent)>>(),
7695            vec![(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, TimedEvent::ThreadEvt(pauser))]
7696        );
7697    }
7698
7699    /// Outstanding blocking external IO can still deliver the signal an
7700    /// indefinite waiter is waiting for, so the deadlock verdict must be
7701    /// deferred rather than reported. `step2c` does not cover this case: it only
7702    /// spins when `timed_waiters` is *empty*, and an indefinite waiter is an
7703    /// entry.
7704    #[test]
7705    fn outstanding_external_io_defers_the_indefinite_wait_verdict() {
7706        let config = Config::default();
7707        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7708        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7709        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7710        let pauser = DetTid::from_raw(100);
7711        let io_thread = DetTid::from_raw(101);
7712
7713        scheduler
7714            .blocked
7715            .timed_waiters
7716            .insert(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, pauser);
7717        scheduler
7718            .blocked
7719            .external_io_blockers
7720            .insert(io_thread, ExternalOpId::new(io_thread, 0));
7721
7722        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7723        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7724        assert_eq!(
7725            scheduler
7726                .blocked
7727                .timed_waiters
7728                .iter()
7729                .collect::<Vec<(LogicalTime, TimedEvent)>>(),
7730            vec![(LogicalTime::INDEFINITE, TimedEvent::ThreadEvt(pauser))]
7731        );
7732    }
7733
7734    #[test]
7735    fn physical_exit_barrier_begins_when_last_process_thread_is_logically_dead() {
7736        let config = Config {
7737            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: true,
7738            ..Config::default()
7739        };
7740        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7741        let leader = DetTid::from_raw(100);
7742        let worker = DetTid::from_raw(101);
7743        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(leader, leader, true);
7744        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(leader, worker, false);
7745        for dettid in [leader, worker] {
7746            scheduler.next_turns.insert(
7747                dettid,
7748                ThreadNextTurn {
7749                    dettid,
7750                    child_tid_addr: 0,
7751                    req: Ivar::new(),
7752                    resp: Ivar::new(),
7753                },
7754            );
7755        }
7756
7757        scheduler.logically_kill_thread(&leader, &leader, MmId::initial(leader));
7758        assert!(scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty());
7759
7760        scheduler.logically_kill_thread(&worker, &leader, MmId::initial(leader));
7761        assert_eq!(
7762            scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits,
7763            BTreeSet::from([leader])
7764        );
7765    }
7766
7767    #[test]
7768    fn final_root_and_orphan_exits_release_exact_pid_barriers() {
7769        let config = Config {
7770            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: true,
7771            ..Config::default()
7772        };
7773        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7774        let root = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7775        let child = DetPid::from_raw(200);
7776        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(root, root, true);
7777        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(root, child, true);
7778        for dettid in [root, child] {
7779            scheduler.next_turns.insert(
7780                dettid,
7781                ThreadNextTurn {
7782                    dettid,
7783                    child_tid_addr: 0,
7784                    req: Ivar::new(),
7785                    resp: Ivar::new(),
7786                },
7787            );
7788        }
7789
7790        scheduler.logically_kill_thread(&root, &root, MmId::initial(root));
7791        assert_eq!(
7792            scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits,
7793            BTreeSet::from([root])
7794        );
7795        assert!(!scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(child));
7796        assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(root));
7797        assert!(scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty());
7798
7799        scheduler.logically_kill_thread(&child, &child, MmId::initial(child));
7800        assert_eq!(
7801            scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits,
7802            BTreeSet::from([child])
7803        );
7804        assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(child));
7805        assert!(scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty());
7806    }
7807
7808    #[test]
7809    fn final_child_exit_does_not_block_parent_timer() {
7810        let config = Config {
7811            backend_reports_physical_process_exits: true,
7812            ..Config::default()
7813        };
7814        let mut scheduler = Scheduler::new(&config);
7815        let global_time = Arc::new(Mutex::new(GlobalTime::new(&config)));
7816        let initial_time = global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos();
7817        let deadline = initial_time + LogicalTime::from_nanos(1_000);
7818        let parent = DetPid::from_raw(100);
7819        let child = DetPid::from_raw(200);
7820        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(parent, parent, true);
7821        scheduler.thread_tree.add_child(parent, child, true);
7822        scheduler.next_turns.insert(
7823            parent,
7824            ThreadNextTurn {
7825                dettid: parent,
7826                child_tid_addr: 0,
7827                req: Ivar::new(),
7828                resp: Ivar::new(),
7829            },
7830        );
7831        scheduler.priorities.insert(parent, DEFAULT_PRIORITY);
7832        scheduler.blocked.timed_waiters.insert(deadline, parent);
7833
7834        assert!(scheduler.begin_physical_process_exit(child));
7835        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7836        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), initial_time);
7837        assert!(scheduler.complete_physical_process_exit(child));
7838        assert!(scheduler.pending_physical_process_exits.is_empty());
7839        assert!(scheduler.step2d_handle_empty_queue(&global_time).is_err());
7840        assert_eq!(global_time.lock().unwrap().as_nanos(), deadline);
7841    }

7843    /// Build an `HbRuntime` from a JSON happens-before spec for testing.
7844    fn hb_runtime(json: &str) -> HbRuntime {
7845        let program = detcore_model::happens_before::HappensBeforeSpec::from_json(json)
7846            .unwrap()
7847            .normalize()
7848            .unwrap();
7849        HbRuntime::new(program)
7850    }
7851
7852    /// The enforcement predicates that drive `hb_checkpoint`: an anchor is
7853    /// reached at exactly its `SyscallCount` on the right thread, an AFTER anchor
7854    /// of a Hard edge is blocked until its BEFORE anchor fires, and firing the
7855    /// BEFORE anchor opens the gate. This mirrors the two-thread race validated
7856    /// end-to-end (main_write < worker_write forcing A before B).
7857    #[test]
7858    fn hb_runtime_gate_opens_only_after_before_anchor_fires() {
7859        let mut hb = hb_runtime(
7860            r#"{
7861              "version": 1,
7862              "threads": { "main": {"dettid": 3}, "worker": {"dettid": 5} },
7863              "events": {
7864                "main_write":   {"thread": "main",   "syscalls": 47},
7865                "worker_write": {"thread": "worker", "syscalls": 8}
7866              },
7867              "edges": [ {"before": "main_write", "after": "worker_write", "strength": "hard"} ]
7868            }"#,
7869        );
7870        let main = DetTid::from_raw(3);
7871        let worker = DetTid::from_raw(5);
7872
7873        // Anchors resolve to the addressed (thread, count) and nothing else.
7874        assert_eq!(
7875            hb.anchors_at_syscall(main, 47),
7876            vec!["main_write".to_string()]
7877        );
7878        assert_eq!(
7879            hb.anchors_at_syscall(worker, 8),
7880            vec!["worker_write".to_string()]
7881        );
7882        assert!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(main, 8).is_empty());
7883        assert!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(worker, 47).is_empty());
7884        assert!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(worker, 7).is_empty());
7885
7886        // Before its gating BEFORE anchor fires, the AFTER anchor is blocked and
7887        // the BEFORE anchor is free (it gates nothing).
7888        assert!(hb.anchor_blocked("worker_write"));
7889        assert!(!hb.anchor_blocked("main_write"));
7890
7891        // Firing the BEFORE anchor opens the gate exactly once.
7892        assert!(hb.fired.insert("main_write".to_string()));
7893        assert!(!hb.anchor_blocked("worker_write"));
7894    }
7895
7896    /// A soft edge never parks its AFTER thread, and `spawn_ordinal` addressing
7897    /// resolves against the observed spawn order (index 0 = root).
7898    #[test]
7899    fn hb_runtime_soft_edge_and_spawn_ordinal_resolution() {
7900        let mut hb = hb_runtime(
7901            r#"{
7902              "version": 1,
7903              "threads": {
7904                "root":  {"spawn_ordinal": 0},
7905                "child": {"spawn_ordinal": 1}
7906              },
7907              "events": {
7908                "a": {"thread": "root",  "syscalls": 3},
7909                "b": {"thread": "child", "syscalls": 4}
7910              },
7911              "edges": [ {"before": "a", "after": "b", "strength": "soft"} ]
7912            }"#,
7913        );
7914        // A soft edge biases but never hard-blocks, so its AFTER is never parked.
7915        assert!(!hb.anchor_blocked("b"));
7916
7917        // spawn_ordinal is unresolved until threads are observed at creation time.
7918        let root = DetTid::from_raw(3);
7919        let child = DetTid::from_raw(5);
7920        assert!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(root, 3).is_empty());
7921        hb.note_spawn(root); // index 0 -> root
7922        hb.note_spawn(child); // index 1 -> first spawned child
7923        assert_eq!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(root, 3), vec!["a".to_string()]);
7924        assert_eq!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(child, 4), vec!["b".to_string()]);
7925        // note_spawn is idempotent, so a re-registration does not shift indices.
7926        hb.note_spawn(root);
7927        assert_eq!(hb.anchors_at_syscall(child, 4), vec!["b".to_string()]);
7928    }
7929}
